# DRY audit — all 147 verified findings (raw)
Each finding was produced by a layer-specific finder and then re-verified against the files by a second, adversarial pass. Sites are `path:startline-endline`.

## 1. `_handle_validation_errors` copy-pasted into 8 view classes in 5 mutually incompatible shapes, while a strictly better shared `format_validation_errors` already exists and is already wired up as the DRF exception handler
- **dimension:** views | **severity:** high | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 11 | **~lines:** 115
- **sites:** todo/views/task.py:169-195, todo/views/task.py:367-393, todo/views/team.py:102-123, todo/views/team.py:127-129, todo/views/team.py:319-326, todo/views/team.py:469-489, todo/views/team_creation_invite_code.py:28-30, todo/views/team_creation_invite_code.py:125-127, todo/views/task.py:423-425, todo/views/task_assignment.py:54-56, todo/views/team.py:244-246
- **drift:** FIVE incompatible bodies now answer the same condition (a DRF serializer rejected the payload), not the four claimed - the finder missed todo/views/team.py:244-246, which returns `Response(serializer.errors, status=400)`, i.e. the raw DRF dict with NO `errors` wrapper at all, unlike the `{"errors": {...}}` shape at team.py:127-129 / task.py:425 / task_assignment.py:56.

(1) `ApiErrorResponse` envelope: task.py:169-195, task.py:367-393, team.py:102-123.
(2) `{"errors": <raw DRF dict>}`: team.py:127-129, tcic.py:28-30, tcic.py:125-127, task.py:425, task_assignment.py:56.
(3) bare `serializer.errors`: team.py:246.
(4) envelope with the field name discarded: team.py:319-326 `errors=[{"detail": str(error)} for error in errors.values()]` - `error` is the *list* of ErrorDetails, so the client gets the stringified repr of a list.
(5) hand-built dict: team.py:469-489, literal `"title": "Invalid value"` instead of `ApiErrors.VALIDATION_ERROR`, plain `"source": field` instead of `{ApiErrorSource.PARAMETER: field}`, plus an `"authenticated"` key no other copy has. It also lost the `isinstance(messages, list)` guard the canonical copies carry: `for msg in msgs:` on a non-list value iterates it character by character.

NOTE (corrected by the completeness review, N1) - an earlier draft claimed a schema outage here; that causation was refuted. schema.yaml is simply stale (committed 2025-07-19; the view was added 2025-07-25). The misplaced decorator is real but latent. At todo/views/team.py:452-489 the `@extend_schema(...)` decorator for `DELETE /teams/{team_id}/members/{user_id}` is attached to the pasted `_handle_validation_errors` method (line 469), not to `delete` (line 491). Confirmed empirically: `grep -n "members/{user_id}" schema.yaml` returns NOTHING and `grep -n "ID of the team" schema.yaml` returns nothing - the endpoint is entirely absent from the generated OpenAPI document. The decorator placement is wrong and will drop the endpoint whenever the schema is next regenerated; it is not the cause of its current absence.
- **proposed:** Two steps.

(a) Add `todo/utils/response_utils.py` (the package exists - it currently holds only invite_code_utils.py, jwt_utils.py, task_validation_utils.py) with `def validation_error_response(errors) -> Response`, reusing the already-correct, already-recursive `todo.exceptions.exception_handler.format_validation_errors`.

(b) Delete all 8 `_handle_validation_errors` definitions and the 3 inline copies. Views that can raise switch to `serializer.is_valid(raise_exception=True)` and get the identical envelope for free from the registered handler (this is what task.py:79/285/295, label.py:16, watchlist.py:57/89/141 and role.py:105 already do). Views that must not raise call `validation_error_response(serializer.errors)`.

Call sites change from `return self._handle_validation_errors(serializer.errors)` to `return validation_error_response(serializer.errors)`, or the `if not serializer.is_valid():` block disappears entirely.

Independently and immediately: move the `@extend_schema` block at team.py:452-468 down onto `def delete` at team.py:491 - `DELETE /teams/{team_id}/members/{user_id}` is currently missing from schema.yaml.

## 2. `except ValueError` -> unwrap `ApiErrorResponse` from `e.args[0]` -> DEBUG-aware 500 fallback: 6 view copies, only one carrying the IndexError guard (plus 2 more copies of the vulnerable predicate in services/)
- **dimension:** views | **severity:** high | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 8 | **~lines:** 90
- **sites:** todo/views/task.py:151-167, todo/views/task.py:349-365, todo/views/team.py:45-57, todo/views/team.py:88-100, todo/views/team.py:300-310, todo/views/watchlist.py:96-108, todo/services/task_service.py:655-657, todo/services/watchlist_service.py:101-103
- **drift:** (1) The IndexError bug is fixed in exactly ONE of the eight copies. todo/views/task.py:350 reads `if isinstance(e, ValueError) and e.args and isinstance(e.args[0], ApiErrorResponse):`. The other seven (task.py:152, team.py:46, team.py:89, team.py:301, watchlist.py:97, task_service.py:656, watchlist_service.py:102) index `e.args[0]` with no `e.args` guard, so any `ValueError` raised with no arguments inside the try block - including one thrown by a library, e.g. `int()` on bad input - raises `IndexError` *inside the except handler*. The already-registered handler at todo/exceptions/exception_handler.py:250 also guards correctly (`hasattr(exc, "args") and exc.args and ...`), so the copies are the only unguarded code in the repo.

(2) Fallback status drift. todo/views/team.py:305-310 uses `statusCode=400` / `status=400` / `message=str(e)` where the other five views use 500 / `ApiErrors.UNEXPECTED_ERROR_OCCURRED`. An unrecognised ValueError out of `TeamService.add_team_members` is reported as the client's fault; the identical failure class out of `TeamService.create_team` (team.py:93-100, same file, 200 lines up) is reported as a server fault.

(3) DEBUG-leak drift in both directions. team.py:308 emits `str(e)` with no `settings.DEBUG` guard, so exception text reaches production clients. todo/views/watchlist.py:101-105 does the opposite - `errors=[{"detail": ApiErrors.INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR}]`, the `settings.DEBUG` branch dropped entirely - so a developer running DEBUG=True gets no detail from `POST /watchlist` while every other endpoint gives it.
- **proposed:** `todo/utils/response_utils.py::api_error_response(exc, *, fallback_status=status.HTTP_500_INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR) -> Response`, holding the guarded unwrap and the DEBUG-aware fallback in one place. Each of the six view blocks collapses to `except ValueError as e: return api_error_response(e)`.

The stronger fix, available because `todo.exceptions.exception_handler.handle_exception` is already the project's DRF `EXCEPTION_HANDLER` (todo_project/settings/base.py:102) and already handles this exact case correctly at exception_handler.py:249-254: delete the six try/except blocks and let the ValueError propagate. That yields one envelope, the guard for free, and centralised logging.

team.py:300-310's 400 is the one site needing a decision rather than a mechanical swap - the durable fix is for `TeamService.add_team_members` to raise `ValueError(ApiErrorResponse(statusCode=400, ...))` so the unwrap branch produces the 400, rather than the view's fallback hard-coding it.

## 3. The standalone `except Exception` -> 500 `ApiErrorResponse` fallback pasted 6 more times; one copy leaks exception text in production, and team.py:171-177 turns the same exception into a 404
- **dimension:** views | **severity:** high | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 7 | **~lines:** 55
- **sites:** todo/views/task_assignment.py:76-84, todo/views/task_assignment.py:137-145, todo/views/task_assignment.py:300-308, todo/views/team.py:178-184, todo/views/team.py:311-317, todo/views/task.py:433-439, todo/views/team.py:171-177
- **drift:** (1) Production information leak in exactly one copy. todo/views/task.py:433-439 (`AssignTaskToUserView.patch`, i.e. `PATCH /tasks/<id>/assign`) builds `errors=[{"detail": str(e)}]` with no `settings.DEBUG` guard, while all five siblings gate on DEBUG. `POST /task-assignments` (task_assignment.py:76-84) reaches the *same* `TaskAssignmentService.create_task_assignment` and does guard. A pymongo/bson failure therefore returns raw driver text to any production client on one route and "Internal server error" on the other.

(2) The same copy renames the local from `fallback_response` to `error_response` - the tell that it was pasted and hand-edited.

(3) team.py:184 and team.py:317 return the bare int `status=500` where the other four use `status.HTTP_500_INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR`; team.py:177 and :310 likewise use bare `404` / `400`. Same file, same statement, two conventions.

(4) I verified the finder's fourth-variant claim: todo/views/team.py:171-177 really is a fifth shape of the same block, catching `ValueError` and mapping it to 404 with `message=str(e)`:
```python
        except ValueError as e:
            fallback_response = ApiErrorResponse(statusCode=404, message=str(e), errors=[{"detail": str(e)}])
            return Response(data=fallback_response.model_dump(mode="json"), status=404)
```
so `GET /teams/<id>` renders any ValueError as 404 while `GET /teams` (team.py:45-57) renders the identical exception as 500. None of the seven blocks logs anything.
- **proposed:** Same `todo/utils/response_utils.py::api_error_response(exc)` as the previous finding - the `except Exception` case is exactly its fallback branch with the unwrap attempt failing. That is deliberate: these two findings should share ONE helper, not two, since the fallback body is textually the same in all 11 sites across both.

Stronger fix, same reasoning as before: `handle_exception` (todo/exceptions/exception_handler.py:48, registered at todo_project/settings/base.py:102) already renders this exact envelope with the DEBUG guard, so deleting these blocks outright is a net simplification that also stops views from silently swallowing exceptions.

Independent of any extraction, add the missing `if settings.DEBUG` guard at todo/views/task.py:437 - that one is a live production leak.

## 4. `data.pop("invite_code", None)` redaction repeated at 5 team response sites and omitted at the 6th, which leaks the team invite code on `POST /teams`
- **dimension:** views | **severity:** high | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 6 | **~lines:** 18
- **sites:** todo/views/team.py:40-43, todo/views/team.py:168-170, todo/views/team.py:224-226, todo/views/team.py:251-253, todo/views/team.py:296-298, todo/views/team.py:85-86
- **drift:** Five copies remembered the redaction, one forgot. That the omission is accidental rather than intended is settled twice over:
(a) `TeamInviteCodeView` (todo/views/team.py:350-365) exists solely to hand out the invite code, and gates it behind an explicit creator-or-POC check returning 403 otherwise - while `POST /teams` returns the same secret with no check at all.
(b) `todo/tests/integration/test_team_update.py:135` asserts `self.assertNotIn("invite_code", data)` on the PATCH response, so redaction is tested behaviour on a sibling endpoint and untested on this one.

CORRECTION TO THE FINDER'S REMEDY: the suggested "same-day fix" of adding `data.pop("invite_code", None)` at team.py:85 WOULD NOT WORK. Unlike the five other sites, which dump a `TeamDTO` and therefore have `invite_code` at the top level, `CreateTeamResponse` nests it - the leaked value is at `data["team"]["invite_code"]`, so the line would have to be `data["team"].pop("invite_code", None)`. That the copies are not even structurally interchangeable is itself the argument for moving the rule off the view layer.

I also corrected two site ranges: the finder's `team.py:167-170` starts on `team_dto.users = users_data` (the block is 168-170) and `team.py:250-253` / `295-298` start one line early on the service call.
- **proposed:** Stop redacting at the view layer; make the DTO incapable of carrying the secret into a response.

Preferred: mark the field excluded on the DTO - `todo/dto/team_dto.py:47` becomes `invite_code: str = Field(exclude=True)`. Every `model_dump()` then omits it, all five pops delete themselves, and `POST /teams` stops leaking with no view change. `TeamInviteCodeView` is unaffected because todo/views/team.py:355-361 already reads `team.invite_code` off the repository model (`TeamRepository.get_by_id`), never off a DTO dump.

Alternative if any service relies on `invite_code` round-tripping through `TeamDTO`: drop the field from `TeamDTO` entirely and give `TeamInviteCodeView` a dedicated `TeamInviteCodeDTO`.

Either way the six call sites collapse to a plain `Response(dto.model_dump(mode="json"), ...)` and no future team endpoint can leak the code by forgetting a line. Verify against `todo/services/team_service.py` `join_team_by_invite_code` / `update_team` before flipping, and keep `todo/tests/integration/test_team_update.py:135` as the regression guard.

## 5. Path-parameter `OpenApiParameter` blocks re-declared 17 times (finder said 14) - real boilerplate, but the claimed OpenAPI drift does not exist
- **dimension:** views | **severity:** low | **effort:** small | **copies:** 17 | **~lines:** 95
- **sites:** todo/views/task.py:205-210, todo/views/task.py:232-237, todo/views/task.py:256-261, todo/views/task.py:315-321, todo/views/task.py:404-410, todo/views/task_assignment.py:98-104, todo/views/task_assignment.py:154-160, todo/views/task_assignment.py:263-269, todo/views/watchlist.py:118-124, todo/views/team.py:137-142, todo/views/team.py:192-197, todo/views/team.py:267-272, todo/views/team.py:336-342, todo/views/team.py:375-381, todo/views/team.py:456-456, todo/views/team.py:457-459, todo/views/role.py:123-128
- **drift:** I REFUTED the finder's central drift claim. The `required=` inconsistency does NOT reach the generated document: drf-spectacular forces `required: true` on every PATH parameter regardless. Checked against the committed schema.yaml - the block generated from task.py:205-210 (which omits `required=True`) reads:
```yaml
      - in: path
        name: task_id
        schema:
          type: string
        description: Unique identifier of the task
        required: true
```
identical to the ones generated from the `required=True` sites (schema.yaml:329-334, 367-372, 404-409, 507-512, 536-541, 573-578, 943-948). There is no self-inconsistent OpenAPI document. The `type=str` vs `type=OpenApiTypes.STR` difference at team.py:456-459 likewise renders as `type: string` either way.

What remains is source-level only, and modest: the same parameter spelled out 17 times, with hand-edited descriptions ("Unique identifier of the task" / "...of the task to delete" / "...of the task to update" / "...of the task to update in the watchlist"; "Unique identifier of the team" vs "ID of the team") and two call styles. That is boilerplate worth collapsing, but it has not caused and cannot cause a behaviour or contract defect, so I have downgraded severity from the finder's framing.
- **proposed:** A new `todo/views/schema_params.py` holding small factories rather than bare constants, because four of the nine `task_id` descriptions are genuinely per-handler and a flat constant would flatten them:

`def task_id_param(description: str = "Unique identifier of the task") -> OpenApiParameter`, `def team_id_param(...)`, plus `user_id_param` and `role_id_param`.

Decorators change from a 6-line literal to `parameters=[task_id_param()]` or `parameters=[task_id_param("Unique identifier of the task to delete")]`. `OpenApiParameter` instances are inert descriptors, so returning a fresh one per call (rather than sharing a module-level singleton) also keeps drf-spectacular from ever mutating a shared object. Purely documentation-facing: no runtime behaviour changes, and schema.yaml should regenerate byte-identical apart from the two harmonised descriptions.

## 6. `page` / `limit` query-parameter schema blocks duplicated across 4 paginated endpoints with 4 different descriptions and 2 different documented caps, one of which is enforced nowhere
- **dimension:** views | **severity:** low | **effort:** small | **copies:** 4 | **~lines:** 56
- **sites:** todo/views/task.py:40-51, todo/views/watchlist.py:28-41, todo/views/user.py:35-48, todo/views/team_creation_invite_code.py:145-158
- **drift:** Four blocks describing the same two query params, no two agreeing. Unlike the path-parameter finding, this drift IS visible in the committed schema.yaml, which I checked directly:
- schema.yaml:441-444 `description: Number of tasks per page` (from task.py:46-51 - no defaults, no cap documented at all)
- schema.yaml:806-809 `description: 'Number of results per page (default: 10, max: 100)'` (user.py)
- schema.yaml:860-863 `description: 'Number of tasks per page (default: 10, max: 100)'` (watchlist.py)
- team_creation_invite_code.py documents `max: 50`.

The `required=False` omission at task.py:40-51 does NOT reach the document (drf-spectacular omits `required` for optional query params anyway), so that specific sub-claim is refuted - but the description and cap drift is real and user-visible.

Worse, the documented caps are not all true. The 50 cap at team_creation_invite_code.py:155 is genuinely enforced at team_creation_invite_code.py:194-195 (`if limit < 1 or limit > 50: limit = 10`). The 100 cap that user.py:46 advertises is enforced nowhere - user.py:90 is a bare `int(request.query_params.get("limit", 10))` passed straight to `UserService.get_all_users`. The prose and the code have no common source.
- **proposed:** `todo/views/schema_params.py::pagination_params(item_noun: str, *, max_limit: int, default_limit: int = 10) -> list[OpenApiParameter]`, living alongside the path-param factories from the previous finding, with the cap interpolated into the description so the documented number is a parameter rather than prose.

Decorators become `parameters=[*pagination_params("tasks", max_limit=MAX_PAGE_LIMIT), ...]`. Feeding `max_limit` from the same settings key the serializers already read (todo/serializers/get_tasks_serializer.py:27, get_labels_serializer.py:20, get_watchlist_tasks_serializer.py:20 all use `settings.REST_FRAMEWORK["DEFAULT_PAGINATION_SETTINGS"]["MAX_PAGE_LIMIT"]`) makes the documented cap and the enforced cap the same constant, which is the part of this that stops being cosmetic.

Pair with the pagination-serializer finding so `UsersView` actually enforces what it documents.

## 7. `_check_authorization` admin-email gate and its 403 response block duplicated byte-for-byte across two invite-code views, and absent from the third
- **dimension:** views | **severity:** high | **effort:** small | **copies:** 4 | **~lines:** 26
- **sites:** todo/views/team_creation_invite_code.py:20-26, todo/views/team_creation_invite_code.py:131-137, todo/views/team_creation_invite_code.py:65-71, todo/views/team_creation_invite_code.py:180-186
- **drift:** The third view in the same file, `VerifyTeamCreationInviteCodeView` (todo/views/team_creation_invite_code.py:87-127), has neither the method nor the check. `POST /team-invite-codes/verify` therefore lets any authenticated user probe whether an arbitrary code string is valid and unused (team_creation_invite_code.py:116-121) while generating and listing codes are both admin-only. Whether that is intended is undecidable from the code - which is exactly the cost of the duplication: with the rule pasted into each class instead of declared once, there is no single place that states which endpoints are admin-gated, so an omission is invisible.

The divergence is already recorded silently in the docs: both gated handlers declare `403: OpenApiResponse(...)` in `@extend_schema` (lines 57 and 172) and the ungated one declares only 200/400/401/500 (lines 99-104). The schema faithfully documents the gap without anything flagging it.

The hardcoded string is also a third copy of a constant that already exists: `todo/constants/messages.py:52` defines `UNAUTHORIZED_TITLE = "You are not authorized to perform this action"` and neither call site uses it (both add a trailing period).
- **proposed:** A DRF permission class in a new `todo/middlewares/permissions.py` (the package exists - jwt_auth.py, team_access_middleware.py): `class IsAdminEmail(BasePermission)` with `has_permission(self, request, view)` reading `getattr(request, "user_email", None)` against `settings.ADMIN_EMAILS`. Both views then declare `permission_classes = [IsAdminEmail]` and delete their `_check_authorization` method and their 403 block. DRF raises `PermissionDenied`, which the already-registered `todo.exceptions.exception_handler.handle_exception` renders.

The payoff beyond the 26 lines: which endpoints are admin-gated becomes readable from the class declarations, so `VerifyTeamCreationInviteCodeView` lacking the line is a visible decision rather than an invisible omission.

Two things to verify before switching: (a) the 403 body changes from `{"message": ...}` to the `ApiErrorResponse` envelope, a client-visible change; (b) `request.user_email` must be populated before permission checks run - confirm the ordering in todo/middlewares/jwt_auth.py, since `has_permission` fires after authentication but the attribute here is set by middleware, not by an authentication class.

## 8. Dead copy-pasted branch: the same `if query.validated_data["profile"]` condition tested twice in a row in TaskListView.get, silently dropping `exclude_none=True`
- **dimension:** views | **severity:** medium | **effort:** small | **copies:** 2 | **~lines:** 20
- **sites:** todo/views/task.py:80-88, todo/views/task.py:90-99
- **drift:** The two copies are NOT identical, which is what proves this is a half-finished edit rather than a redundant guard: the surviving block passes `status_filter=` and dumps with plain `mode="json"`; the dead block passes no status filter and dumps with `exclude_none=True`. Someone added status filtering by pasting the existing block above the original and editing the copy, then never deleted the original.

`git log --oneline -- todo/views/task.py` corroborates the mechanism: commit 3e51495 `fix: add status filtering to tasks API with DONE exclusion by default (#227)`.

The consequence is a silent behaviour change nobody chose: the `exclude_none=True` serialisation the profile response used to have was lost in the paste, so `GET /tasks?profile=true` now emits null fields it previously omitted. `todo/tests/integration/test_task_profile_api.py` exercises this endpoint and should be checked against the current payload before touching it.
- **proposed:** The honest answer is that this one is a deletion, not an extraction - do not build a helper for it. Delete todo/views/task.py:90-99.

Before deleting, decide whether `exclude_none=True` was the intended profile behaviour. If it was, the fix is to move that kwarg onto the surviving dump at task.py:88, not just to drop the block; if it was not, deleting is enough. Either way the decision has to be made deliberately, because the code currently contains both answers and ships the one that was never chosen.

The generalisable lesson: what tempted the paste was needing to vary only the `model_dump` kwargs between two otherwise identical branches. If a `success_response(model, status_code, *, exclude_none=False)` helper existed, varying the dump would be a keyword rather than a duplicated nine-line branch - though on its own, as noted in my rejection of candidate 12, that helper does not pay for itself.

## 9. Pagination parsing expressed two incompatible ways across 5 endpoints: raw `int(request.query_params.get(...))` in 2 views (one clamps, one 500s on non-numeric input) versus 3 near-identical serializer declarations
- **dimension:** views | **severity:** medium | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 5 | **~lines:** 55
- **sites:** todo/views/user.py:89-90, todo/views/team_creation_invite_code.py:189-195, todo/serializers/get_tasks_serializer.py:15-31, todo/serializers/get_labels_serializer.py:8-24, todo/serializers/get_watchlist_tasks_serializer.py:8-24
- **drift:** Five sites, three behaviours for the same untrusted input:
- todo/views/team_creation_invite_code.py:189-195 clamps silently and wraps the parse in `try/except ValueError` returning 400 (lines 204-208).
- todo/views/user.py:89-90 does neither. `GET /users?page=abc` raises `ValueError` out of the bare `int()`; `GET /users?limit=100000` is passed straight into `UserService.get_all_users(page, limit)` - while that same endpoint's `@extend_schema` at user.py:46 documents "(default: 10, max: 100)". The cap exists only in the docstring.
- The three serializers reject bad input with a 400 through `is_valid(raise_exception=True)` (task.py:78-79, label.py:15-16, watchlist.py:56-57).

The defaults have drifted between the serializer copies too: get_labels_serializer.py:18 hardcodes `default=10` while get_tasks_serializer.py:25 and get_watchlist_tasks_serializer.py:18 read `DEFAULT_PAGE_LIMIT` from settings - so changing that setting silently moves the page size on four endpoints and not on labels.

And `todo/constants/messages.py:71-73` already carries `PAGE_POSITIVE`, `LIMIT_POSITIVE` and `MAX_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` for precisely this - messages the two raw-parsing views can never emit. (`MAX_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` is in fact used by no serializer either; all three set only a `min_value` message.)
- **proposed:** One `PaginationQueryParamsSerializer` in a new `todo/serializers/pagination_serializer.py`, declaring `page` and `limit` once against the `settings.REST_FRAMEWORK["DEFAULT_PAGINATION_SETTINGS"]` constants and the existing `ValidationErrors` messages (including `MAX_LIMIT_EXCEEDED`, currently unused).

The three existing per-resource serializers inherit from it and keep only their own fields: `GetTaskQueryParamsSerializer` keeps `profile`, `sort_by`, `order`, `teamId`, `status`; `GetLabelQueryParamsSerializer` keeps `search`; `GetWatchlistTaskQueryParamsSerializer` becomes an empty subclass. `UsersView.get` and `ListTeamCreationInviteCodesView.get` adopt it via `is_valid(raise_exception=True)` and delete their raw `int()` parsing and clamping.

Bounds, defaults and the caps documented by the `pagination_params()` schema factory from the earlier finding then all come from one settings key.

This spans serializers/ as well as views/, so it needs coordinating with that dimension. It is also the one finding here with a deliberate behaviour change on both raw sites: `GET /users?limit=100000` starts returning 400 instead of a huge page (a fix), and `GET /team-invite-codes?limit=99` starts returning 400 instead of silently falling back to 10 (a real contract change for any client relying on the clamp).

## 10. The "Task assignment not found" 404 block duplicated byte-for-byte in two handlers of `TaskAssignmentDetailView`, with a third handler already answering the same condition differently
- **dimension:** views | **severity:** medium | **effort:** small | **copies:** 3 | **~lines:** 12
- **sites:** todo/views/task_assignment.py:128-133, todo/views/task_assignment.py:291-296, todo/views/task_assignment.py:185
- **drift:** The two identical copies have not drifted from each other yet - but the third site in the same class already has, which is the whole point. `GET /task-assignments/<id>` and `DELETE /task-assignments/<id>` return `{statusCode, message, errors:[{detail}]}`; `PATCH /task-assignments/<id>` returns `{"error": "Task assignment not found."}` - different envelope key, different message (trailing period added), no `errors` array - for the same missing row, from the same class, 60 lines away.

The literal is hardcoded three times rather than living in `todo/constants/messages.py::ApiErrors`, which already holds the analogous `TASK_NOT_FOUND = "Task with ID {0} not found."`, `TASK_NOT_FOUND_GENERIC` and `TASK_NOT_FOUND_TITLE` (messages.py:35-37) - so the convention for exactly this exists and was not followed.
- **proposed:** Follow the pattern the codebase already uses for the identical task case. Add `TaskAssignmentNotFoundException` to `todo/exceptions/task_exceptions.py` alongside the existing `TaskNotFoundException`, and a branch for it in `todo.exceptions.exception_handler.handle_exception` next to the `TaskNotFoundException` branch (exception_handler.py:170-179), which already renders a 404 `ApiErrorResponse` with `title=ApiErrors.RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND_TITLE`.

Add the two literals to `todo/constants/messages.py::ApiErrors`: `TASK_ASSIGNMENT_NOT_FOUND = "Task assignment not found"` and `TASK_ASSIGNMENT_NOT_FOUND_DETAIL = "No assignment found for task {0}"`.

All three handlers then raise instead of hand-building, both duplicated blocks vanish, and the PATCH handler's divergent shape is pulled onto the same envelope - which is where the real value is, and which is a client-visible change to that endpoint's 404 body worth flagging in the changelog. `todo/tests/unit/views/test_task_assignment.py` will need updating.

## 11. MISSED BY FINDER: team-lookup-and-404 block duplicated byte-for-byte across TeamInviteCodeView and TeamActivityTimelineView
- **dimension:** views | **severity:** low | **effort:** small | **copies:** 2 | **~lines:** 6
- **sites:** todo/views/team.py:355-357, todo/views/team.py:401-403
- **drift:** These two are the only team endpoints that call `TeamRepository.get_by_id` directly from the view rather than going through `TeamService`. Every other team endpoint routes the missing-team condition through the service and gets a different 404 shape: `TeamDetailView.get` (todo/views/team.py:171-177) renders it as an `ApiErrorResponse` envelope with `statusCode`/`message`/`errors`, while these two render a bare `{"detail": "Team not found."}`. Same resource, same condition, two bodies.

The literal `"Team not found."` is hardcoded twice even though `todo/constants/messages.py` already holds the exactly-parallel `ApiErrors.TASK_NOT_FOUND_GENERIC = "Task not found."` (messages.py:36) and `USER_NOT_FOUND_GENERIC = "User not found."` (messages.py:54) - there is a `<RESOURCE>_NOT_FOUND_GENERIC` convention in this codebase and the team case is the one that skipped it.

Because the view reaches into the repository directly, both endpoints also bypass whatever access checks `TeamService` applies - worth confirming against todo/middlewares/team_access_middleware.py.
- **proposed:** Add `ApiErrors.TEAM_NOT_FOUND_GENERIC = "Team not found."` to todo/constants/messages.py to match the existing `TASK_NOT_FOUND_GENERIC` / `USER_NOT_FOUND_GENERIC`, and add a `TeamNotFoundException` to todo/exceptions/team_exceptions.py (which already holds `NotTeamAdminException`, `CannotRemoveOwnerException`, `CannotRemoveTeamPOCException`) with a branch in `handle_exception` beside the existing `TaskNotFoundException` one.

Both views then use a single `TeamService.get_team_or_raise(team_id)` accessor, which is also the right place to enforce the access rules these two currently skip by talking to the repository directly. The 404 body converges on the same envelope every other team endpoint emits - a client-visible change on `GET /teams/<id>/invite-code` and `GET /teams/<id>/activity-timeline`.

## 12. MISSED BY FINDER: three consecutive `except` clauses with byte-identical bodies in RemoveTeamMemberView.delete
- **dimension:** views | **severity:** low | **effort:** small | **copies:** 3 | **~lines:** 6
- **sites:** todo/views/team.py:506-507, todo/views/team.py:508-509, todo/views/team.py:510-511
- **drift:** Three copies of a two-line handler is exactly the shape that drifts next: any change to the 403 body (adding a `title`, moving to `ApiErrors.UNAUTHORIZED_TITLE`, switching to the `ApiErrorResponse` envelope the rest of the codebase uses) has to be made three times, and a fourth team-permission exception added later will be pasted rather than appended to a tuple. `e.message` is also assumed on all three without a common base class guaranteeing it - todo/exceptions/team_exceptions.py should be checked for whether they share one.

The surrounding handler already shows what drift looks like here: the trailing `except Exception as e` at team.py:512-513 maps ANY unexpected error to 400 with `str(e)` in the body and no `settings.DEBUG` guard, while the equivalent catch-all in every other view in this file (team.py:178-184, team.py:311-317) maps to 500 and does guard on DEBUG.
- **proposed:** Collapse the three clauses into one tuple-catch: `except (NotTeamAdminException, CannotRemoveTeamPOCException, CannotRemoveOwnerException) as e:`. Six lines become two, the 403 body has one definition, and a future permission exception is added to the tuple rather than pasted as a fourth clause.

No abstraction module is needed - this is a pure local collapse with zero behaviour change and zero risk, which is why it is worth doing even though the line count is small. If todo/exceptions/team_exceptions.py gives these three a shared base (e.g. `TeamPermissionException`), catching the base is cleaner still.

While in this handler, note two adjacent issues outside this finding's scope: `print(f"DEBUG: RemoveTeamMemberView.delete called ...")` at team.py:492 writes to stdout instead of logging, and the trailing `except Exception` at team.py:512-513 returns 400 with unguarded `str(e)` where the rest of the file returns a DEBUG-guarded 500.

## 13. MISSED BY FINDER: hand-built `{statusCode, message, data}` success envelope written out 5 times across auth.py and user.py, with `statusCode` present in 3 and dropped in 2 - and no success counterpart to `ApiErrorResponse` exists
- **dimension:** views | **severity:** medium | **effort:** small | **copies:** 5 | **~lines:** 32
- **sites:** todo/views/auth.py:48-54, todo/views/auth.py:176-182, todo/views/user.py:64-71, todo/views/user.py:79-85, todo/views/user.py:113-118
- **drift:** The envelope has already split in two within a single file. todo/views/user.py:66 includes `"statusCode": 404` on the failure branch, but the two success branches eleven and forty-nine lines later - user.py:79-85 and user.py:113-118 - emit only `{"message", "data"}` with no `statusCode` key at all. A client parsing `GET /users` cannot rely on `statusCode` being present: it appears on the 404 and vanishes on the 200.

The status source drifts alongside it: auth.py:48-54 and auth.py:176-182 put `status.HTTP_200_OK` *inside* the body and pass no `status=` kwarg to `Response`; user.py:64-71 passes the bare int `404` both inside the body and as the kwarg; user.py:79-85 passes bare `200` as the kwarg with no key in the body; user.py:113-118 passes `status.HTTP_200_OK` as the kwarg with no key in the body. Four conventions, five sites, two files.

The asymmetry that caused this is structural: `todo/dto/responses/error_response.py` gives the error path a typed `ApiErrorResponse` model, and there is no equivalent for the success path - so every success envelope is hand-typed, and each one is free to differ.
- **proposed:** Add the missing counterpart to the existing error model: `todo/dto/responses/api_success_response.py::ApiSuccessResponse(BaseModel)` with `statusCode: int`, `message: str`, `data: Any | None = None`, mirroring `ApiErrorResponse` in todo/dto/responses/error_response.py.

Pair it with `todo/utils/response_utils.py::success_envelope(message: str, data=None, status_code=status.HTTP_200_OK) -> Response` - this one earns its keep where the bare `model_dump` helper I rejected in candidate 12 does not, because it collapses a 5-to-8-line literal to one call AND makes the `statusCode` key impossible to forget.

All five sites become one line each. The client-visible change is that `GET /users` 200 responses gain a `statusCode` key they currently lack (additive, low risk); todo/tests/unit/views/test_auth.py and todo/tests/integration/test_user_profile_api.py should be checked for exact-dict assertions on these bodies.

## 14. "Load task, 404, then creator-or-assignee" write guard copied 4× in TaskService and a 5th time in TaskRepository
- **dimension:** services | **severity:** high | **effort:** small | **copies:** 5 | **~lines:** 44
- **sites:** todo/services/task_service.py:293-303 (update_task), todo/services/task_service.py:376-386 (update_task_with_assignee_from_dict), todo/services/task_service.py:468-478 (update_task_with_assignee), todo/services/task_service.py:544-554 (defer_task), todo/repositories/task_repository.py:270-279 (delete_by_id — 5th copy, other layer)
- **drift:** CORRECTION to the finder: it claimed delete_task "raises TaskNotFoundException where the other four would raise PermissionError for the same situation". That is wrong — task_repository.py:279 raises PermissionError for exactly that case. The real, verified drift is different and still supports extraction: (a) the repository copy filters on isDeleted:False (task_repository.py:270) while TaskRepository.get_by_id (task_repository.py:259-264) does NOT filter isDeleted at all, so a soft-deleted task can still be updated, deferred and re-assigned through the four service copies but cannot be deleted — the authorization rule and the liveness rule drifted apart across the layer boundary; (b) the repo copy compares `user_id != task.get("createdBy")` on a dict while the service copies compare a model attribute, so a missing createdBy field degrades differently; (c) the service layer reaches into the private helper TaskRepository._get_assigned_task_ids_for_user four separate times (:301, :384, :476, :552), so renaming or re-scoping that private method is a four-site edit.
- **proposed:** Add `TaskService._get_task_for_write(cls, task_id: str, user_id: str) -> TaskModel` in todo/services/task_service.py, placed next to _process_labels_for_update. It performs the get_by_id, raises TaskNotFoundException, runs the creator-or-assignee check, raises PermissionError(ApiErrors.UNAUTHORIZED_TITLE), and returns the loaded model so no caller re-fetches. update_task, update_task_with_assignee_from_dict, update_task_with_assignee and defer_task each replace their 11-line preamble with one line. Second step (separate commit, because it is API-visible): have TaskRepository.delete_by_id stop doing its own check and have TaskService.delete_task call the same guard, keeping the isDeleted:False semantics by adding an `include_deleted: bool = False` filter to the guard rather than to each caller.

## 15. Assignee-existence validation copied 5× with three different behaviours for an unknown user_type
- **dimension:** services | **severity:** high | **effort:** small | **copies:** 5 | **~lines:** 52
- **sites:** todo/services/task_service.py:305-318 (update_task), todo/services/task_service.py:388-401 (update_task_with_assignee_from_dict), todo/services/task_service.py:480-492 (update_task_with_assignee), todo/services/task_service.py:598-615 (create_task — extended form), todo/services/task_assignment_service.py:29-39 (create_task_assignment)
- **drift:** All three claimed divergences verified. (1) Unknown user_type: task_assignment_service.py:38-39 raises ValueError("Invalid user_type"); all four TaskService copies have no else branch and fall through silently. (2) task_service.py:608-611 (create_task) additionally validates the optional team_id on a user assignment — `if team_id: team = TeamRepository.get_by_id(team_id); if not team: raise ValueError(...)`; none of the three update copies validate team_id, so an update can attach a non-existent team that create would reject. (3) The fetched-user local is named `assignee_data` at :312 but `user_data` at :395 and :486 — hand-copy fingerprint. Note the finder's own hedge is resolved: UpdateTaskSerializer.validate_assignee (todo/serializers/update_task_serializer.py:74-75) already rejects user_type outside {team,user} for the two dict paths, so making the else branch raise everywhere is behaviourally free on those endpoints; only update_task_with_assignee (CreateTaskDTO.assignee is an unvalidated `dict | None`, todo/dto/task_dto.py:42) is genuinely unguarded today.
- **proposed:** New `todo/utils/assignee_validation_utils.py` (sits beside the existing todo/utils/task_validation_utils.py, which is already the precedent for this kind of shared validator) exposing `validate_assignee_exists(assignee_id: str, user_type: str, team_id: str | None = None) -> None`. It raises UserNotFoundException for a missing user, the new TeamNotFoundException (see finding on the nine bare ValueErrors) for a missing team or missing team_id, and ValueError("Invalid user_type") for anything else. All five sites become a single call; create_task passes team_id, the update paths gain that validation for free.

## 16. Task update-payload mapping loop written 3× — one copy has a comparison bug, one has the only audit log, none has all the rules
- **dimension:** services | **severity:** high | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 3 | **~lines:** 48
- **sites:** todo/services/task_service.py:324-335 + 347-368 (update_task), todo/services/task_service.py:404-449 (update_task_with_assignee_from_dict), todo/services/task_service.py:494-529 (update_task_with_assignee)
- **drift:** Stronger than the finder claimed, and now concretely provable. Both update_task (view todo/views/task.py:297-301) and update_task_with_assignee_from_dict (todo/views/task.py:343-345) are fed `serializer.validated_data` from the SAME UpdateTaskSerializer — identical input, two divergent implementations. (a) Real bug from copying the DTO version into the dict version: task_service.py:429 tests `validated_data.get("status") == TaskStatus.IN_PROGRESS`, but TaskStatus is a plain Enum (todo/constants/task.py:4-10) and UpdateTaskSerializer.status is a ChoiceField over `status.name` strings (todo/serializers/update_task_serializer.py:18-22), so the comparison is always False and the startedAt rule is DEAD on that endpoint; two lines later (:434) the same method compares against `TaskStatus.DEFERRED.value`, i.e. the correct string form. The DTO-based copy at :519 (`dto.status == TaskStatus.IN_PROGRESS`) works because dto.status really is an enum. (b) Only copy 1 writes the status_changed audit row (:353-363), so a status change routed through either of the other two endpoints produces no audit record. (c) The deferredDetails clearing rules exist only in copy 2 (:432-440); copy 3 never got them. (d) Copy 1 alone performs no-op writes.
- **proposed:** Two classmethods on TaskService: `_build_task_update_payload(cls, current_task: TaskModel, fields: dict, user_id: str) -> dict` owning the assignee skip, unchanged-value skip, labels/enum/direct mapping, the startedAt rule and the deferredDetails rules; and `_apply_task_update(cls, current_task: TaskModel, payload: dict, user_id: str) -> TaskModel` owning the updatedBy stamp, the TaskRepository.update call, the TaskNotFoundException on a miss and the single status_changed audit write. The three public methods then differ only in how they normalise input into `fields` (validated_data, validated_data-minus-assignee, or dto.model_dump). Normalise status to its string value once, at the top of _build_task_update_payload, so the enum-vs-string comparison bug cannot recur.

## 17. TeamModel -> TeamDTO mapped field-by-field 5× in one file
- **dimension:** services | **severity:** medium | **effort:** small | **copies:** 5 | **~lines:** 44
- **sites:** todo/services/team_service.py:145-155 (create_team), todo/services/team_service.py:212-222 (get_user_teams), todo/services/team_service.py:247-257 (get_team_by_id), todo/services/team_service.py:314-324 (join_team_by_invite_code), todo/services/team_service.py:371-381 (update_team)
- **drift:** Verified. (a) All five omit TeamDTO.users, which the DTO declares (todo/dto/team_dto.py:52) — so the enrichment has to be bolted on outside the service, in the view: todo/views/team.py:165-167 does `users = UserService.get_users_by_team_id(team_id); team_dto.users = [u.dict() for u in users]`. (b) The codebase already has the right convention one directory over: RoleDTO.from_model (todo/dto/role_dto.py:25-55) is a real classmethod mapper with attribute validation and enum unwrapping, and RoleService uses it (todo/services/role_service.py:17, :27). team_service is the outlier. (c) get_user_teams (team_service.py:209-210) re-fetches each team one at a time inside the loop — `team = TeamRepository.get_by_id(str(user_team.team_id))` — an N+1 that exists only because there is no shared mapper to feed from a batch query.
- **proposed:** `TeamDTO.from_model(cls, team: TeamModel, users: list | None = None) -> TeamDTO` in todo/dto/team_dto.py, mirroring RoleDTO.from_model. All five sites collapse to `TeamDTO.from_model(team)`, and todo/views/team.py:165-167 becomes `TeamDTO.from_model(team, users=[u.dict() for u in users])` — the users-enrichment finally has one place to live. Guard the `str(None)` hazard the finder flagged inside the mapper (`str(team.updated_by) if team.updated_by else None`) rather than in five call sites.

## 18. The 500-error ApiErrorResponse wrapper pasted into 5 service except blocks, one with the wrong source label
- **dimension:** services | **severity:** medium | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 5 | **~lines:** 60
- **sites:** todo/services/task_service.py:655-670 (create_task, ValueError re-raise guard), todo/services/task_service.py:671-684 (create_task, catch-all), todo/services/watchlist_service.py:49-62 (get_watchlisted_tasks, catch-all), todo/services/watchlist_service.py:101-116 (add_task, ValueError re-raise guard), todo/services/watchlist_service.py:117-130 (add_task, catch-all), (same envelope unpacked, out of dimension: todo/views/task.py:152, todo/views/task.py:350, todo/views/team.py:46, :89, :301, todo/views/watchlist.py:97)
- **drift:** Both claims verified plus a third. (a) watchlist_service.py:110 still reports `source={ApiErrorSource.PARAMETER: "task_repository"}` inside WatchlistService.add_task, which writes to the watchlist collection — the label was inherited from the TaskService block it was copied from and now mislabels the failing component in the response payload. (b) The `e.args[0]` IndexError hazard is real AND has already been fixed in exactly one of the seven copies: todo/views/task.py:350 reads `if isinstance(e, ValueError) and e.args and isinstance(e.args[0], ApiErrorResponse):` while todo/views/task.py:152, todo/views/team.py:46/:89/:301, todo/views/watchlist.py:97, todo/services/task_service.py:656 and todo/services/watchlist_service.py:102 all still index blindly. That is a one-copy bugfix that never propagated — the textbook signal. It matters because pydantic's ValidationError subclasses ValueError. (c) The DEBUG ternary is parenthesised in task_service and not in watchlist_service.
- **proposed:** New module `todo/exceptions/error_response_utils.py` with two functions: `reraise_if_api_error(e: BaseException) -> None` (safely `if isinstance(e, ValueError) and e.args and isinstance(e.args[0], ApiErrorResponse): raise e`) and `raise_api_error(status_code: int, message: str, source: str, exc: Exception, title: str = ApiErrors.UNEXPECTED_ERROR) -> NoReturn` which builds the envelope and applies the DEBUG ternary once. `source` is a required positional argument so it can never be silently inherited by the next copy-paste. Each except block drops to two lines. The same two helpers also de-duplicate the six unpack sites in todo/views/.

## 19. Pagination link math and URL building reimplemented in 4 services with 2 rounding formulas and 2 response shapes
- **dimension:** services | **severity:** medium | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 4 | **~lines:** 48
- **sites:** todo/services/task_service.py:137-157 (_build_pagination_links + build_page_url), todo/services/watchlist_service.py:188-208 (_build_pagination_links + build_page_url), todo/services/label_service.py:64-83 (prepare_pagination_links + build_page_url), todo/services/team_creation_invite_code_service.py:62-67 (inline in get_all_codes)
- **drift:** All five divergences verified. (1) Rounding: math.ceil on float division at task_service.py:141 and watchlist_service.py:192 vs integer `(total_count + limit - 1) // limit` at label_service.py:32 and team_creation_invite_code_service.py:62. (2) Contract: three return LinksData(next=, prev=); TeamCreationInviteCodeService returns bare `next_url`/`previous_url` strings on its response object, so one paginated endpoint exposes a different shape for the same concept. (3) URL construction: three use reverse_lazy + urlencode; the fourth hand-builds an f-string with a caller-supplied `base_url` and no escaping. (4) Out-of-range guard: only label_service.py:34-40 returns ApiErrors.PAGE_NOT_FOUND when page > total_pages. (5) task_service.py:693-707 (get_tasks_for_user) validates page and limit and then hardcodes `links=None` on both returns — the links were simply never wired up on that path.
- **proposed:** New `todo/utils/pagination_utils.py` with `total_pages(total_count: int, limit: int) -> int` (one rounding rule) and `build_links(view_name: str, page: int, limit: int, total_count: int, **extra_params) -> LinksData` where extra_params feeds urlencode. TaskService calls `build_links("tasks", page, limit, total, sort_by=sort_by, order=order)`, WatchlistService `build_links("watchlist", page, limit, count)`, LabelService `build_links("labels", page, limit, total, search=search)`. Migrate the invite-code endpoint to LinksData as a separate, announced change. Add an optional `page_out_of_range(page, total_pages) -> bool` that endpoints opt into, so LabelService's guard is available without silently converting today's empty-200 into an error elsewhere.

## 20. AuditLogRepository.create(AuditLogModel(...)) hand-built at 13 call sites with magic action strings and inconsistent id coercion
- **dimension:** services | **severity:** medium | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 13 | **~lines:** 78
- **sites:** todo/services/team_service.py:136-142 (team_created), todo/services/team_service.py:157-163 (team_creation_invite_code_consumed), todo/services/team_service.py:305-311 (member_joined_team), todo/services/team_service.py:362-368 (poc_changed), todo/services/team_service.py:456-463 (member_added_to_team), todo/services/team_service.py:511-517 (member_removed_from_team / member_left_team), todo/services/task_service.py:355-363 (status_changed), todo/services/task_assignment_service.py:46-53 (unassigned_from_team), todo/services/task_assignment_service.py:75-82 (assigned_to_member), todo/services/task_assignment_service.py:86-93 (assigned_to_team), todo/services/team_creation_invite_code_service.py:29-34 (team_creation_invite_code_generated), todo/views/task_assignment.py:245-253 (reassign_executor — 12th site, missed), todo/repositories/task_assignment_repository.py:429-435 (assigned_to_team — 13th site, missed)
- **drift:** All three claimed drifts verified, and two more found. (1) Id coercion differs per site: `team_id=created_team.id` / `team.id` (already PyObjectId) at :138, :307, :458 vs `team_id=PyObjectId(team_id)` from a string at :364, :513; views/task_assignment.py:250 passes `spoc_id=request.user_id` as a raw string with no coercion at all. (2) Only team_service.py:515 defends a missing actor (`PyObjectId(removed_by_user_id) if removed_by_user_id else PyObjectId(user_id)`); the other twelve throw on a None actor. (3) The thirteen action strings are free-text literals with no constants module, unlike every other enumerated value (todo/constants/task.py, todo/constants/role.py) — and "assigned_to_team" is now written from two different layers. (4) NEW: team_service.py:461 passes `details={"added_member_id": member_id}` but AuditLogModel (todo/models/audit_log.py) has no `details` field, so under pydantic 2.10's default extra="ignore" that payload is silently discarded — the only site that tried to record per-member context records nothing. (5) NEW: create_team writes one "team_created" row (:136-142) and no per-member rows, while add_team_members writes one "member_added_to_team" row per member (:455-463), so members added at creation time have no audit trail.
- **proposed:** New `todo/constants/audit.py` with `class AuditAction(str, Enum)` enumerating the thirteen literals, and `todo/services/audit_service.py` with `AuditService.record(action: AuditAction, *, performed_by: str | PyObjectId | None, task_id=None, team_id=None, **fields) -> None`. It coerces every id through one `_oid()` helper that accepts str/ObjectId/None, tolerates a missing actor, and — after the team deciding whether audit is best-effort — optionally logs-and-swallows storage failures so an audit write cannot take down the business operation. Every one of the 13 sites becomes one call. Adding a real `details: dict | None` field to AuditLogModel is a prerequisite so team_service.py:461 stops silently dropping data.

## 21. TaskAssignmentModel -> DTO mapped 3× over two near-identical DTO classes; the create path silently drops team_id and assignee_name
- **dimension:** services | **severity:** medium | **effort:** small | **copies:** 3 | **~lines:** 27

## 22. _prepare_label_dtos duplicated in TaskService and WatchlistService; only one copy got the ObjectId conversion fix
- **dimension:** services | **severity:** medium | **effort:** small | **copies:** 3 | **~lines:** 24
- **sites:** todo/services/task_service.py:203-214 (TaskService._prepare_label_dtos), todo/services/watchlist_service.py:140-152 (WatchlistService._prepare_label_dtos), todo/services/label_service.py:85-111 (LabelService.prepare_label_dto — third LabelDTO builder)
- **drift:** Both drifts verified. (1) The `# Convert here!` comment at watchlist_service.py:142 is a bugfix comment applied to one copy only. LabelRepository.list_by_ids is typed `List[ObjectId]` and queries `{"_id": {"$in": ids}}` (todo/repositories/label_repository.py:13-18), so string ids match nothing. TaskService's copy passes label_ids straight through and survives only because TaskModel.labels is `List[PyObjectId]` (todo/models/task.py:33) — an undocumented, load-bearing assumption in one copy and an explicit conversion in the other. (2) LabelService.prepare_label_dto (label_service.py:103-110) passes createdAt, updatedAt, createdBy and updatedBy to LabelDTO, but LabelDTO declares only id/name/color (todo/dto/label_dto.py:4-7); under pydantic 2.10 (requirements.txt:14) extra kwargs default to "ignore", so all four are discarded — which makes the 16 lines at label_service.py:88-101 that fabricate createdBy/updatedBy UserDTOs dead work whose output never reaches a client.
- **proposed:** `LabelDTO.from_model(cls, label: LabelModel) -> LabelDTO` in todo/dto/label_dto.py, plus one shared `prepare_label_dtos(label_ids: Iterable[str | ObjectId]) -> list[LabelDTO]` in todo/utils/ that normalises ids to ObjectId exactly once before hitting the repository. TaskService._prepare_label_dtos and WatchlistService._prepare_label_dtos are deleted and their two call sites (task_service.py:161, watchlist_service.py:156) point at the shared function; LabelService.prepare_label_dto uses LabelDTO.from_model. Decide separately whether LabelDTO should actually grow the four audit fields or whether label_service.py:88-101 should be deleted — extracting the mapper without that decision only relocates the dead code.

## 23. Assignee-name resolution by user_type duplicated, with opposite behaviour when the assignee record is gone
- **dimension:** services | **severity:** medium | **effort:** small | **copies:** 2 | **~lines:** 16
- **sites:** todo/services/task_service.py:217-231 (_prepare_assignee_dto), todo/services/task_assignment_service.py:133-141 (get_task_assignment)
- **drift:** Verified: the two copies answer the identical 'assignment row exists but the user/team record is gone' question in opposite ways. TaskService._prepare_assignee_dto returns None on an unknown user_type (:227) and None again on a lookup miss (:229-230); prepare_task_dto then emits `assignee=None` (task_service.py:169), so the task renders as unassigned. TaskAssignmentService.get_task_assignment instead substitutes "Unknown User" / "Unknown Team" / "Unknown" and still returns a populated assignment. The same orphaned row is therefore reported as 'no assignee' by GET /tasks and 'assigned to Unknown User' by the assignment endpoint. This dispatch is also the same `user_type -> UserRepository / TeamRepository` switch that appears five more times in the assignee-validation finding, so a single resolver covers seven sites in total.
- **proposed:** `resolve_assignee(assignee_id: str, user_type: str) -> tuple[Any | None, str | None]` in todo/utils/assignee_validation_utils.py (the same module as validate_assignee_exists), returning the fetched record and its display name, with one documented policy for a miss. Fold it into TaskAssignmentDTO.from_model so both endpoints get the same answer: `TaskAssignmentDTO.from_model(assignment, assignee_name=resolve_assignee(...)[1])`. Pick one behaviour for a missing record — a placeholder name is the safer default since it preserves the fact that an assignment exists — and record the choice in the helper's docstring rather than in two call sites.

## 24. Five non-equivalent implementations of "is this user an active member of this team"
- **dimension:** services | **severity:** high | **effort:** large | **copies:** 6 | **~lines:** 22
- **sites:** todo/services/team_service.py:282-285 (loop over get_by_user_id, is_active), todo/services/team_service.py:410-414 (any(...) over get_by_user_id, is_active), todo/services/team_service.py:425-426 (membership of get_users_by_team_id list), todo/services/team_service.py:487-491 (UserService.get_users_by_team_id, compare ids), todo/services/team_service.py:544-549 (UserRoleService.has_role MEMBER/TEAM), todo/services/task_service.py:82-93 (TeamRepository.is_user_team_member)
- **drift:** The implementations are demonstrably not equivalent. (a) and (b)/(e) read different queries: get_by_user_id filters `{"user_id": user_id, "is_active": True}` (todo/repositories/team_repository.py:217) while get_users_by_team_id filters `{"team_id": team_id, "is_active": True}` (:229) — and TeamRepository.is_user_team_member (:118-123), which TaskService.get_tasks depends on at task_service.py:85, is built on the latter. That the id types genuinely disagree in stored data is not speculation: the second, separately-defined UserTeamDetailsRepository (todo/repositories/user_team_details_repository.py:21-26 and again :45-50) brute-forces FOUR queries per lookup — {oid,oid}, {str,oid}, {oid,str}, {str,str} — precisely because it cannot rely on the stored type. (c) answers a different question entirely (a user_roles row, not a user_team_details row) and will disagree whenever TeamService._assign_user_role swallowed a failure (team_service.py:179-184). Error messages for the same condition also diverge three ways: "User is already a member of this team." (:285) vs "You must be a member of the team to add other members" (:414) vs ValidationErrors.USER_NOT_TEAM_MEMBER (:547). Compounding it, team_service imports two different classes both named UserTeamDetailsRepository — from todo.repositories.team_repository at line 7 and from todo.repositories.user_team_details_repository at line 504.
- **proposed:** One authority: `TeamMembershipService` in todo/services/team_membership_service.py with `is_member(user_id: str, team_id: str) -> bool` and `require_member(user_id: str, team_id: str) -> None` raising a single NotTeamMemberException(ValidationErrors.USER_NOT_TEAM_MEMBER), both delegating to exactly one repository query with one id-coercion rule. All six sites call it; TeamRepository.is_user_team_member becomes a thin delegate or is deleted; the duplicate UserTeamDetailsRepository class is merged away. Sequence matters: land the id-type normalisation (pick ObjectId, backfill, delete the four-query fallbacks in todo/repositories/user_team_details_repository.py) as its own change FIRST, since consolidating the query shape before that will grant or revoke access on real data.

## 25. PaginationConfig declared 3× (one not reading settings) and page/limit validation written twice with two exception types
- **dimension:** services | **severity:** medium | **effort:** small | **copies:** 5 | **~lines:** 26
- **sites:** todo/services/task_service.py:51-55 (@dataclass PaginationConfig), todo/services/watchlist_service.py:23-26 (plain class PaginationConfig), todo/services/label_service.py:14-19 (@dataclass PaginationConfig, hardcoded DEFAULT_LIMIT), todo/services/task_service.py:126-135 (_validate_pagination_params), todo/services/user_service.py:97-114 (_validate_search_params)
- **drift:** Textbook. (1) label_service.py:17 hardcodes `DEFAULT_LIMIT: int = 10` instead of reading settings, where todo_project/settings/base.py:104 says DEFAULT_PAGE_LIMIT = 20 — so the labels endpoint silently pages differently from tasks and watchlist; it also adds a SEARCH field the other two lack. (2) watchlist_service.py:23 is a plain class where the other two are @dataclass. (3) todo/constants/messages.py:71-73 already defines PAGE_POSITIVE / LIMIT_POSITIVE / MAX_LIMIT_EXCEEDED with byte-identical text; UserService uses them, TaskService hardcodes the same strings as literals at :129, :132, :135. (4) They cap differently — PaginationConfig.MAX_LIMIT is 200 from settings (base.py:105) vs a hardcoded 100 in UserService. (5) They raise different types for the same condition: django.core.exceptions.ValidationError with a bare message (task_service.py:3) vs rest_framework.exceptions.ValidationError with a field-keyed dict (user_service.py:8), so identical bad input produces two different HTTP error shapes.
- **proposed:** Move PaginationConfig to a single `todo/constants/pagination.py` (all three modules import it; label_service keeps SEARCH as its own local default or gains a documented override) and add `validate_pagination_params(page: int, limit: int, max_limit: int = PaginationConfig.MAX_LIMIT) -> None` to todo/utils/pagination_utils.py, raising one exception type and using the ValidationErrors constants. Keep max_limit an explicit per-caller argument so UserService's 100 stays 100 rather than being silently widened to 200.

## 26. Two unrelated exception classes both named UserNotFoundException — one maps to 404, the other falls through to 500
- **dimension:** services | **severity:** high | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 2 | **~lines:** 20
- **sites:** todo/exceptions/user_exceptions.py:4-13 (plain Exception, takes user_id), todo/exceptions/auth_exceptions.py:40-42 (BaseAuthException, takes only a message), todo/services/user_service.py:26-30 (raises the auth variant), todo/services/task_service.py:260-265, :314 (raises the user_exceptions variant), todo/services/task_assignment_service.py:31-33 (raises the user_exceptions variant), todo/repositories/user_repository.py:27, :44 (raises the auth variant), todo/exceptions/exception_handler.py:173-181 and :192-200 (the handler branch itself is duplicated)
- **drift:** The duplication is already load-bearing and wrong. todo/exceptions/exception_handler.py:26 imports ONLY the user_exceptions variant, so its `elif isinstance(exc, UserNotFoundException)` branches map only TaskService/TaskAssignmentService failures to 404. The auth variant extends BaseAuthException (auth_exceptions.py:4-7, 40-42) but NOT AuthException, so it also misses the `elif isinstance(exc, AuthException)` branch at exception_handler.py:155 — meaning UserService.get_user_by_id raising 'user not found' falls through to a generic 500 while TaskService raising it for the identical reason returns 404. And the handler contains the same `isinstance(exc, UserNotFoundException)` branch twice, at :173-181 and :192-200; the second is unreachable dead code, itself a copy-paste artifact (the only difference is ApiErrorSource.PARAMETER vs ApiErrorSource.PATH).
- **proposed:** Delete `auth_exceptions.UserNotFoundException`. Keep the richer `todo/exceptions/user_exceptions.UserNotFoundException(user_id=None)` and repoint UserService (user_service.py:4-7, :20, :29) and UserRepository (user_repository.py:10, :27, :44) at it. Then collapse exception_handler.py:173-200 to a single branch that uses `{ApiErrorSource.PATH: "user_id"} if user_id else {ApiErrorSource.PARAMETER: "user_id"}`. Because user_repository raises the auth variant inside `except ... from e` and user_service re-raises it by name in a tuple with APIException/DRFValidationError (user_service.py:20), every `except UserNotFoundException` and `except BaseAuthException` in views and middleware must be re-checked in the same commit.

## 27. UserTeamDetailsModel built with the same six-field boilerplate at 5 sites — including a field the model does not have
- **dimension:** services | **severity:** medium | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 5 | **~lines:** 32
- **sites:** todo/services/team_service.py:82-89 (create_team, members), todo/services/team_service.py:94-101 (create_team, POC), todo/services/team_service.py:106-113 (create_team, creator), todo/services/team_service.py:291-298 (join_team_by_invite_code), todo/services/team_service.py:437-444 (add_team_members)
- **drift:** NEW and decisive: `role_id` is not a field on UserTeamDetailsModel at all (todo/models/team.py:46-59 declares only user_id, team_id, is_active, created_at, updated_at, created_by, updated_by), so under pydantic 2.10's default extra="ignore" the DEFAULT_ROLE_ID="1" written at all five sites is silently discarded on every insert. Five copies of a line that does nothing — nobody noticed because there is no single place to look. The surrounding sequence has also drifted: create_team's three copies (:81-114) add rows and assign roles in a separate later loop (:121-134); join_team_by_invite_code adds one row then calls _assign_user_role (:302); add_team_members assigns roles only inside `if new_user_teams:` (:447-452) and writes its audit rows in yet another loop (:455-463). And _assign_user_role deliberately swallows failures (:179-184), so a membership row can exist with no corresponding role record, silently.
- **proposed:** `TeamService._add_members(cls, team_id: PyObjectId, member_ids: Iterable[str], actor_id: str, role: RoleName = RoleName.MEMBER) -> list[UserTeamDetailsModel]` that de-duplicates the id set, builds the rows, calls create_many, assigns the roles and writes the member_added audit entries as ONE unit — which also closes the missing-audit gap in create_team noted in the audit-log finding. Delete the dead `role_id=DEFAULT_ROLE_ID` and the DEFAULT_ROLE_ID constant (team_service.py:26) rather than enshrining it in the helper; it is already a no-op. create_team then calls `cls._add_members(created_team.id, {*member_ids, dto.poc_id, created_by_user_id} - {None}, created_by_user_id)` once instead of three guarded blocks.

## 28. "Team not found" raised as a bare ValueError at 9 sites in 2 message formats, with no exception class behind it
- **dimension:** services | **severity:** medium | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 9 | **~lines:** 20
- **sites:** todo/services/team_service.py:244-246 (get_team_by_id), todo/services/team_service.py:342-344 (update_team), todo/services/team_service.py:403-405 (add_team_members), todo/services/task_service.py:316-318, todo/services/task_service.py:399-401, todo/services/task_service.py:490-492, todo/services/task_service.py:609-611, todo/services/task_service.py:613-615, todo/services/task_assignment_service.py:35-37, todo/services/team_service.py:420-422 (same gap for a missing user)
- **drift:** Verified by grep across todo/services: exactly two message formats for one condition — "Team with id {x} not found" (3 sites, all in team_service.py) vs "Team not found: {x}" (6 sites, all in task_service.py/task_assignment_service.py) — split by file, which is how you can tell they were written independently. Neither string lives in todo/constants/messages.py even though every other user-facing message does. todo/exceptions/team_exceptions.py defines BaseTeamException, CannotRemoveOwnerException, NotTeamAdminException and CannotRemoveTeamPOCException but has NO TeamNotFoundException — so the one team error that occurs nine times is the only one with no class, todo/exceptions/exception_handler.py cannot map it to 404 the way it maps TaskNotFoundException at :182-190, and it degrades to a generic 500. team_service.py:422 is the same gap for users, where UserNotFoundException already exists and is used elsewhere for exactly that.
- **proposed:** Add `ApiErrors.TEAM_NOT_FOUND = "Team with id {0} not found"` to todo/constants/messages.py and `TeamNotFoundException(BaseTeamException)` taking a team_id to todo/exceptions/team_exceptions.py, plus a handler branch beside TaskNotFoundException in exception_handler.py. Replace all nine raises; replace team_service.py:422 with the existing UserNotFoundException. Pair it with `TeamService.get_team_or_raise(team_id) -> TeamModel` so the three fetch-and-raise preambles (:244-246, :342-344, :403-405) also collapse to one line. Critically, this must land together with narrowing the two catch-alls at team_service.py:169-170 and :478-479 (`except Exception as e: raise ValueError(f"Failed to ...: {str(e)}")`), or the new typed exception is swallowed and nothing improves.

## 29. "Deferred task overrides stored status" business rule computed independently in two DTO mappers
- **dimension:** services | **severity:** low | **effort:** small | **copies:** 2 | **~lines:** 8
- **sites:** todo/services/task_service.py:178-181 (prepare_task_dto), todo/services/watchlist_service.py:163-168 (prepare_watchlisted_task_dto)
- **drift:** The two read-side copies have not yet diverged textually, but they are two of five places that reason about deferral with no owner, and the write side has already drifted: defer_task writes `status = TaskStatus.TODO.value` while stashing deferredDetails (task_service.py:581-585); update_task_with_assignee_from_dict clears deferredDetails on a non-DEFERRED status and rewrites status back to the current value on DEFERRED (task_service.py:432-440); update_task_with_assignee has none of those rules (task_service.py:518-520 handles only startedAt). Both read-side copies compare a stored deferredTill against `datetime.now(timezone.utc)` with no tzinfo normalisation, while defer_task explicitly normalises before writing (task_service.py:559-560) — so a naive datetime read back from Mongo raises TypeError in both mappers, and the fix would have to be applied twice. Note DeferredDetailsModel.deferredTill is `datetime | None` (todo/models/task.py:16), so a None also raises in both copies.
- **proposed:** `effective_task_status(status, deferred_details) -> str` in a new todo/utils/task_status_utils.py, taking plain values rather than a model (WatchlistService's copy operates on an aggregation result, not a TaskModel, per watchlist_service.py:155). It owns the None checks, the tz normalisation and the comparison. Both mappers call it. Ideally the write-side deferral rules move next to it so the whole deferral concept lives in one module — but the read-side extraction stands on its own and is safe to land first.

## 30. MISSED: PostgresSyncService._sync_labels_table and _sync_roles_table are the same 50-line routine twice
- **dimension:** services | **severity:** medium | **effort:** small | **copies:** 2 | **~lines:** 50
- **sites:** todo/services/postgres_sync_service.py:128-180 (_sync_labels_table), todo/services/postgres_sync_service.py:182-228 (_sync_roles_table), todo/services/postgres_sync_service.py:96-108 (_get_mongo_collection_count — the soft-delete rule duplicated a third time)
- **drift:** The two copies have already drifted in observability: the labels copy logs `f"Will sync {mongo_count - postgres_count} labels to PostgreSQL"` (:144) and `logger.debug(f"Creating label in PostgreSQL: {postgres_data}")` (:165); the roles copy has neither, so when the roles sync misbehaves there is no equivalent diagnostic. The soft-delete knowledge is duplicated a third time and inconsistently: _get_mongo_collection_count branches on `if collection_name == "labels": count_documents({"isDeleted": {"$ne": True}}) else count_documents({})` (:100-104), and _sync_labels_table repeats that same filter inline at :148 while _sync_roles_table repeats the empty one at :201 — so the count and the fetch can disagree the moment a third collection is added, and adding one means writing the whole 50-line method a third time (sync_all_tables at :37-40 is already a table-driven list, which shows the author intended this to be data, not code).
- **proposed:** One parameterised `_sync_collection(self, *, table_name: str, collection_name: str, model, transform, mongo_filter: dict) -> bool` on PostgresSyncService, and turn the sync_operations list at :37-40 — which is already table-driven — into the single source of truth by putting the five per-collection values in it. _sync_labels_table and _sync_roles_table disappear; adding a third collection becomes one tuple. Fold the soft-delete decision into that same table so _get_mongo_collection_count takes the filter instead of branching on the collection name.

## 31. MISSED: DualWriteService repeats the model-lookup guard and the sync-failure tail in all three CRUD methods
- **dimension:** services | **severity:** low | **effort:** small | **copies:** 3 | **~lines:** 27
- **sites:** todo/services/dual_write_service.py:63-66 + 85-89 (create_document), todo/services/dual_write_service.py:104-107 + 138-142 (update_document), todo/services/dual_write_service.py:156-159 + 179-183 (delete_document)
- **drift:** Modest but real: the three copies of the same failure policy have diverged in what they treat as recoverable. update_document adds `except postgres_model.DoesNotExist: return self.create_document(...)` (:135-137) and delete_document adds `except postgres_model.DoesNotExist: logger.warning(...); return True` (:176-178), while create_document has no DoesNotExist branch at all — three sites, three answers to 'the row is not there'. The labels handling drifted the same way: create_document guards `if collection_name == "tasks" and labels:` (:79) where update_document calls _sync_task_labels unconditionally on `if collection_name == "tasks":` (:129), so an update with an empty label list clears labels while a create with an empty list does not. The subclass repeats a fourth near-identical guard shape four times as well (todo/services/enhanced_dual_write_service.py:24-26, 34-36, 44-46, 54-56).
- **proposed:** Two private helpers on DualWriteService: `_require_model(self, collection_name: str)` returning the model or None after logging once, and `_fail(self, collection_name: str, mongo_id: str, verb: str, exc: Exception) -> bool` that formats the message, logs, records the failure and returns False. Each of the three methods drops 6 lines and the 'not found' policy becomes visible as a single explicit choice per method rather than an accident of which copy was edited last. Honest assessment: this is the smallest item here — worth doing when the file is next touched, not on its own.

## 32. Two rival UserTeamDetailsRepository classes over the same collection, with opposite delete semantics
- **dimension:** repositories | **severity:** high | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 2 | **~lines:** 70
- **sites:** todo/repositories/team_repository.py:126-387, todo/repositories/user_team_details_repository.py:6-68, todo/repositories/team_repository.py:273-319, todo/repositories/user_team_details_repository.py:34-68, todo/repositories/team_repository.py:379, todo/services/team_service.py:7, todo/services/team_service.py:504-506
- **drift:** The two implementations are mutually incompatible on the same rows. (1) Delete semantics: soft (is_active=False, dual-write update_document) vs hard (delete_one, dual-write delete_document). Removing a member through TeamService.remove_member_from_team destroys the row; removing the same member by editing the member list through update_team_members only flips is_active. (2) Read predicate: every read in the team_repository copy filters is_active (lines 217, 229, 256, 268, 280, 285), while the other copy's get_by_user_and_team (user_team_details_repository.py:21-26) has no is_active filter, so it returns rows the first copy treats as removed. (3) Collection-name source drifted: UserTeamDetailsModel.collection_name vs the hand-typed literal "user_team_details".
- **proposed:** Collapse to one class in todo/repositories/user_team_details_repository.py, keyed off UserTeamDetailsModel.collection_name, and re-export it from team_repository for the existing import path. Because the two removal semantics are genuinely different operations (leave/replace vs purge), do NOT merge them into one method — expose both explicitly on the single class: `deactivate_membership(team_id, user_id, updated_by) -> bool` (today's team_repository.remove_user_from_team) and `purge_membership(team_id, user_id) -> bool` (today's remove_member_from_team), plus one `find_membership(user_id, team_id, *, active_only: bool = True)` that both use. Then update team_service.py:504-506 to call the explicitly named method it actually wants, and delete the function-local import. Test patch targets must move with it: todo/tests/unit/services/test_team_service.py:264,289 patch todo.repositories.user_team_details_repository.UserTeamDetailsRepository.remove_member_from_team, and todo/tests/unit/repositories/test_user_repository.py:100 patches todo.repositories.team_repository.UserTeamDetailsRepository.

## 33. Dual-write sync epilogue (instantiate service, hand-build payload, call, re-import logging, warn) repeated in 21 repository methods
- **dimension:** repositories | **severity:** high | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 21 | **~lines:** 330
- **sites:** todo/repositories/team_repository.py:27-48, todo/repositories/team_repository.py:142-161, todo/repositories/team_repository.py:186-206, todo/repositories/team_repository.py:296-315, todo/repositories/task_assignment_repository.py:27-48, todo/repositories/task_assignment_repository.py:137-158, todo/repositories/task_assignment_repository.py:209-230, todo/repositories/task_assignment_repository.py:272-293, todo/repositories/task_assignment_repository.py:334-355, todo/repositories/task_assignment_repository.py:464-520, todo/repositories/task_repository.py:225-251, todo/repositories/task_repository.py:323-348, todo/repositories/user_repository.py:73-91, todo/repositories/user_role_repository.py:43-62, todo/repositories/user_role_repository.py:144-163, todo/repositories/watchlist_repository.py:44-63, todo/repositories/watchlist_repository.py:349-368, todo/repositories/audit_log_repository.py:18-42, todo/repositories/team_creation_invite_code_repository.py:39-58, todo/repositories/team_creation_invite_code_repository.py:73-92, todo/repositories/user_team_details_repository.py:56-65
- **drift:** CONFIRMED drift, with one of the finder's claims corrected. (1) Failure handling: 20 sites log and continue; task_assignment_repository.py:514-520 logs and then `return False`, aborting the caller. (2) Sites nested inside a blanket `except Exception: return False` (task_assignment_repository.py:233-234, :296-297, :358-359; team_repository.py:318-319) swallow a dual-write exception with no log at all. (3) The function-local `import logging; logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)` runs 21 times and in user_role_repository.py shadows the module-level logger already defined at line 11 (shadowed at :59-61 and :160-162). (4) Message phrasing: 20 distinct strings for one event (the finder said 12; the actual distinct count is 20). CORRECTION: the finder's claim that the collection_name literal 'drifted from the Mongo collection it mirrors' is WRONG.
