"watchlists" and "task_assignments" are keys of DualWriteService.COLLECTION_MODEL_MAP (todo/services/dual_write_service.py:31-43), which deliberately differ from WatchlistModel.collection_name=="watchlist" and TaskAssignmentModel.collection_name=="task_details". A helper must therefore NOT derive the argument from cls.collection_name.
- **proposed:** Add the epilogue to MongoRepository in todo/repositories/common/mongo_repository.py as `@classmethod def sync_to_postgres(cls, op: Literal["create","update","delete"], mongo_id, data: dict | None = None) -> bool`. It owns the single EnhancedDualWriteService instance, resolves the target from a new required class attribute `postgres_collection_name` (NOT cls.collection_name — two collections legitimately differ), and emits one standard warning through a module-level logger. Leave the per-entity payload dicts at the call sites for now (they are separately addressed by the payload-mapper findings); this removes the ~5-line tail from all 21 sites and the 21 function-local logging imports. Unifying failure handling requires an explicit decision on task_assignment_repository.py:514-520 — keep it as the one hard-fail by having that call site check the return value itself, rather than changing the helper's contract.

## 34. "Try ObjectId, then try the raw string" query fallback hand-written 10 times in three different shapes
- **dimension:** repositories | **severity:** high | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 10 | **~lines:** 115
- **sites:** todo/repositories/task_assignment_repository.py:57-69, todo/repositories/task_assignment_repository.py:76-89, todo/repositories/task_assignment_repository.py:112-133, todo/repositories/task_assignment_repository.py:183-205, todo/repositories/task_assignment_repository.py:245-268, todo/repositories/task_assignment_repository.py:308-330, todo/repositories/task_assignment_repository.py:372-385, todo/repositories/user_team_details_repository.py:11-32, todo/repositories/user_team_details_repository.py:36-53, todo/repositories/task_repository.py:132-138
- **drift:** CONFIRMED and worse than claimed. (1) task_assignment_repository.py:112-133 runs BOTH update_many calls unconditionally with no `modified_count == 0` guard, unlike its three verified-identical siblings at :183-205, :245-268 and :308-330 — so the string pass always re-runs. (2) task_assignment_repository.py:82 is an outright bug: `if not list(task_assignments_data):` consumes the cursor, so when the ObjectId form DOES match, the comprehension at :87 iterates the exhausted cursor and the method returns []. (3) The write side keeps feeding the problem, with mixed encodings written by otherwise-identical blocks: ObjectId(user_id) at :118, :129, :188, :201, :314, :325 versus the plain string user_id at :251 and :264 (the finder cited :200 and :263; the correct lines are :201 and :264).
- **proposed:** Add todo/repositories/common/id_filters.py with `def id_or_str(value) -> dict` returning `{"$in": [ObjectId(value), value]}` when ObjectId.is_valid(value) else `{"$in": [value]}`, generalizing Shape C which already works in two places. Every filter then reads `{"task_id": id_or_str(task_id), "is_active": True}` and one query matches either encoding, deleting the second update_one/find_one at all seven Shape-A/B sites. Pair it with `def actor_id(value) -> ObjectId` used wherever updated_by/created_by is written, so new rows stop adding to the mixed encoding. The four-way permutation blocks collapse to a single two-field query. Note the index caveat: on task_details.task_id the guarded form short-circuits on the ObjectId hit while $in over mixed types can widen the scan — measure before shipping the Shape-A conversions, and treat the helper as a bridge to a one-encoding data migration.

## 35. TaskAssignment to Postgres payload dict written six times in one file, three of them identical
- **dimension:** repositories | **severity:** high | **effort:** small | **copies:** 6 | **~lines:** 66
- **sites:** todo/repositories/task_assignment_repository.py:28-38, todo/repositories/task_assignment_repository.py:138-148, todo/repositories/task_assignment_repository.py:210-220, todo/repositories/task_assignment_repository.py:273-283, todo/repositories/task_assignment_repository.py:335-345, todo/repositories/task_assignment_repository.py:471-481
- **drift:** Three of the six are the same code under different names, which is exactly how the fourth drifted unnoticed. update_executor (:273-283) carries `"is_active": current_assignment.is_active` where its three verified twins hardcode False, and sources assignee_id/user_type from the new executor rather than the current row. The batch copy (:471-481) reads `"assignee_id": str(assignment["team_id"])` — assignee taken from the team field. The create copy (:28-38) is the only one rendering `updated_by` as `str(x) if x else None`; the other five unconditionally `str(user_id)`.
- **proposed:** One mapper in a new todo/repositories/common/postgres_payloads.py: `def task_assignment_payload(src: TaskAssignmentModel | Mapping, *, is_active: bool | None = None, assignee_id=None, user_type=None, updated_by=None, updated_at=None) -> dict`. It reads via a small accessor that handles both model attributes and raw-document keys (with .get defaults for documents predating a field), and each call site passes only the fields it genuinely overrides — which makes the update_executor divergence explicit at the call site instead of buried in a re-typed dict. The three identical sites (:138-148, :210-220, :335-345) collapse to one call each.

## 36. MISSED BY FINDER: Task to Postgres payload built three times across two files, and the third copy's key-name drift silently drops updated_by
- **dimension:** repositories | **severity:** high | **effort:** small | **copies:** 3 | **~lines:** 45
- **sites:** todo/repositories/task_repository.py:227-241, todo/repositories/task_repository.py:324-338, todo/repositories/task_assignment_repository.py:494-509
- **drift:** The third copy writes the key `"updated_by"` where the two originals write `"updatedBy"`. The consumer, DualWriteService._transform_task_data, reads only the camelCase form — todo/services/dual_write_service.py:262: `"updated_by": str(data.get("updatedBy", "")) if data.get("updatedBy") else None`. So on the reassign-tasks-from-user-to-team path the Postgres row's updated_by is silently written as None while the other two paths populate it. The same copy also passes `"deferredDetails": None` (:499), a key _transform_task_data never reads, so it is dead payload. Neither divergence is detectable at the call site because the payload is a re-typed literal.
- **proposed:** Add `def task_payload(src: TaskModel | Mapping, **overrides) -> dict` to the same todo/repositories/common/postgres_payloads.py proposed above, with the key names written once. All three sites become one call; the batch site passes `status=TaskStatus.TODO.value, updated_by=performed_by_user_id`, and the misspelled key becomes impossible to reintroduce because the caller no longer names keys. Worth doing together with the TaskAssignment mapper — same module, same shape of fix.

## 37. UserRole scope-to-team_id query builder duplicated verbatim, twice plus a drifted third
- **dimension:** repositories | **severity:** medium | **effort:** small | **copies:** 3 | **~lines:** 30
- **sites:** todo/repositories/user_role_repository.py:95-106, todo/repositories/user_role_repository.py:125-136, todo/repositories/user_role_repository.py:70-84
- **drift:** Three copies that no longer agree on the predicate. get_user_roles (:81-82) applies `query["team_id"] = team_id` for ANY scope when team_id is given, while :102-103 and :132-133 apply it only when `scope == "TEAM"`. More consequentially, get_user_roles normalizes the enum at :78 and :83 (`scope.value if hasattr(scope, "value") else scope`) and the two literal copies omit that coercion entirely — so passing a RoleScope enum instead of a str to get_by_user_role_scope_team or remove_role_by_id compares an enum against a stored string and silently matches nothing.
- **proposed:** A private builder on UserRoleRepository: `@classmethod def _scope_query(cls, *, user_id=None, scope=None, team_id=None, role_object_id=None, active_only=True) -> dict`, doing the enum-to-str coercion once and the TEAM/GLOBAL team_id rule once, used by all three methods. The id guard becomes the shared `to_object_id(value, default=...)` helper so the None-vs-False sentinel difference is one visible argument rather than a duplicated four-line block. Land the enum-coercion fix as its own commit — it is the part with behavior impact, and unifying the team_id rule picks a winner between :81-82 and :102-103.

## 38. Per-model Postgres finder template pasted 11 times with un-adapted field names (seven query fields that do not exist)
- **dimension:** repositories | **severity:** medium | **effort:** small | **copies:** 11 | **~lines:** 33
- **sites:** todo/repositories/postgres_repository.py:234-236, todo/repositories/postgres_repository.py:238-240, todo/repositories/postgres_repository.py:242-244, todo/repositories/postgres_repository.py:253-255, todo/repositories/postgres_repository.py:264-266, todo/repositories/postgres_repository.py:268-270, todo/repositories/postgres_repository.py:279-281, todo/repositories/postgres_repository.py:283-285, todo/repositories/postgres_repository.py:294-296, todo/repositories/postgres_repository.py:298-300, todo/repositories/postgres_repository.py:302-304
- **drift:** CONFIRMED, and the finder undercounted: SEVEN of the eleven (not five) name fields that do not exist and would raise django.core.exceptions.FieldError. Verified against the models: :238-240 and :242-244 filter user_mongo_id/team_mongo_id on PostgresTaskAssignment whose real fields are assignee_id and team_id (todo/models/postgres/task_assignment.py:15,17); :253-255 filters user_mongo_id on PostgresWatchlist whose field is user_id (todo/models/postgres/watchlist.py:16); :264-266 and :268-270 filter user_mongo_id/team_mongo_id on PostgresUserRole whose fields are user_id/team_id (todo/models/postgres/user_role.py:8,11); :294-296 filters user_mongo_id and :298-300 filters collection_name on PostgresAuditLog, which has neither (todo/models/postgres/audit_log.py:5-19). Only :234-236, :279-285 and :302-304 name real fields. Pagination drifted too: the three audit-log copies slice [skip:skip+limit], the other eight ignore paging. Active-row filtering drifted: PostgresTeamRepository.get_by_user (:193-197) applies is_active=True to PostgresUserTeamDetails while PostgresUserTeamDetailsRepository.get_by_user (:279-281) queries the same table without it.
- **proposed:** Add to BasePostgresRepository (todo/repositories/postgres_repository.py, after _apply_filters at :83-104): `def find_by(self, *, filters=None, skip: int = 0, limit: int = 100, order_by: str | None = None, **lookups) -> List[models.Model]` that validates each lookup name with `self.model_class._meta.get_field()`, runs `self.model_class.objects.filter(**lookups)`, applies _apply_filters, and slices [skip:skip+limit]. Every concrete finder becomes `return self.find_by(user_id=user_id, skip=skip, limit=limit)` against the model the repo was constructed with, so the field name can no longer be a hand-typed string that belongs to a different entity. The get_field assertion surfaces all seven broken lookups the first time each method is called (or at import time if run over the registry in a test). Severity is capped at medium only because grep confirms nothing outside postgres_repository.py imports any Postgres*Repository — the payoff is latent. The seven correct field names still have to be decided by hand; the abstraction prevents recurrence, it cannot infer them.

## 39. Per-entity repository contracts declared in abstract_repository.py and re-declared, never inherited, in postgres_repository.py
- **dimension:** repositories | **severity:** medium | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 2 | **~lines:** 150
- **sites:** todo/repositories/abstract_repository.py:50-200, todo/repositories/postgres_repository.py:108-304, todo/repositories/abstract_repository.py:147-153, todo/repositories/postgres_repository.py:247-255, todo/repositories/abstract_repository.py:128-144, todo/repositories/postgres_repository.py:228-244
- **drift:** Grep across the repo for each of the ten names (AbstractUserRepository, AbstractTaskRepository, AbstractTeamRepository, AbstractLabelRepository, AbstractRoleRepository, AbstractTaskAssignmentRepository, AbstractWatchlistRepository, AbstractUserRoleRepository, AbstractUserTeamDetailsRepository, AbstractAuditLogRepository) returns ZERO references outside abstract_repository.py itself — nothing subclasses or imports them, so nothing checks the copies against each other. The signatures have already diverged as a result: the abstract side gives get_by_user three different shapes for three entities ((user_id, filters, skip, limit) for tasks at :68-70, (user_id, skip, limit) for audit logs at :188, bare (user_id) for watchlist/user-role/user-team-details at :151/:160/:174), and the Postgres side faithfully copied that inconsistency (:135-137, :294, :253/:264/:279) — which is the root of the 'three copies paginate, eight ignore skip/limit' drift in the finder finding above.
- **proposed:** Bind or delete; do not leave two unlinked copies. Binding: change each concrete class to inherit its entity ABC — `class PostgresTaskAssignmentRepository(BasePostgresRepository, AbstractTaskAssignmentRepository)` — and drop the redundant `AbstractRepository` from the base list (BasePostgresRepository already extends it, postgres_repository.py:20). Python then enforces the signatures, and the get_by_team stub at postgres_repository.py:146-152 plus every paging-shape mismatch becomes an instantiation-time TypeError instead of latent documentation. Deleting: remove abstract_repository.py:50-200 and keep only the generic AbstractRepository, on the grounds that the entity contracts are documentation no code checks. Either way, first normalize get_by_user to one signature `(id, *, filters=None, skip=0, limit=100)` so the contract stops encoding three paging conventions. Note the Mongo repositories can satisfy neither today: they are classmethod-based (TaskRepository.get_by_id is a @classmethod) and cannot implement an instance-method ABC, so the stated goal at abstract_repository.py:10-12 is unenforced on both sides.

## 40. Mongo insert prologue (stamp timestamps, model_dump, insert_one, assign inserted_id) in nine create methods
- **dimension:** repositories | **severity:** medium | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 9 | **~lines:** 55
- **sites:** todo/repositories/team_repository.py:19-25, todo/repositories/team_repository.py:134-140, todo/repositories/team_repository.py:170-184, todo/repositories/task_assignment_repository.py:19-25, todo/repositories/audit_log_repository.py:12-16, todo/repositories/user_role_repository.py:38-41, todo/repositories/team_creation_invite_code_repository.py:66-71, todo/repositories/watchlist_repository.py:39-42, todo/repositories/task_repository.py:212-223
- **drift:** WatchlistRepository.create (watchlist_repository.py:39-42) is the drifted copy on every axis: `doc = watchlist_model.model_dump(by_alias=True)` — no mode="json", no exclude_none, so it writes explicit nulls and non-JSON types the other eight exclude; it then needs a `doc.pop("_id", None)` no sibling needs; and it assigns `watchlist_model.id = str(insert_result.inserted_id)`, a str into a field the other eight fill with the raw ObjectId (Document.id is typed PyObjectId and pydantic does not validate on assignment here, so this is stored unvalidated). The timestamp step drifted four ways for what should be one rule: TaskAssignmentRepository sets updated_at = None (:21), TeamRepository sets updated_at to now (:21), AuditLogRepository sets only timestamp (:13), and UserRoleRepository/TeamCreationInviteCodeRepository set only created_at (:38, :67), leaving whatever the model default was.
- **proposed:** On MongoRepository: `@classmethod def insert(cls, doc: Document, *, session=None) -> Document` that stamps timestamps according to a class-level `timestamp_fields` declaration, dumps with the single canonical `model_dump(mode="json", by_alias=True, exclude_none=True)`, inserts, assigns the raw inserted_id back onto the model and returns it — plus `insert_many` for the bulk case at team_repository.py:170-184. Each create reduces to `cls.insert(model)` followed by its dual-write epilogue, and the watchlist copy's three deviations disappear. Keep the timestamp policy as a per-class declaration rather than one hardcoded rule, since 'created only' vs 'created and updated' vs 'timestamp' is a genuine per-entity choice; the win is that the choice is declared next to collection_name instead of retyped inside each create. TaskRepository.create (:212-223) fits only partially — it inserts inside a transaction with an atomic counter — so give insert() a `session=` passthrough and leave the counter logic in place.

## 41. find_one to Model-or-None finder duplicated five times across RoleRepository and TeamRepository
- **dimension:** repositories | **severity:** low | **effort:** small | **copies:** 5 | **~lines:** 40
- **sites:** todo/repositories/role_repository.py:49-55, todo/repositories/role_repository.py:57-63, todo/repositories/role_repository.py:65-71, todo/repositories/team_repository.py:52-64, todo/repositories/team_repository.py:66-78
- **drift:** Within each file the copies are still consistent, so the drift here is cross-class and weaker than claimed: RoleRepository routes every document through the `_document_to_model` hook (:42-47) that coerces scope to the RoleScope enum, while TeamRepository calls the model constructor directly; RoleRepository has no try at all, so an invalid id raises bson.InvalidId out of `ObjectId(role_id)` at :52, while TeamRepository wraps the whole body in `except Exception: return None` (:63-64, :77-78), collapsing 'absent' and 'malformed' into the same answer.
- **proposed:** On MongoRepository: `@classmethod def find_one_model(cls, model_cls, filter: dict, *, document_hook=None) -> Optional[Model]` — one find_one, one None-on-absent contract, with RoleRepository's `_document_to_model` passed as the optional hook rather than reinvented per repo. The five methods become one line each. If a second repository ever needs the hook, promote it to a `from_document` classmethod on the model instead of letting document_hook accumulate callers. Do not fold in the three sites I rejected: their exception contracts are part of their public API.

## 42. Django get-or-None try/except duplicated eight times in the Postgres layer, six of them bypassing self.model_class
- **dimension:** repositories | **severity:** low | **effort:** small | **copies:** 8 | **~lines:** 40
- **sites:** todo/repositories/postgres_repository.py:37-42, todo/repositories/postgres_repository.py:114-119, todo/repositories/postgres_repository.py:121-126, todo/repositories/postgres_repository.py:183-188, todo/repositories/postgres_repository.py:206-211, todo/repositories/postgres_repository.py:220-225, todo/repositories/postgres_repository.py:53-63, todo/repositories/postgres_repository.py:65-72
- **drift:** The real, verified drift is that six subclass copies bypass the base class they inherit: PostgresUserRepository.get_by_email (:114-119) hardcodes `PostgresUser.objects` even though `self.model_class` is exactly PostgresUser (set at :112), and the same at :121-126, :183-188, :206-211, :220-225 — so these repositories no longer honour their own constructor, which is the same defect class as the 11 finders above.
- **proposed:** One method on BasePostgresRepository (todo/repositories/postgres_repository.py, next to get_by_id at :36): `def get_one(self, **lookups) -> Optional[models.Model]: try: return self.model_class.objects.get(**lookups) except self.model_class.DoesNotExist: return None`. get_by_id becomes `self.get_one(mongo_id=id)`, get_by_email becomes `self.get_one(email_id=email)`, and update/delete (:53-72) call it and branch on None instead of re-nesting a try. Using self.model_class fixes the hardcoded-model drift for free. Deliberately let MultipleObjectsReturned propagate — centralizing makes it tempting to swallow, which would hide a data-integrity problem. Severity is low only because nothing outside this module imports these classes.

## 43. Filter-and-paginate body copied four times in PostgresTaskRepository, hardcoding PostgresTask instead of self.model_class
- **dimension:** repositories | **severity:** low | **effort:** small | **copies:** 4 | **~lines:** 30
- **sites:** todo/repositories/postgres_repository.py:44-51, todo/repositories/postgres_repository.py:135-144, todo/repositories/postgres_repository.py:154-163, todo/repositories/postgres_repository.py:165-174, todo/repositories/postgres_repository.py:146-152
- **drift:** The three subclass bodies hardcode `PostgresTask.objects` (:139, :158, :169) instead of the self.model_class the constructor was given at :133 — the same defect as the get-or-None and finder copies, and the reason those broken *_mongo_id lookups were never caught. And the fourth sibling was never written: PostgresTaskRepository.get_by_team (:146-152) is a stub — `# This would need to be implemented based on your team-task relationship / # For now, returning empty list / return []` — so three of four sort-alike methods query and one silently returns nothing while presenting an identical signature.
- **proposed:** Fold all four into the `find_by(**lookups, filters, skip, limit)` helper proposed for the finder duplication — they are the same method with a different kwarg. get_by_status becomes `return self.find_by(status=status, filters=filters, skip=skip, limit=limit)`, and the get_by_team stub becomes either a real `find_by` call once the team-task relationship is decided or an explicit `raise NotImplementedError`, so it stops masquerading as a working query. Do this together with the finder/field-name fix; on its own it is churn against a module nothing imports.

## 44. try/except ObjectId conversion guard repeated seven times with three different sentinels
- **dimension:** repositories | **severity:** low | **effort:** small | **copies:** 7 | **~lines:** 28
- **sites:** todo/repositories/task_repository.py:305-308, todo/repositories/user_role_repository.py:95-98, todo/repositories/user_role_repository.py:125-128, todo/repositories/user_team_details_repository.py:12-15, todo/repositories/user_team_details_repository.py:16-19, todo/repositories/user_team_details_repository.py:37-40, todo/repositories/user_team_details_repository.py:41-44
- **drift:** Three different outcomes for the same invalid input, in code that is otherwise identical: return None (task_repository.py:305-308, user_role_repository.py:95-98), return False (user_role_repository.py:125-128 — a block whose enclosing twelve lines diff against :95-106 in exactly one line), and fall through to the raw string (the four blocks in user_team_details_repository.py). Elsewhere the same conversion is unguarded and will raise: task_repository.py:261 in get_by_id, :377 per element in get_by_ids, and team_repository.py:59 guarded only by a blanket except around the whole method. UserRepository uses a third converter, PyObjectId(user_id) (user_repository.py:23, :40), which raises ValueError rather than bson.InvalidId.
- **proposed:** One converter beside the existing PyObjectId — todo/repositories/common/id_filters.py (same module as id_or_str): `_RAISE = object()` and `def to_object_id(value, *, default=_RAISE)` returning the ObjectId, or `default` when the value is not a valid id, catching bson.errors.InvalidId and TypeError specifically rather than bare Exception. A caller writes `obj_id = to_object_id(task_id, default=None)` so the sentinel choice is one visible argument instead of a four-line block, and the None-vs-False difference at user_role_repository.py:95-98/:125-128 becomes reviewable. Add `to_object_ids(values)` for the list cases at task_repository.py:377 and :134. Note that narrowing `except Exception` to InvalidId is a real behavior change: TypeErrors those blocks currently swallow would start escaping.

## 45. Two competing ways to acquire a Mongo collection, plus a hardcoded name that bypasses the model
- **dimension:** repositories | **severity:** low | **effort:** small | **copies:** 2 | **~lines:** 10
- **sites:** todo/repositories/common/mongo_repository.py:16-20, todo/repositories/user_repository.py:15-17, todo/repositories/user_team_details_repository.py:7
- **drift:** Because it does not subclass, UserRepository forgoes the base's caching, its get_client()/get_database() accessors, and the __init_subclass__ guard that requires a declared collection_name — and it calls cls._get_collection() five times (:22, :39, :49, :106, :120), constructing a DatabaseManager on each call. Its collection name is the literal "users" rather than UserModel.collection_name (todo/models/user.py:14), and user_team_details_repository.py:7 drifted the same way with the literal "user_team_details" instead of UserTeamDetailsModel.collection_name (todo/models/team.py:51). Those two hand-typed names belong to exactly the two repositories involved in the duplicate-class finding above.
- **proposed:** Make UserRepository a MongoRepository subclass with `collection_name = UserModel.collection_name`, delete _get_collection, and replace the five call sites with cls.get_collection(); set user_team_details_repository.py:7 to `UserTeamDetailsModel.collection_name`. That leaves one collection-acquisition path in the layer and makes the __init_subclass__ guard actually cover every repository. Check the test fixtures first: MongoRepository caches the handle on the class for the process lifetime, and DatabaseManager.reset() (todo_project/db/config.py:43-47) clears only the singleton, not an already-cached cls.collection — so a subclassed UserRepository could hold a stale handle across tests that swap databases, where today it re-resolves per call.

## 46. find-then-map-then-swallow list read duplicated four times in UserTeamDetailsRepository
- **dimension:** repositories | **severity:** low | **effort:** small | **copies:** 4 | **~lines:** 44
- **sites:** todo/repositories/team_repository.py:210-220, todo/repositories/team_repository.py:261-271, todo/repositories/team_repository.py:222-232, todo/repositories/team_repository.py:249-259
- **drift:** CORRECTION: the finder's headline drift claim is FALSE. It asserts that at :217 and :268 'the try does not actually cover the query execution because pymongo defers it, while the eager list(...) pair at :229/:256 is covered'. Reading the blocks, the mapping comprehension is INSIDE the try at :218 and :269, so the deferred query executes within the try in all four methods; the list(...) difference is cosmetic. The remaining, real drift is narrower: :222-232 and :249-259 issue the same query twice for overlapping data, and all four collapse any failure — including a model-validation failure — into an empty list, which callers in todo/services/team_service.py read as 'no members'.
- **proposed:** On MongoRepository: `@classmethod def find_models(cls, model_cls, filter: dict, *, sort=None, skip=0, limit=None) -> list` that executes eagerly and maps documents through model_cls, letting exceptions propagate; the four methods become one-line filters over it and get_users_by_team_id/get_users_and_added_on_by_team_id become projections over one shared read. Do NOT bake the `except Exception: return []` into the helper — keep it at whichever call sites genuinely want to degrade, so the choice is visible. Note that removing the blanket handler is a behavior change: team_service currently treats [] as 'no members' rather than 'read failed'.

## 47. UserRepository.get_all_users is UserRepository.search_users with an empty filter
- **dimension:** repositories | **severity:** low | **effort:** small | **copies:** 2 | **~lines:** 10
- **sites:** todo/repositories/user_repository.py:100-113, todo/repositories/user_repository.py:115-125
- **drift:** None yet — same sort field, same direction, same return tuple. The exposure is prospective and this layer has a track record: the equivalent sibling pair elsewhere has already split (WatchlistRepository.get_by_user_and_task at :29 has no isActive predicate while get_watchlisted_tasks at :75 does; TaskRepository.delete_by_id at :270 guards isDeleted while get_by_id at :261 does not). A soft-delete or is_active predicate added to one of these two and not the other would reproduce that exactly.
- **proposed:** Collapse to one method on UserRepository: `@classmethod def list_users(cls, *, query: str | None = None, page: int = 1, limit: int = 10) -> tuple[list[UserModel], int]` that builds `search_filter = {"$or": [...]} if query else {}` and runs the single body — so the filter, the sort and the tuple shape are defined once. Keep search_users/get_all_users as two-line delegations if the service-layer call sites and their tests are not worth touching in the same change. Worth folding in while doing the MongoRepository read helper; not worth a standalone commit.

## 48. Each entity's field list is restated a third time in DualWriteService._transform_*_data, and three of the eleven copies no longer match their Django model
- **dimension:** models | **severity:** high | **effort:** large | **copies:** 11 | **~lines:** 130
- **sites:** todo/models/postgres/role.py:13-20, todo/models/role.py:14-21, todo/services/dual_write_service.py:301-309, todo/models/postgres/label.py:13-20, todo/models/label.py:10-16, todo/services/dual_write_service.py:291-299, todo/models/postgres/task_assignment.py:13-28, todo/models/task_assignment.py:17-27, todo/services/dual_write_service.py:311-323, todo/services/dual_write_service.py:377-390, todo/models/postgres/task.py:14-35, todo/services/dual_write_service.py:249-264, todo/models/postgres/team.py:14-29, todo/services/dual_write_service.py:268-278, todo/models/postgres/team.py:71-82, todo/services/dual_write_service.py:400-408, todo/models/postgres/user.py:14-22, todo/services/dual_write_service.py:282-289, todo/models/postgres/watchlist.py:14-23, todo/services/dual_write_service.py:327-335, todo/models/postgres/user_role.py:8-14, todo/services/dual_write_service.py:411-419, todo/models/postgres/audit_log.py:8-19, todo/services/dual_write_service.py:422-437, todo/models/postgres/team_creation_invite_code.py:15-25, todo/services/dual_write_service.py:441-449
- **drift:** I verified the finder's four drift claims individually. Two hold, one is hypothetical, one is false.

CONFIRMED (2): PostgresRole has `permissions = models.JSONField(default=dict)` (todo/models/postgres/role.py:16) but RoleModel has no `permissions` field at all, so `"permissions": data.get("permissions", {})` (dual_write_service.py:306) writes `{}` on every single row. Conversely RoleModel's `scope`, `is_active`, `created_by`, `updated_by` (todo/models/role.py:16-21) have no Postgres columns and are silently dropped by the transform.

CONFIRMED (4), with a correction: `_sync_task_assignment_update` (dual_write_service.py:377-390) passes `status=`, `user_mongo_id=`, `team_mongo_id=`, `assigned_at=`, `completed_at=`, `assigned_by=` to `PostgresTaskAssignment.objects.create()`. I checked `todo/models/postgres/task_assignment.py:13-28` — none of those six columns exist; the real columns are task_mongo_id, assignee_id, user_type, team_id, is_active. This is a stale fourth copy of the mapping that would raise TypeError on every call. The finder did not notice it is DEAD CODE: `grep -rn '_sync_task_assignment_update' todo/` returns only the definition at line 360. It is still valid drift evidence (a copy that rotted unnoticed) but not a live bug.

PARTLY CONFIRMED (3): PostgresLabel has `description` (postgres/label.py:16) which LabelModel lacks, and LabelModel's `isDeleted`/`createdBy`/`updatedBy` (label.py:12,15,16) have no Postgres columns — verified. But the claimed consequence is not live: `grep -rn 'delete_document' todo/` shows exactly one non-service caller, `todo/repositories/user_team_details_repository.py:57`. Labels never reach `delete_document`, so the hard-delete at dual_write_service.py:171 is latent, not occurring.

REJECTED (1): the finder claims `executor_id` reassignments are lost from Postgres. A full grep for `executor_id` (excluding previous_/new_executor_id) shows it is declared at todo/models/task_assignment.py:26 and read at task_assignment_service.py:114,149 and task_service.py:238 — but NEVER written by any repository. `TaskAssignmentRepository.update_executor` (task_assignment_repository.py:237-275) writes `assignee_id`, not `executor_id`, to Mongo, and that field IS synced (line 275). So nothing is lost from Postgres that exists in Mongo; the field is unwritten in both stores.
- **proposed:** Reject the finder's codegen proposal (an EntitySchema generating both the pydantic Document and the Django model). PyObjectId vs CharField(24), DeferredDetailsModel vs a OneToOne table, and List[PyObjectId] vs the PostgresTaskLabel junction cannot come from one declaration without per-entity branching, and a model factory fights Django's migration autodetector — that is rule-3 'the abstraction makes it worse'.

Extract only the Django-model <-> transform layer. New module `todo/models/postgres/mapping.py` holding one declarative `EntityMap` per entity, keyed by collection name, replacing all eleven `_transform_*_data` methods and the eleven-branch if/elif at dual_write_service.py:209-233. `EntityMap.model` also replaces `COLLECTION_MODEL_MAP` (dual_write_service.py:31-43), which is the same fix candidate 10 needs, so do both in one change.

Call-site change: `DualWriteService._transform_data_for_postgres` drops from 47 lines (209-235 plus the 11 methods) to five lines; `_get_postgres_model` reads `REGISTRY[name].model`. The payoff is the accompanying parametrized test, which fails today on `_sync_task_assignment_update`'s six phantom columns and flags `permissions` as mapped-from-nothing.

## 49. The mongo_id + sync-metadata block and its two indexes are byte-identical in all 11 Postgres models
- **dimension:** models | **severity:** medium | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 11 | **~lines:** 145
- **sites:** todo/models/postgres/audit_log.py:6, todo/models/postgres/audit_log.py:21-31, todo/models/postgres/audit_log.py:36,42, todo/models/postgres/label.py:11, todo/models/postgres/label.py:23-33, todo/models/postgres/label.py:38,40, todo/models/postgres/role.py:11, todo/models/postgres/role.py:23-33, todo/models/postgres/role.py:38,40, todo/models/postgres/task.py:12, todo/models/postgres/task.py:38-48, todo/models/postgres/task.py:53,59, todo/models/postgres/task_assignment.py:11, todo/models/postgres/task_assignment.py:31-41, todo/models/postgres/task_assignment.py:46,52, todo/models/postgres/team.py:12, todo/models/postgres/team.py:32-42, todo/models/postgres/team.py:47,50, todo/models/postgres/team.py:69, todo/models/postgres/team.py:85-95, todo/models/postgres/team.py:101,105, todo/models/postgres/team_creation_invite_code.py:12, todo/models/postgres/team_creation_invite_code.py:28-38, todo/models/postgres/team_creation_invite_code.py:43,47, todo/models/postgres/user.py:12, todo/models/postgres/user.py:25-35, todo/models/postgres/user.py:40,43, todo/models/postgres/user_role.py:6, todo/models/postgres/user_role.py:16-26, todo/models/postgres/user_role.py:38,44, todo/models/postgres/watchlist.py:12, todo/models/postgres/watchlist.py:26-36, todo/models/postgres/watchlist.py:41,45
- **drift:** The block itself has not drifted; the drift is in its vocabulary escaping into literals. `grep -rn '"SYNCED"|"PENDING"|"FAILED"' todo/` confirms the finder exactly: "SYNCED" is typed as a bare string at todo/services/dual_write_service.py:124, :166, :204 and todo/services/postgres_sync_service.py:163, :214, while "PENDING" and "FAILED" appear ONLY inside the 11 model choice lists and are never assigned anywhere in the codebase. `DualWriteService._record_sync_failure` (dual_write_service.py:470-478) appends to an in-memory list and never touches `sync_status` or `sync_error`, so two of the three declared states and the `sync_status` index are dead in all 11 copies. `models.Index(fields=["mongo_id"])` is also redundant in all 11 — `unique=True` on that column already creates the index.
- **proposed:** New module `todo/models/postgres/common/base.py` with an abstract `SyncedModel` carrying mongo_id and the three sync columns, and `todo/constants/sync.py` with a `SyncStatus` TextChoices that the two services import instead of typing "SYNCED". Index names must be templated with `%(class)s_` so the abstract base does not collide across the 11 concrete tables, and each child's `Meta` must subclass `SyncedModel.Meta` for `indexes` to be inherited. Drop the redundant `mongo_id` index while doing it.

Call-site change: `class PostgresUser(models.Model):` becomes `class PostgresUser(SyncedModel):`, the 12 lines are deleted, and the child `Meta` keeps only its own entity indexes. Verify the generated migration contains nothing but index renames before applying.

## 50. The same 5-line save() override is pasted into 9 Postgres models, truncated in a 10th, absent from 2, and it discards the created_at the sync just read from Mongo
- **dimension:** models | **severity:** high | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 9 | **~lines:** 44
- **sites:** todo/models/postgres/label.py:46-50, todo/models/postgres/role.py:46-50, todo/models/postgres/task.py:65-69, todo/models/postgres/task_assignment.py:58-62, todo/models/postgres/team.py:56-60, todo/models/postgres/team.py:111-115, todo/models/postgres/user.py:49-53, todo/models/postgres/watchlist.py:55-59, todo/models/postgres/team_creation_invite_code.py:53-56, todo/services/dual_write_service.py:118, todo/services/postgres_sync_service.py:161-168
- **drift:** Three drifts, all verified.

(1) PostgresAuditLog and PostgresUserRole have no save() override at all (confirmed: the grep returns 9 hits across 11 models, and neither file appears). Both have a `created_at` column (audit_log.py:14 uses `timestamp`, user_role.py:13 uses `created_at`), so those two preserve the Mongo timestamp handed to them while the other nine overwrite it. Same concept, opposite behavior, decided per file with no comment either way.

(2) The override defeats the backfill. `PostgresSyncService._sync_labels_table` reads `postgres_data = self.dual_write_service._transform_label_data(label)` (postgres_sync_service.py:161) — whose `created_at` is `data.get("createdAt")` straight from Mongo (dual_write_service.py:297) — then calls `PostgresLabel.objects.create(**postgres_data)` (postgres_sync_service.py:168). `objects.create()` routes through `save()` with pk still None, so label.py:47-48 throws the historical date away and stamps `timezone.now()`. Every backfilled label gets today's date; backfilled audit logs and user roles keep theirs.

(3) `DualWriteService.update_document` carries `preserve_fields = {"created_at", "mongo_id"}` (dual_write_service.py:118) — a workaround for this exact class of behavior, written once in the service to compensate for nine copies in the models. Note it only guards the attribute-copy loop; `postgres_instance.save()` at line 126 still stamps `updated_at`, so the workaround is itself incomplete.
- **proposed:** Delete all nine overrides and put the behavior once on a `TimestampedModel` abstract base (stack it with `SyncedModel` from the previous finding), respecting a caller-supplied value instead of clobbering it. Keep `updated_at` assignable rather than switching to `auto_now=True`, because `dual_write_service.py:373` sets `updated_at=timezone.now()` explicitly and `auto_now` would make that a silent no-op.

Call-site change: `class PostgresLabel(SyncedModel, TimestampedModel):` and lines 19-20 and 46-50 both disappear. Behavior change to flag in review: the nine models that currently stamp `now()` unconditionally would start honoring a Mongo-supplied `created_at`, which is the intended fix but does change what backfilled rows contain.

## 51. Soft-delete flag plus uniqueness constraint: the sync-failure bug was fixed in PostgresUserRole and left standing in PostgresUserTeamDetails
- **dimension:** models | **severity:** medium | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 3 | **~lines:** 9
- **sites:** todo/models/postgres/user_role.py:12, todo/models/postgres/user_role.py:30-36, todo/models/postgres/team.py:78, todo/models/postgres/team.py:99, todo/models/postgres/watchlist.py:17, todo/models/postgres/watchlist.py:50, todo/models/postgres/task_assignment.py:20, todo/services/team_service.py:281-299
- **drift:** The git history claim checks out exactly, and I found the live exposure the finder cited the wrong lines for.

`git log -1 --format=%h -- <each postgres model>` shows ten of eleven files last touched by 781100f (2025-08-29) and user_role.py alone by e58e9ec (2025-09-19). `git show e58e9ec -- todo/models/postgres/user_role.py` is a one-hunk diff replacing `unique_together = ["user_id", "role_name", "scope", "team_id"]` with the conditional UniqueConstraint, and the commit body contains verbatim: "include is_active in unique constraint to prevent sync failures" and "fix bug where in postgres_user_role where data sync failed if a user was removed and added more than once".

CORRECTION to the finder: they cited todo/repositories/team_repository.py:328-330 as the re-add path calling `PostgresUserTeamDetails.objects.create()`. Those lines actually contain `collection = cls.get_collection()` / a comment / `find_one(...)`, and `add_user_to_team` (team_repository.py:321-359) REACTIVATES an inactive row in place with `update_one` — it never re-inserts, so that path does not trip the constraint (it has its own separate defect: the reactivation branch performs no dual-write at all, so Postgres keeps is_active=False).

The real exposure is one layer up, in `TeamService.join_team_by_invite_code` (todo/services/team_service.py:281-299), which I verified: the membership check is `if str(user_team.team_id) == str(team.id) and user_team.is_active:` — INACTIVE memberships fall straight through to `UserTeamDetailsRepository.create(user_team)` at line 299. That inserts a fresh Mongo document and calls `dual_write_service.create_document(collection_name="user_team_details", ...)` (team_repository.py:153-155), which reaches `postgres_model.objects.create(**postgres_data)` (dual_write_service.py:76) with a (user_id, team_id) pair that already has a row — violating `unique_together` at postgres/team.py:99. That is precisely the remove-then-re-join scenario #255 fixed one file over. PostgresWatchlist is defensive only: `WatchlistRepository.update` (watchlist_repository.py:337-341) reactivates in place.
- **proposed:** Put the flag and its constraint in one place so they cannot be declared apart. `todo/models/postgres/common/base.py` gets a `SoftDeletableModel` abstract base holding `is_active`, plus an `active_unique(*fields, name=...)` helper that pins the invariant half (`condition=Q(is_active=True)`) while taking only the genuinely varying halves. The helper is worth it precisely because the pinned half is the part that got forgotten twice.

Call-site change: `class PostgresUserTeamDetails(SyncedModel, SoftDeletableModel):`, line 78 deleted, and line 99 becomes `constraints = [active_unique("user_id", "team_id", name="uniq_active_user_team")]`. This needs a data migration, not just a schema one: Django must drop the unique_together index and add a partial one, and any duplicate inactive rows that accumulated must be reconciled first. Fix PostgresUserTeamDetails (live bug) and PostgresWatchlist (defensive) in the same change.

## 52. The created/updated/by/soft-delete quartet is re-declared in 10 pydantic documents under four mutually incompatible naming conventions
- **dimension:** models | **severity:** medium | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 10 | **~lines:** 45
- **sites:** todo/models/label.py:12-16, todo/models/task.py:34, todo/models/task.py:38-41, todo/models/watchlist.py:12-16, todo/models/team.py:31-35, todo/models/team.py:55-59, todo/models/task_assignment.py:21-25, todo/models/role.py:17-21, todo/models/user_role.py:20-22, todo/models/user.py:20-21, todo/models/team_creation_invite_code.py:19-20, todo/models/audit_log.py:17
- **drift:** Four conventions for one concept, verified by reading all ten files: (a) camelCase with REQUIRED createdAt and no default -- LabelModel, TaskModel, WatchlistModel; (b) snake_case with default_factory on BOTH created_at and updated_at, so updated_at is non-null from birth -- TeamModel, UserTeamDetailsModel; (c) snake_case with default_factory on created_at only and `updated_at: datetime | None = None` -- TaskAssignmentModel, UserModel, UserRoleModel; (d) snake_case with a REQUIRED created_at and nullable updated_at -- RoleModel. The soft-delete flag forks the same way across four spellings: `isDeleted` (label.py:12, task.py:34), `is_deleted` (team.py:35), `isActive` (watchlist.py:12), `is_active` (team.py:55, task_assignment.py:21, role.py:17, user_role.py:20).

The downstream cost is verified and measurable: DualWriteService needs two different bodies for the same three fields -- `data.get("createdAt")` at dual_write_service.py:259 and :297 versus `data.get("created_at")` at :276 and :287 -- and PostgresSyncService carries a comment at postgres_sync_service.py:99-104 explaining that labels alone use `isDeleted` for soft deletes while everything else has none. That comment is documentation of the drift.

Note the same fork was copied onto the Django side: PostgresTeam and PostgresUserTeamDetails use `updated_at = models.DateTimeField(default=timezone.now)` (team.py:29, :82) while the other six use `null=True, blank=True` -- exactly mirroring convention (b) vs (c).
- **proposed:** Two mixins beside `Document` in `todo/models/common/audited.py`: `AuditedDocument` (created_at/updated_at/created_by/updated_by under one agreed nullability rule) and `SoftDeletableDocument` (is_deleted). Ship it in two stages so the risky half is separable.

Stage 1, low risk: replace the nine identical lambdas with one `utcnow()` helper in `todo/models/common/audited.py` and apply `AuditedDocument` to the seven snake_case documents only (TeamModel, UserTeamDetailsModel, TaskAssignmentModel, UserModel, UserRoleModel, RoleModel, TeamCreationInviteCodeModel). No wire-format change, no alias needed. Pick convention (c) -- defaulted created_at, nullable updated_at -- since it is the majority and matches the Postgres columns.

Stage 2, risky, do separately: bring the three camelCase documents in with explicit `alias=` per field so the MongoDB keys stay `createdAt`/`isDeleted`. This must be verified against `model_dump(by_alias=True)` at watchlist_repository.py:39 and task_assignment_repository.py:23, and it changes TaskModel/LabelModel `createdAt` from required to defaulted, which todo/tests/unit/models/test_task.py:20-30 asserts on (`required_fields = ["title", "createdAt", "createdBy"]`).

## 53. The same three-branch ObjectId validator is hand-written 10 times with four different error strings, and two same-named copies in one file disagree on null handling and return type
- **dimension:** models | **severity:** high | **effort:** small | **copies:** 10 | **~lines:** 48
- **sites:** todo/models/common/pyobjectid.py:9-15, todo/models/team.py:11-17, todo/models/team.py:37-43, todo/models/team.py:61-63, todo/models/team_creation_invite_code.py:25-31, todo/models/task_assignment.py:29-35, todo/dto/task_assignment_dto.py:13-18, todo/dto/task_assignment_dto.py:20-25, todo/dto/task_assignment_dto.py:34-39, todo/dto/task_dto.py:59-63, todo/constants/messages.py:64
- **drift:** The name collision is real and I confirmed the resolution. todo/models/team.py:10-17 defines `ObjectIdValidatorMixin.validate_object_id`, which RAISES on None ("Object ID cannot be None"), calls `PyObjectId.is_valid`, and returns `v` UNCONVERTED. Twenty lines below, `TeamModel(Document, ObjectIdValidatorMixin)` defines a method of the exact same name at :37-43 that does the opposite on all three counts. `UserTeamDetailsModel`, in the same file, does not define it and its delegating validator at :61-63 is literally `return cls.validate_object_id(v)` — so it resolves through the MRO to the mixin's version. Two sibling models in one 63-line file, one same-named validator, two null policies and two return types.

Error strings for one condition have forked four ways, all verified: `f"Invalid ObjectId: {v}"` (team.py:42, team_creation_invite_code.py:30, task_assignment.py:34, pyobjectid.py:15); `f"Invalid Object ID format: {v}"` (team.py:16); three bespoke strings in the DTO — `f"Invalid task ID: {value}"` (task_assignment_dto.py:17), `f"Invalid assignee ID: {value}"` (:24), `f"Invalid original team ID: {value}"` (:38); and the constant `INVALID_OBJECT_ID = "{0} is not a valid ObjectId."` (constants/messages.py:64), which exists for exactly this and is used by only ONE of the ten sites (task_dto.py:62).

One nuance the finder got right to flag: with pydantic 2.10.1 and V1-style `@validator` (default pre=False), `PyObjectId.validate` runs FIRST as the type validator, so by the time these validators run the value is already an ObjectId or None. The `ObjectId.is_valid` branch in all three verbatim copies is therefore unreachable — they are pure dead re-coercion. The mixin's raise-on-None is the one exception that adds real behavior.
- **proposed:** Delete the per-model validators rather than extracting a shared one; the shared implementation already exists as `PyObjectId.validate`, and the three verbatim copies are re-running the annotated type's own job. Concretely, in `todo/models/common/pyobjectid.py`, make `validate` raise `ValueError(ValidationErrors.INVALID_OBJECT_ID.format(value))` so the message lives in constants/ like every other validation message, then delete `ObjectIdValidatorMixin` (team.py:10-17) and the four `@validator` blocks at team.py:37-43, team.py:61-63, team_creation_invite_code.py:25-31 and task_assignment.py:29-35.

For the DTOs, which need `str` back rather than `ObjectId`, add one `StrObjectId` annotated type next to `PyObjectId` and annotate the fields with it, replacing the three hand-written near-copies at task_assignment_dto.py:13-18, :20-25 and :34-39.

One field needs care before deletion: the mixin's raise-on-None currently enforces required-ness for UserTeamDetailsModel's user_id/team_id/created_by/updated_by, which plain `PyObjectId` does not (PyObjectId.validate returns None for None). Give those four fields an explicit non-optional check, or keep them annotated so pydantic rejects None, before removing the mixin.

## 54. An inherited field is re-declared character-for-character in four documents, and one of three copied model_config lines silently dropped use_enum_values
- **dimension:** models | **severity:** low | **effort:** small | **copies:** 4 | **~lines:** 7
- **sites:** todo/models/common/document.py:10, todo/models/role.py:13, todo/models/user_role.py:15, todo/models/task.py:26, todo/models/task_assignment.py:17, todo/models/role.py:23, todo/models/user_role.py:24, todo/models/task.py:43, todo/models/common/document.py:17-20, todo/services/dual_write_service.py:240-247
- **drift:** TaskModel's config kept `ser_enum="value"` but dropped `use_enum_values=True`, so TaskModel holds live enum objects while RoleModel and UserRoleModel hold raw values. The test asserts the TaskModel side directly — todo/tests/unit/models/test_task.py:16-17: `self.assertEqual(task.priority, TaskPriority.HIGH)  # Enum value`. That inconsistency is exactly what forces the defensive code at todo/services/dual_write_service.py:240-247:
```
        priority = data.get("priority", 3)
        if hasattr(priority, "value"):  # If it's an enum, get its value
            priority = priority.value
        status = data.get("status", "TODO")
        if hasattr(status, "value"):  # If it's an enum, get its value
            status = status.value
```
That is code written to survive not knowing which of two conventions a given model uses.

Separately, `ser_enum` is not a pydantic ConfigDict key. requirements.txt pins pydantic==2.10.1, where ConfigDict is a total=False TypedDict and unknown keys are stored but never read — no error, no warning, no effect. It was copied into three files and has never done anything in any of them. (I could not execute this check; pydantic is not installed in this container, so this rests on the pinned version's documented key set rather than a run.)
- **proposed:** Two changes of very different risk; ship the safe one alone.

Safe and immediate: delete the four redundant `id` re-declarations (role.py:13, user_role.py:15, task.py:26, task_assignment.py:17). They restate `Document`'s line exactly, so removal is a no-op today and stops four copies from surviving any future change to the base's alias. Also delete the bogus `ser_enum="value"` key from all three configs.

Behavioral, do separately with the enum handling in the same commit: convert `Document`'s deprecated `class Config` (document.py:17-20) to `model_config = ConfigDict(...)` so every document inherits one policy, and delete the two now-identical subclass copies at role.py:23 and user_role.py:24. Standardize on NOT setting `use_enum_values` (matching TaskModel and the test at test_task.py:16-17) rather than setting it everywhere, then simplify dual_write_service.py:240-247 to unconditional `.value` access. Setting it uniformly would break that test and the `TaskPriority` json_encoder at todo/dto/task_dto.py:63-64.

## 55. The ("user","team") vocabulary is declared five times and validated three times redundantly, with the same error string copied verbatim into two near-identical dead validators
- **dimension:** models | **severity:** medium | **effort:** small | **copies:** 5 | **~lines:** 18
- **sites:** todo/models/task_assignment.py:20, todo/models/task_assignment.py:37-40, todo/dto/task_assignment_dto.py:10, todo/dto/task_assignment_dto.py:27-32, todo/dto/task_assignment_dto.py:47, todo/dto/task_assignment_dto.py:61, todo/serializers/create_task_serializer.py:72-73, todo/models/postgres/task_assignment.py:16, todo/models/postgres/task.py:20-21, todo/constants/task.py:4-15, todo/models/postgres/user_role.py:9-10, todo/constants/role.py:21-24, todo/models/audit_log.py:16, todo/models/postgres/audit_log.py:13
- **drift:** Both validators are not merely duplicated, they are DEAD. The field is already annotated `Literal["user", "team"]` in both classes (models/task_assignment.py:20, dto/task_assignment_dto.py:10), and with pydantic V1-style `@validator` at default pre=False the Literal is enforced before the validator body runs — so neither `if v not in ["user", "team"]` branch is reachable. Two copies of unreachable code, kept in sync by hand across two layers.

The secondary half is weaker but real: `priority = models.IntegerField(default=3)  # 1=HIGH, 2=MEDIUM, 3=LOW` and `status = models.CharField(max_length=20, default="TODO")` (postgres/task.py:20-21) restate TaskPriority/TaskStatus (constants/task.py:4-15) as a comment and two magic defaults with no `choices`, so `postgres_tasks.status` can hold any string. `role_name`/`scope` (postgres/user_role.py:9-10) likewise carry no choices even though `ROLE_SCOPE_CHOICES` sits ready at constants/role.py:21-24 — and I confirmed by grep that constant has exactly one consumer in the entire codebase, todo/serializers/get_roles_serializer.py:8. The AuditLog `action` vocabulary is worst off: its eleven legal values exist only inside a trailing comment at todo/models/audit_log.py:16 and as `models.CharField(max_length=100)` at postgres/audit_log.py:13.
- **proposed:** Add `class UserType(str, Enum)` to a new `todo/constants/assignment.py` and have every layer reference it instead of retyping the pair. Delete both `validate_user_type` validators outright — the annotation already enforces the set, so there is nothing to move — and replace the serializer's hand-rolled check with the constant.

Call-site change: models/task_assignment.py loses lines 37-40 and its field becomes `user_type: UserType`; dto/task_assignment_dto.py loses lines 27-32 and its three Literals become `UserType`; postgres/task_assignment.py:16 becomes `choices=UserType.choices()`. Note the interaction with the previous finding: turning `user_type` into an Enum changes what `model_dump(mode="json")` writes into Mongo (task_assignment_repository.py:23) unless the enum subclasses `str`, which the sketch does deliberately.

Treat the choices-on-Django-columns half as a separate, lower-priority change: adding `choices` to an existing column is migration-visible and will fail loudly on any legacy row holding an out-of-vocabulary value — likely for `postgres_audit_logs.action`, whose vocabulary was never enforced anywhere. Audit the existing data first.

## 56. Each entity is registered in three parallel lists, and the collection name disagrees with the sync routing key for two of the eleven
- **dimension:** models | **severity:** medium | **effort:** small | **copies:** 3 | **~lines:** 46
- **sites:** todo/models/watchlist.py:8, todo/models/task_assignment.py:15, todo/services/dual_write_service.py:31-43, todo/services/dual_write_service.py:209-233, todo/repositories/watchlist_repository.py:56, todo/repositories/watchlist_repository.py:361, todo/repositories/task_assignment_repository.py:41, todo/repositories/task_assignment_repository.py:151, todo/repositories/task_assignment_repository.py:223, todo/repositories/task_assignment_repository.py:286, todo/repositories/task_assignment_repository.py:348, todo/repositories/task_assignment_repository.py:468, todo/repositories/team_repository.py:154, todo/repositories/user_role_repository.py:55
- **drift:** Two of the eleven names have already forked into different values for the same entity, verified by reading both sides. `WatchlistModel.collection_name = "watchlist"` (singular, todo/models/watchlist.py:8) but the routing key is `"watchlists"` (plural, dual_write_service.py:38), typed as a literal at watchlist_repository.py:56 and :361. `TaskAssignmentModel.collection_name = "task_details"` (task_assignment.py:15) but the routing key is `"task_assignments"` (dual_write_service.py:37), typed as a literal at task_assignment_repository.py:41, :151, :223, :286, :348 and inside a batch-operation dict at :468.

So for two entities the string naming the Mongo collection and the string naming the sync route are different values, and every caller has to know which of the two to type at which call. Nothing validates that a `COLLECTION_MODEL_MAP` key corresponds to any real collection: a typo falls through the if/elif chain at line 231 into `_transform_generic_data`, or returns None from `_get_postgres_model` and merely logs at dual_write_service.py:65.
- **proposed:** Same registry as the first finding — build these once and derive all three uses. `todo/models/postgres/mapping.py` holds one `EntityMap` per entity carrying `collection` (the routing key), `model` (the Django class) and `document` (the pydantic class); `COLLECTION_MODEL_MAP` becomes a comprehension over it and the eleven-branch if/elif disappears entirely.

Call-site change: repositories stop typing string literals and pass their own ClassVar — `collection_name=cls.collection_name` — which makes a name that does not exist a fast failure instead of a silent fall-through. Sequencing matters, and the finder's risk note is correct: the map keys "watchlists"/"task_assignments" are load-bearing today, so switching the six literal sites to the ClassVar values MUST land in the same commit as changing the map keys (or as renaming the two ClassVars), or sync breaks for those two entities. I checked that these strings are routing keys only — they are never persisted; `_record_sync_failure` (dual_write_service.py:470-478) keeps them in an in-memory list that is never written anywhere — so whichever spelling wins, no stored data needs migrating.

## 57. ObjectId-format guard re-typed in 23 places across serializers/, dto/, models/, utils/ and views/, with 7 different error strings
- **dimension:** dto_serializers | **severity:** high | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 23 | **~lines:** 100
- **sites:** todo/serializers/create_task_assignment_serializer.py:13-16, todo/serializers/create_task_assignment_serializer.py:18-21, todo/serializers/remove_from_team_serializer.py:10-13, todo/serializers/remove_from_team_serializer.py:15-18, todo/serializers/update_team_serializer.py:15-18, todo/serializers/create_team_serializer.py:18-23, todo/serializers/create_team_serializer.py:25-29, todo/serializers/create_task_serializer.py:56-60, todo/serializers/create_task_serializer.py:67-71, todo/serializers/create_task_serializer.py:74-76, todo/serializers/update_task_serializer.py:41-54, todo/serializers/update_task_serializer.py:77-78, todo/serializers/create_watchlist_serializer.py:10-15, todo/dto/task_assignment_dto.py:13-18, todo/dto/task_assignment_dto.py:20-25, todo/dto/task_assignment_dto.py:34-39, todo/dto/task_dto.py:59-63, todo/utils/task_validation_utils.py:22-38, todo/views/watchlist.py:173-174, todo/models/task_assignment.py:29-35, todo/models/team.py:10-17, todo/models/team.py:37-43, todo/models/team_creation_invite_code.py:25-31
- **drift:** Seven distinct user-facing strings now answer the same question. (1) ValidationErrors.INVALID_OBJECT_ID = "{0} is not a valid ObjectId." (todo/constants/messages.py:64) at the 4 team/assignment serializers and todo/dto/task_dto.py:62. (2)(3)(4) hand-rolled f-strings at todo/dto/task_assignment_dto.py:17, :24, :38 -- "Invalid task ID:", "Invalid assignee ID:", "Invalid original team ID:". (5) ValidationErrors.INVALID_TASK_ID_FORMAT = "Please enter a valid Task ID format." (create_watchlist_serializer.py:14). (6) ApiErrors.INVALID_TASK_ID = "Invalid task ID format" (task_validation_utils.py:29). (7) bare literal "Invalid task_id" at todo/views/watchlist.py:174. So POST /watchlist, GET /watchlist?task_id=, and POST /task-assignments return three different 400 bodies for the identical malformed task id.

More drift inside the idiom itself: todo/serializers/create_team_serializer.py:19-20 has a blank/None short-circuit (`if not value or not value.strip(): return None`) that the otherwise-identical todo/serializers/update_team_serializer.py:15-18 lacks, so a blank poc_id is accepted on create and 400s on update. And todo/models/team.py:20 declares `class TeamModel(Document, ObjectIdValidatorMixin)` but then redefines `validate_object_id` at :37-43, silently shadowing the mixin's copy at :10-17 -- which has the opposite None policy (mixin raises "Object ID cannot be None", TeamModel returns None) and a different message ("Invalid Object ID format: {v}").

CORRECTION to the finder: its strictness sub-claim is wrong. `ObjectId.is_valid(None)` returns False and `ObjectId(str(None))` raises InvalidId -- both reject, so the two mechanisms do not actually disagree on None. The message drift is the real defect; the mechanism drift is cosmetic.
- **proposed:** Two shared primitives, one per validation stack.

NEW FILE todo/serializers/fields.py:
  class ObjectIdField(serializers.CharField) -- overrides `to_internal_value(self, data)`; runs the single `ObjectId.is_valid` check and raises `ValidationErrors.INVALID_OBJECT_ID.format(value)`.
  def object_id_list_field(**kwargs) -> serializers.ListField -- returns `ListField(child=ObjectIdField(), **kwargs)` for `labels` and `member_ids`.

NEW FILE todo/dto/validators.py:
  `ObjectIdStr = Annotated[str, AfterValidator(_require_object_id)]` and `OptionalObjectIdStr = Annotated[Optional[str], AfterValidator(_require_object_id_or_none)]`, both raising the same `ValidationErrors.INVALID_OBJECT_ID` text.

Call-site change: every `def validate_<x>_id` / `def validate_labels` / `def validate_member_ids` listed above is deleted and the field declaration changes type. todo/serializers/remove_from_team_serializer.py drops from 18 lines to 3. todo/dto/task_assignment_dto.py loses three of its four validators (lines 13-25 and 34-39). todo/serializers/create_watchlist_serializer.py:10-15 and todo/utils/task_validation_utils.py:22-38 adopt `ObjectIdField` / the shared check, which is where the response-body change happens -- do that one behind a version note since INVALID_TASK_ID_FORMAT is a different string.

The models/ copies (todo/models/task_assignment.py:29-35, team.py:37-43, team_creation_invite_code.py:25-31) should instead just delete their validators: those fields are already typed `PyObjectId`, and todo/models/common/pyobjectid.py:13-14 already performs the same check.

## 58. `user_type in ["user","team"]` re-checked 5 times -- 4 of them unreachable dead code, and one copy has the words swapped
- **dimension:** dto_serializers | **severity:** medium | **effort:** small | **copies:** 5 | **~lines:** 18
- **sites:** todo/dto/task_assignment_dto.py:27-32, todo/serializers/create_task_assignment_serializer.py:23-26, todo/serializers/create_task_serializer.py:72-73, todo/serializers/update_task_serializer.py:74-75, todo/models/task_assignment.py:37-41
- **drift:** todo/serializers/update_task_serializer.py:74-75 reads `if not user_type or user_type not in ["team", "user"]: raise serializers.ValidationError("user_type must be either 'team' or 'user'")` -- both the list and the message have the two words in the opposite order from the other four copies. That is the signature of a retyped rather than copied block.

CORRECTION to the finder: FOUR of the five copies are unreachable, not three. It missed todo/serializers/create_task_serializer.py:72-73, which sits inside `validate()` reading a value that already passed the `ChoiceField(choices=["user", "team"])` declared at lines 30-32, so it can never fire either. The other dead copies: todo/dto/task_assignment_dto.py:27-32 (behind `user_type: Literal["user", "team"]` at line 10), todo/serializers/create_task_assignment_serializer.py:23-26 (behind the ChoiceField at lines 9-11), todo/models/task_assignment.py:37-41 (behind the `Literal` at line 20). Only update_task_serializer's copy is load-bearing, because there `user_type` lives inside an opaque `DictField` (line 23) that DRF does not introspect. So the one copy that actually runs is also the one whose message drifted -- exactly the wrong copy to have diverged.

Also drifted: todo/dto/watchlist_dto.py:13 declares `user_type: str  # "user" or "team"` -- the constraint demoted to a comment while its two siblings use `Literal`.
- **proposed:** Add to todo/constants/task.py:

  class AssigneeType(str, Enum): USER = "user"; TEAM = "team"
  with `@classmethod def choices(cls) -> list[str]` returning `[m.value for m in cls]`,
  and `AssigneeTypeStr = Literal["user", "team"]` for the pydantic side.

Call-site changes: every `choices=["user", "team"]` becomes `choices=AssigneeType.choices()` (create_task_assignment_serializer.py:10, create_task_serializer.py:31); every `Literal["user", "team"]` becomes `AssigneeTypeStr` (task_assignment_dto.py:10, :47, :61; models/task_assignment.py:20); todo/dto/watchlist_dto.py:13 changes from `str  # "user" or "team"` to `AssigneeTypeStr`. The four dead validator bodies are deleted outright. update_task_serializer's `assignee` DictField (line 23) is replaced by a nested `AssigneeSerializer` whose `user_type` is the same ChoiceField, which is what makes the fifth copy deletable too.

## 59. dueAt + timezone cross-field validation copied between create and update task serializers, then fixed differently in each
- **dimension:** dto_serializers | **severity:** high | **effort:** small | **copies:** 2 | **~lines:** 32
- **sites:** todo/serializers/create_task_serializer.py:81-96, todo/serializers/update_task_serializer.py:82-101
- **drift:** Two real, behaviour-changing divergences confirmed.

(1) Error shape. create raises `{"timezone": ValidationErrors.REQUIRED_TIMEZONE}` -- a scalar string (line 86); update assigns `errors["timezone"] = [ValidationErrors.REQUIRED_TIMEZONE]` -- a one-element list (line 88). The same validation failure therefore serialises as a string on POST /tasks and as an array on PATCH /tasks/{id}. This matters concretely because todo/views/team.py:102-119 `_handle_validation_errors` branches on `isinstance(messages, list)` and produces a different number of ApiErrorDetail entries for each shape.

(2) try/except scope. create wraps only the `ZoneInfo(timezone_str)` call (lines 87-90); update wraps the ZoneInfo call AND the date comparison (lines 90-99). A ZoneInfoNotFoundError raised during the comparison is a clean 400 on PATCH and an uncaught 500 on POST. Both copies share the same unfixed hole: `ZoneInfo("")` and `ZoneInfo("../x")` raise ValueError, not ZoneInfoNotFoundError, so neither catches it.

CORRECTION to the finder: its drift (1), `if due_at:` vs `if due_at is not None:`, is cosmetic, not behavioural. `dueAt` is a DRF DateTimeField, and every `datetime` instance is truthy; the only other possible value is `None`, which both guards skip. There is no falsy-but-present dueAt. Two of the three claimed drifts hold; the null-guard one does not.
- **proposed:** NEW FILE todo/serializers/validators.py:

  def validate_due_at_in_timezone(due_at: datetime | None, timezone_str: str | None) -> dict[str, list[str]]

Returns the accumulated error mapping, empty when clean, always with list values (the shape todo/views/team.py:102-119 `_handle_validation_errors` already handles best). It makes one decision about which exceptions to catch -- `(ZoneInfoNotFoundError, ValueError)`, closing the hole both copies share -- and one decision about scope: only the ZoneInfo construction is guarded, the comparison is not, so a bug in the comparison surfaces instead of being mislabelled a timezone error.

Call-site change: `CreateTaskSerializer.validate` loses lines 81-96 and `UpdateTaskSerializer.validate` loses lines 82-101, each becoming two lines. POST /tasks changes from scalar to list error values -- treat as the versioned change, and it is the whole point of the extraction.

## 60. page/limit query-param declarations restated in 3 serializers plus 2 hand-rolled service copies, with 3 message sets and a halved default
- **dimension:** dto_serializers | **severity:** high | **effort:** small | **copies:** 5 | **~lines:** 65
- **sites:** todo/serializers/get_labels_serializer.py:8-24, todo/serializers/get_watchlist_tasks_serializer.py:8-24, todo/serializers/get_tasks_serializer.py:15-31, todo/services/user_service.py:97-114, todo/services/task_service.py:126-135, todo/views/user.py:89-90
- **drift:** (1) Default page size. todo/serializers/get_labels_serializer.py:18 hardcodes `default=10`; the other two read `DEFAULT_PAGE_LIMIT`, which is 20 (todo_project/settings/base.py:104). GET /labels silently pages at half the size of every other list endpoint. The same 10-vs-20 split is duplicated a second time in the `PaginationConfig` dataclass that is itself triplicated: todo/services/label_service.py:17 `DEFAULT_LIMIT: int = 10` against todo/services/task_service.py:54 and todo/services/watchlist_service.py:25 which both read `DEFAULT_PAGE_LIMIT`.
(2) Message wording. todo/serializers/get_tasks_serializer.py:20 and :29 use raw literals "page must be greater than or equal to 1" / "limit must be greater than or equal to 1" while the other two serializers use `ValidationErrors.PAGE_POSITIVE` = "Page must be a positive integer" / `LIMIT_POSITIVE`. todo/services/task_service.py:129-135 uses a third wording again. Same condition, three different 400 bodies.
(3) Max limit. todo/services/user_service.py:110-111 hardcodes `if limit > 100 ... .format(100)` while MAX_PAGE_LIMIT is 200 (settings/base.py:105) -- the user-search endpoint caps at half the documented maximum. None of the three serializers sets a `max_value` message at all, so `ValidationErrors.MAX_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` is reachable only through the two hand-rolled service copies.
(4) No validation at all on the user path: todo/views/user.py:89-90 does bare `int(request.query_params.get("page", 1))`, so `?page=abc` raises an uncaught ValueError -> 500 rather than 400.

CORRECTION: the finder's range for `_validate_search_params` is todo/services/user_service.py:97-114, not 97-119.
- **proposed:** NEW FILE todo/serializers/pagination.py containing `class PaginationQueryParamsSerializer(serializers.Serializer)` with the two IntegerFields, both defaults and the max read from `settings.REST_FRAMEWORK["DEFAULT_PAGINATION_SETTINGS"]`, and both `min_value` AND `max_value` messages from `ValidationErrors`.

Call-site changes: `GetWatchlistTaskQueryParamsSerializer` becomes a bare subclass (its whole body, lines 8-24, deletes); `GetLabelQueryParamsSerializer` keeps only `search` and `validate_search`; `GetTaskQueryParamsSerializer` keeps only profile/sort_by/order/teamId/status. todo/views/user.py:89-90 adopts the serializer instead of bare `int(...)`, after which todo/services/user_service.py:104-111 drops its page/limit branches and keeps only the query-string check, and todo/services/task_service.py:126-135 deletes (its two callers at :79 and :701 are already behind `GetTaskQueryParamsSerializer`).

Call out separately, as a behaviour change rather than a refactor: GET /labels default page size goes 10 -> 20, and GET /users?limit= cap goes 100 -> 200.

## 61. Success-response envelope `{statusCode, message, data}` restated 4 times with three names for the message field and three for the payload
- **dimension:** dto_serializers | **severity:** low | **effort:** small | **copies:** 4 | **~lines:** 14
- **sites:** todo/dto/responses/create_task_response.py:6-9, todo/dto/responses/create_watchlist_response.py:6-9, todo/dto/responses/create_task_assignment_response.py:5-7, todo/dto/responses/get_task_by_id_response.py:5-6
- **drift:** The message field carries three different names for one concept: `successMessage` (create_task_response.py:8, create_watchlist_response.py:8), `message` (create_task_assignment_response.py:7, create_team_response.py:14, generate_team_creation_invite_code_response.py:15) and absent entirely (get_task_by_id_response.py:5-6). The message SOURCE drifted too: the two oldest copies pull from `AppMessages` (todo/constants/messages.py:2-9) while todo/dto/responses/create_task_assignment_response.py:7 hardcodes the literal "Task assignment created successfully" even though AppMessages is the established home for exactly these strings, and todo/dto/responses/create_team_response.py:14 declares `message: str` with no default at all despite `AppMessages.TEAM_CREATED` existing at messages.py:4. `statusCode` survives only on the two oldest copies, so the newer views compensate by hardcoding it: todo/views/task_assignment.py:62 and todo/views/team.py:86 both pass `status=status.HTTP_201_CREATED` by hand.

CORRECTION to the finder: it claimed 7 instances and ~40 lines. Three of its seven are not copies of this shape and I dropped them -- todo/dto/responses/create_team_response.py:5-14 wraps its payload under `team:` with a docstring, todo/dto/responses/generate_team_creation_invite_code_response.py:4-15 flattens `code`/`description` as siblings of `message` with per-field `Field(description=...)`, and todo/dto/responses/get_user_teams_response.py:6-8 is a list envelope (`teams` + `total`), not a single-entity one. Real count is 4 classes, ~14 lines.
- **proposed:** NEW FILE todo/dto/responses/success_response.py:

  T = TypeVar("T")
  class SuccessResponse(BaseModel, Generic[T]): statusCode: int = 200; message: str; data: T

The four classes keep their own names (so drf-spectacular component names in `OpenApiResponse(response=CreateTaskResponse)` at todo/views/task_assignment.py:107-109 etc. are unchanged) and shrink to a subclass line plus their two overridden defaults. Field names converge on `message` and `data`; `statusCode` reappears on the two classes that lost it, which lets todo/views/task_assignment.py:62 and todo/views/team.py:86 read the status off the response instead of hardcoding it.

Do NOT use bare aliases (`CreateTaskResponse = SuccessResponse[TaskDTO]`): that loses the per-class default message and renames the OpenAPI component to `SuccessResponse_TaskDTO_`. Subclassing keeps both. Rename `successMessage` -> `message` behind a version bump, and move the hardcoded "Task assignment created successfully" into `AppMessages` while you are there.

## 62. CreateTeamDTO and UpdateTeamDTO duplicate poc_id/member_ids validation -- and the N+1 fix landed on the copy that is dead code
- **dimension:** dto_serializers | **severity:** high | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 2 | **~lines:** 50
- **sites:** todo/dto/team_dto.py:14-28, todo/dto/update_team_dto.py:40-54, todo/dto/team_dto.py:30-39, todo/dto/update_team_dto.py:28-38, todo/serializers/create_team_serializer.py:18-29, todo/serializers/update_team_serializer.py:15-18
- **drift:** A performance fix landed on one copy only. todo/dto/update_team_dto.py:47-50 batches:

        # Batch validate all member IDs in a single database query
        existing_users = UserRepository.get_by_ids(value)
        existing_ids = {str(user.id) for user in existing_users}
        invalid_ids = [member_id for member_id in value if member_id not in existing_ids]

while todo/dto/team_dto.py:20-24 still loops:

        invalid_ids = []
        for member_id in value:
            user = UserRepository.get_by_id(member_id)
            if not user:
                invalid_ids.append(member_id)

`UserRepository.get_by_ids` exists at todo/repositories/user_repository.py:30, so the fixed version is available to both.

EVIDENCE THE FINDER MISSED, and it inverts the priority: `UpdateTeamDTO` is entirely dead. I grepped the whole tree -- the only references outside its own definition are two mock patch strings, todo/tests/unit/services/test_team_service.py:333 and :398 (`@patch("todo.dto.update_team_dto.UserRepository.get_by_id")`). Nothing imports it; todo/views/team.py:212-220 validates with `UpdateTeamSerializer` and hands the bare `poc_id` to `TeamService.update_team`. So the batch fix was applied to an unused class while the live team-creation path (todo/views/team.py:82 -> `CreateTeamDTO`) still pays one round trip per member.

Second drift, across layers: format and existence checking were split rather than shared. todo/serializers/create_team_serializer.py:18-29 checks format only (`ObjectId.is_valid`) and `CreateTeamDTO` checks existence only, so `CreateTeamDTO(...)` constructed outside a request hands a malformed id straight to the repository. Same split for update at todo/serializers/update_team_serializer.py:15-18 vs todo/dto/update_team_dto.py:28-38.

Also a pydantic-era drift within one file family: team_dto.py uses deprecated v1 `@validator` (lines 14, 30) while update_team_dto.py uses v2 `@field_validator` + `@classmethod` (lines 12, 20, 28, 40).
- **proposed:** NEW FILE todo/dto/validators/user_refs.py with `require_existing_user(user_id: str | None) -> str | None` and `require_existing_users(user_ids: list[str] | None) -> list[str] | None`, the latter using the batched `UserRepository.get_by_ids`. Expose them as `ExistingUserId` / `ExistingUserIds` Annotated aliases so both DTOs just declare the type.

Call-site change: todo/dto/team_dto.py:14-39 (26 lines, two validators) and todo/dto/update_team_dto.py:28-54 (27 lines, two validators) both delete; `poc_id` and `member_ids` become annotated field declarations.
