Combine with the ObjectIdStr alias from finding 1 so format and existence are both enforced in one place for create and update.

Sequencing note, and I think the finder's own caveat is right: a DB round trip inside a pydantic constructor makes `CreateTeamDTO(...)` untestable without a database. The better end state is existence checking in `TeamService` and format-only in the DTOs. But that is a larger move, and it does not change the immediate priority: whichever layer wins, the batched implementation must replace the N+1 in `CreateTeamDTO`, which is the live path. Fix the N+1 first, then decide the layer.

## 63. TeamCreationInviteCodeDTO and TeamCreationInviteCodeListItemDTO: the same 8 fields, 5 verbatim descriptions, drifted types -- and the canonical one is dead
- **dimension:** dto_serializers | **severity:** medium | **effort:** small | **copies:** 2 | **~lines:** 22
- **sites:** todo/dto/team_creation_invite_code_dto.py:20-30, todo/dto/responses/get_team_creation_invite_codes_response.py:6-16
- **drift:** Three divergences, all verified. (1) The `id` description was edited on one side only -- "Unique identifier for the team invite code" (team_creation_invite_code_dto.py:23) vs "Unique identifier for the team creation invite code" (get_team_creation_invite_codes_response.py:9). (2) `created_by`/`used_by` types diverge: `str` with "User ID of the admin who generated this code" in the DTO, untyped `dict` with "User details of who created this code" in the list item -- the list variant was upgraded to an embedded user object and surrendered type safety to do it. (3) `TeamCreationInviteCodeDTO` has zero imports anywhere in the repo (I grepped): the file's canonical DTO is dead while its copy, nested inside a response module, is the one the service actually uses at todo/services/team_creation_invite_code_service.py:50-60.

I also pinned down the repository projection the finder flagged as unknown risk. todo/repositories/team_creation_invite_code_repository.py:112-131 builds `created_by_user = {"id": str(user.id), "name": user.name}` and `"created_by": created_by_user or {}`, `"used_by": used_by_user`. So the `dict` is exactly `{id, name}` -- the shape of `UsersDTO` (todo/dto/user_dto.py:21-23). Typing the field properly is safe today, with one caveat: `created_by` falls back to `{}` when the user lookup misses, so the field must be `Optional`.
- **proposed:** Delete `TeamCreationInviteCodeListItemDTO` from todo/dto/responses/get_team_creation_invite_codes_response.py:6-16 and promote `TeamCreationInviteCodeDTO` (todo/dto/team_creation_invite_code_dto.py:20-30) to the single definition, retyping `created_by: Optional[UsersDTO]` and `used_by: Optional[UsersDTO]` to match what the repository actually produces.

Call-site changes: `GetTeamCreationInviteCodesResponse.codes` (get_team_creation_invite_codes_response.py:22-24) types as `List[TeamCreationInviteCodeDTO]`; the import block at todo/services/team_creation_invite_code_service.py:8-11 drops `TeamCreationInviteCodeListItemDTO` and imports `TeamCreationInviteCodeDTO` from todo/dto/team_creation_invite_code_dto.py; the construction at service lines 50-59 changes only its class name. Because the repository already emits `{id, name}` dicts, pydantic coerces them into `UsersDTO` with no service change -- but guard `created_by` as Optional so the `or {}` fallback at repository line 129 does not start raising. Low risk overall: one of the two classes has no users.

## 64. Three overlapping user DTOs -- the service fills one with another's fields and silently drops them, and the list comprehension is written twice

- **sites:** todo/dto/user_dto.py:6-10, todo/dto/user_dto.py:13-18, todo/dto/user_dto.py:21-23, todo/services/user_service.py:41-55, todo/services/user_service.py:116-130, todo/views/user.py:98-104
- **drift:** There is a live bug caused directly by the two near-identical user DTOs sitting side by side. `UserSearchDTO` has zero imports anywhere in the repo (grepped), yet todo/services/user_service.py:47-53 constructs a `UserDTO` with exactly `UserSearchDTO`'s field set:

                    UserDTO(
                        id=str(user.id),
                        name=user.name,
                        email_id=user.email_id,
                        created_at=user.created_at,
                        updated_at=user.updated_at,
                    )

`UserDTO` (user_dto.py:6-10) declares none of email_id/created_at/updated_at and sets no `model_config`, so under pydantic 2.10.1 (requirements.txt:14) the default `extra="ignore"` discards all three silently. `get_users_by_ids` reads as though it returns email and timestamps; it returns `{id, name, addedOn, tasksAssignedCount}`. Its consumer todo/services/user_service.py:58-76 then sets `addedOn`/`tasksAssignedCount` on those same objects, so the two field sets were clearly meant to be one model.

Second drift: todo/services/user_service.py:117 declares `get_all_users(...) -> tuple[List[UsersDTO], int]` and already builds the DTOs at :122-128, yet todo/views/user.py:96-104 re-runs the identical comprehension over that result, wrapping DTOs in DTOs. It only works because `UsersDTO` happens to expose `.id` and `.name`, so `str(user.id)` on an already-str id is a no-op. The view has to do this because the sibling branch, `UserService.search_users` (user_service.py:33), returns raw `UserModel`s -- two functions on the same class returning two different types for the same concept, which is the duplication paying for itself.

CORRECTION to the finder's ranges: `get_users_by_ids` is todo/services/user_service.py:41-55 (not 41-54), `get_all_users` is :116-130 (not 115-129), and the view comprehension is todo/views/user.py:98-104 (not 97-104).
- **proposed:** Collapse to one `UserDTO` in todo/dto/user_dto.py with `id`/`name` required and `email_id`, `created_at`, `updated_at`, `addedOn`, `tasksAssignedCount` optional, plus a `@classmethod from_model(cls, user_model, **extras) -> UserDTO` -- mirroring the `RoleDTO.from_model` pattern that already exists at todo/dto/role_dto.py:25-55, so this is an established convention in the codebase rather than a new one. Set `model_config = ConfigDict(extra="forbid")` so the next mismatched constructor call raises instead of silently dropping fields.

Call-site changes: todo/services/user_service.py:46-54 becomes `users.append(UserDTO.from_model(user))`; the comprehension at :122-128 becomes `[UserDTO.from_model(u) for u in users]`; `UserService.search_users` returns the same DTO type so both branches agree, and todo/views/user.py:98-104 deletes entirely, consuming the service's list directly. `UserSearchDTO` and `UsersDTO` are deleted.

Heed the finder's caveat: `UsersDTO` is what the public search endpoint returns, so the merge must not leak `email_id`. Give the response an explicit projection -- `UserSearchResponseDTO.users: List[UserDTO]` serialised with `model_dump(exclude={"users": {"__all__": {"email_id"}}})`, or a `.public()` method -- rather than relying on `exclude_none`, which would leak the field the moment a path populates it.

## 65. TaskAssignmentDTO and TaskAssignmentResponseDTO are the same 11 fields with two transposed and one dropped -- the copy-paste carried a code comment verbatim
- **dimension:** dto_serializers | **severity:** medium | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 2 | **~lines:** 36
- **sites:** todo/dto/task_assignment_dto.py:42-54, todo/dto/task_assignment_dto.py:57-68, todo/services/task_assignment_service.py:109-120, todo/services/task_assignment_service.py:143-155, todo/dto/watchlist_dto.py:10-13
- **drift:** The two copies disagree on content and on field order. `TaskAssignmentDTO` lists assignee_name before user_type (lines 46-47) and carries `team_id` (line 49); `TaskAssignmentResponseDTO` lists user_type before assignee_name (lines 61-62) and has no `team_id`. Confirmed at the endpoints: GET /task-assignments/{task_id} returns `TaskAssignmentResponseDTO` via todo/services/task_assignment_service.py:143 and therefore omits the team a task was reassigned from, while POST returns `TaskAssignmentDTO` inside `CreateTaskAssignmentResponse` (service line 122) and includes the key. Same entity, same endpoint family, two payload shapes.

Note the omission is currently vacuous, which strengthens the case for collapsing: the POST construction at service lines 109-120 never passes `team_id` either, so the field is always null on both paths despite `TaskAssignmentModel.team_id` being populated at todo/models/task_assignment.py:27. The DTO split is hiding an unfinished feature rather than protecting a deliberate projection.

Third, smaller cut of the same entity: todo/dto/watchlist_dto.py:10-13 `AssigneeDTO{assignee_id, assignee_name, user_type}` -- where `user_type: str  # "user" or "team"` demotes to a comment the `Literal["user", "team"]` both siblings declare.

CORRECTION to the finder: it counted AssigneeDTO as a third full copy. It is not -- 3 fields against 11, different purpose, no shared verbatim text. The strong duplication is the two 11-field classes; AssigneeDTO is a related type-drift site.
- **proposed:** Delete `TaskAssignmentResponseDTO` (todo/dto/task_assignment_dto.py:57-68) and return `TaskAssignmentDTO` from both paths. If omitting `team_id` from the GET response is genuinely intended, express that at serialisation -- `dto.model_dump(exclude={"team_id"})` at the one view that needs it -- rather than by maintaining a second class; that keeps the omission visible and local instead of encoded in a duplicated field list.

Then introduce `class AssigneeSummary(BaseModel){assignee_id: str, assignee_name: Optional[str], user_type: AssigneeTypeStr}` in todo/dto/task_assignment_dto.py using the alias from finding 2; `TaskAssignmentDTO` inherits it, and `AssigneeDTO` in todo/dto/watchlist_dto.py:10-13 is replaced by it, which fixes the bare-`str` user_type as a side effect.

Call-site changes: todo/services/task_assignment_service.py:143-155 changes its class name and gains `team_id=str(assignment.team_id) if assignment.team_id else None`; the same line should be added at :109-120, since neither construction populates it today. todo/dto/watchlist_dto.py:31 becomes `assignee: Optional[AssigneeSummary] = None`.

The finder's caveat -- that collapsing might start leaking the originating team to watchers -- is worth checking but is currently moot: the field is null on every path. Confirm intent, then either populate it on both or drop it from the model.

## 66. TaskDTO and WatchlistDTO restate 12 shared task fields with different nested types for the same field, plus an identical copy-pasted Config block
- **dimension:** dto_serializers | **severity:** medium | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 2 | **~lines:** 40
- **sites:** todo/dto/task_dto.py:14-34, todo/dto/watchlist_dto.py:16-34
- **drift:** Three shared field names carry different types in the two copies. `deferredDetails` is `DeferredDetailsDTO` in TaskDTO (task_dto.py:26) but `DeferredDetailsModel` in WatchlistDTO (watchlist_dto.py:22), imported straight from todo/models/task.py -- and the two differ where it counts: `DeferredDetailsDTO.deferredBy: UserDTO` (todo/dto/deferred_details_dto.py:9) against `DeferredDetailsModel.deferredBy: str | None` (todo/models/task.py:17). The same logical field therefore serialises as a nested user object on GET /tasks and as a bare id string on GET /watchlist, and the watchlist response leaks a Mongo model type through the DTO layer. `labels` is `List[LabelDTO]` (task_dto.py:23) vs untyped `list` (watchlist_dto.py:26). `assignee` is `TaskAssignmentDTO` vs `AssigneeDTO`. The id field name diverged too (`id` vs `taskId`), and WatchlistDTO exposes `isDeleted` (line 25) that TaskDTO deliberately does not.

CORRECTION to the finder's risk assessment, and it makes the fix cheaper than claimed: it warned that tightening `labels` would force WatchlistService to do the hydration TaskService does. It already does. todo/services/watchlist_service.py:156 calls `cls._prepare_label_dtos(watchlist_model.labels)`, defined at :141-152, which returns `[LabelDTO(...)]` exactly like TaskService's. So `labels: list` is merely an untyped declaration over data that is already `LabelDTO` at runtime -- tightening it to `List[LabelDTO]` costs nothing and no extra query. Only `deferredDetails` is genuinely passed through raw (watchlist_service.py:179 `deferredDetails=watchlist_model.deferredDetails`) and needs the user hydration.
- **proposed:** NEW `class TaskSummaryDTO(BaseModel)` in todo/dto/task_dto.py carrying the twelve shared fields with one canonical type each -- `labels: List[LabelDTO]`, `deferredDetails: DeferredDetailsDTO | None`, `assignee: AssigneeSummary | None` (the base from finding 11) -- and the single `class Config: json_encoders = {TaskPriority: lambda x: x.name}`.

Call-site changes: `TaskDTO(TaskSummaryDTO)` adds only `id`, `startedAt`, `updatedAt`, `updatedBy`, `in_watchlist`; `WatchlistDTO(TaskSummaryDTO)` adds only `watchlistId`, `isDeleted`, and `taskId: str = Field(alias="id")`. Both `class Config` blocks delete.

Stage it: the `labels` and `Config` parts land for free today. The `deferredDetails` part requires todo/services/watchlist_service.py:179 to build a `DeferredDetailsDTO` -- one user lookup per deferred row, and only for rows that are deferred -- so measure the watchlist list endpoint before committing to that half. The `isDeleted` field being present on the watchlist payload and absent from the task payload is worth confirming as deliberate before the base class makes it uniform.

## 67. CreateTaskSerializer and UpdateTaskSerializer redeclare the same seven fields with quietly incompatible constraints
- **dimension:** dto_serializers | **severity:** high | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 2 | **~lines:** 45
- **sites:** todo/serializers/create_task_serializer.py:10-49, todo/serializers/update_task_serializer.py:11-34
- **drift:** Four concrete divergences on the same seven fields, all verified.

(1) `title`. create_task_serializer.py:10 is `required=True, allow_blank=False` with NO max_length; update_task_serializer.py:11 is `required=False, allow_blank=True, max_length=255`. A 300-character title is accepted on POST /tasks and rejected on PATCH /tasks/{id}. Worse, update's field layer explicitly permits a blank title and its own `validate_title` (lines 36-39) then rejects it -- the field declaration and the method contradict each other inside one class.
(2) `timezone`. required=True on create (line 39), required=False on update (line 29), while both `validate()` bodies enforce mandatory-if-dueAt-present. Two mechanisms for one rule.
(3) `assignee`. Three flat fields on create -- assignee_id (27-29), user_type (30-32), team_id (45-49) -- recombined into a dict at create_task_serializer.py:74-79, versus a single opaque `DictField` on update (line 23). The update path therefore silently drops the `team_id` the create path accepts and stores.
(4) Defaults. create supplies `default=TaskPriority.LOW.name` and `default=TaskStatus.TODO.name` (lines 17, 23); update supplies none.
- **proposed:** NEW `class TaskWriteSerializer(serializers.Serializer)` in todo/serializers/task_fields.py holding the seven field declarations once -- title, description, priority, status, labels, timezone, dueAt -- plus `validate_title` and the shared `validate()` from finding 3, and a nested `AssigneeSerializer` (finding 2) so team_id is handled identically on both paths. Enum choices come from `TaskPriority.choices()` / `TaskStatus.choices()` (finding 16).

Call-site changes: `CreateTaskSerializer(TaskWriteSerializer)` keeps only its defaults and `required=True` overrides; `UpdateTaskSerializer(TaskWriteSerializer)` keeps only startedAt/isAcknowledged and is instantiated with DRF's own `partial=True`, which is the idiomatic spelling of 'update is create with everything optional' and removes the need to restate `required=False` on every field. The one constraint that must be settled rather than inherited is `title`: pick a max_length and apply it to both, since today the two endpoints disagree.

The finder's caveats are fair and should shape the sequencing: `timezone` genuinely need not be required on a PATCH that does not touch dueAt (so keep that as an explicit override, not an accident), and a nested assignee serializer on update is a request-shape change for clients currently posting a flat dict -- ship that endpoint last. `partial=True` also changes DRF's absent-vs-null handling, which the update path distinguishes by hand today; check `validate_labels` and `validate_assignee` against it before switching.

## 68. validate_labels written twice; create reports the first bad id, update reports all of them
- **dimension:** dto_serializers | **severity:** medium | **effort:** small | **copies:** 2 | **~lines:** 19
- **sites:** todo/serializers/create_task_serializer.py:56-60, todo/serializers/update_task_serializer.py:41-54
- **drift:** One live divergence, and I have to retract two of the finder's three.

LIVE: reporting granularity. create raises on the FIRST bad id (create_task_serializer.py:58-59), update collects ALL of them and raises a list (update_task_serializer.py:48-52). A POST with three malformed labels returns one error string; the identical PATCH body returns three. Combined with todo/views/task.py:143 -> `_handle_validation_errors`, which branches on `isinstance(messages, list)`, the two endpoints emit structurally different 400 bodies for the same input.

RETRACTED (1): the None guard is not drift, it is required. update's `labels` ListField is `allow_null=True` (update_task_serializer.py:27), so `validate_labels` really can receive None; create's (create_task_serializer.py:33-38) is not allow_null, so DRF's `ListField.validate_empty_values` rejects `"labels": null` with "This field may not be null." before the method runs. No TypeError is reachable on create, contrary to the claim.

RETRACTED (2): the `isinstance(value, (list, tuple))` guard at update_task_serializer.py:45-46 is unreachable. `ListField.to_internal_value` already rejects non-lists with "Expected a list of items but got type ..." before any `validate_<field>` method is called, so `ValidationErrors.INVALID_LABELS_STRUCTURE` is dead code -- not a hardening the create copy lacks.

So one of three claimed drifts is real. It is still a genuine, user-visible inconsistency, and this finding overlaps finding 1 (both copies are ObjectId-list checks); fixing finding 1 subsumes this one.
- **proposed:** Prefer the `object_id_list_field()` factory from finding 1 over a new helper -- `ListField(child=ObjectIdField())` makes DRF do the per-item validation, both `validate_labels` methods delete outright, and the reporting shape becomes uniform because DRF's own child-field error aggregation produces an index-keyed dict for every list field in the project rather than a bespoke shape per serializer. The dead `INVALID_LABELS_STRUCTURE` branch goes with it.

If a standalone function is preferred instead, put `validate_object_id_list(value, *, allow_none=True) -> list[str]` in todo/serializers/validators.py implementing collect-all reporting, and have both methods delegate.

Either way this is a client-visible change on POST /tasks: `errors["labels"]` goes from a scalar string to a collection. Ship it with the same version bump as finding 3, which changes the create-path error shape for the same reason.

## 69. Enum-choices comprehension repeated 5 times with .name in four and .value in one, and case-insensitivity applied to only one endpoint
- **dimension:** dto_serializers | **severity:** medium | **effort:** small | **copies:** 5 | **~lines:** 28
- **sites:** todo/serializers/create_task_serializer.py:14-25, todo/serializers/update_task_serializer.py:13-22, todo/serializers/get_tasks_serializer.py:47-51, todo/serializers/get_tasks_serializer.py:7-11, todo/dto/task_dto.py:47-57
- **drift:** (1) The case-insensitivity fix landed on one copy of the idiom and not the others. `CaseInsensitiveChoiceField` (todo/serializers/get_tasks_serializer.py:7-11) upper-cases incoming values in `to_internal_value`, but it is applied only to `status` on the list endpoint (line 47); create and update use plain `ChoiceField`. Confirmed consequence: `GET /tasks?status=todo` succeeds while `POST /tasks {"status":"todo"}` is a 400.
(2) `.name` vs `.value`. For TaskStatus the two coincide today (todo/constants/task.py:4-9 assigns `TODO = "TODO"` etc.), so nothing is broken yet -- but TaskPriority has `HIGH = 1` (task.py:13), so the two idioms are already not interchangeable, and the first TaskStatus member whose value differs from its name silently splits the filter vocabulary from the write vocabulary.
(3) A third mechanism for the same string->enum mapping. `TaskPriority[value]` at todo/dto/task_dto.py:50 is a NAME lookup, so it agrees with the four `.name` serializers and disagrees with the one `.value` serializer -- by coincidence, not by a shared definition. And these DTO parsers re-do coercion that DRF's ChoiceField already performed on every request path (todo/views/task.py:140-146).
- **proposed:** Give the enums the vocabulary, in todo/constants/task.py: `TaskStatus.choices()` and `TaskPriority.choices()` classmethods returning the canonical `.name` list, plus a shared `coerce(cls, value)` classmethod for the string->member mapping.

Call-site changes: the five comprehensions become `choices=TaskPriority.choices()` / `choices=TaskStatus.choices()`, which forces the .name/.value question to be answered once. Move `CaseInsensitiveChoiceField` out of todo/serializers/get_tasks_serializer.py:7-11 into todo/serializers/fields.py (alongside `ObjectIdField` from finding 1) and use it at all four remaining sites so the leniency decision is made once instead of per file. `parse_priority`/`parse_status` at todo/dto/task_dto.py:47-57 collapse to a single reusable `Annotated` coercer, or delete outright if the ChoiceField is trusted to have done the job.

The finder's caveat is the real decision and should be settled before the mechanical change: making all four case-insensitive loosens two write endpoints -- `POST /tasks {"priority":"low"}` is correctly a 400 today and would start succeeding. Tightening GET /tasks to be case-sensitive is the equally valid direction and is the smaller behaviour change. Either way, pick one and let the shared field enforce it.

## 70. Auth-token branches duplicated inside handle_exception's single elif chain: 3 exception types handled twice, 3 branches dead, and the surviving copy is the wrong one
- **dimension:** exceptions | **severity:** high | **effort:** small | **copies:** 7 | **~lines:** 85
- **sites:** todo/exceptions/exception_handler.py:56-64, todo/exceptions/exception_handler.py:65-80, todo/exceptions/exception_handler.py:81-96, todo/exceptions/exception_handler.py:98-113, todo/exceptions/exception_handler.py:114-129, todo/exceptions/exception_handler.py:130-145, todo/exceptions/exception_handler.py:146-154
- **drift:** Confirmed and slightly broader than claimed. TokenExpiredError's live branch (:56-64) has no early return, so it falls to the shared tail at :290-295 which builds ApiErrorResponse(statusCode, message, errors) with no authenticated argument; ApiErrorResponse.authenticated is `bool | None = None` (todo/dto/responses/error_response.py:23) and the tail dumps with exclude_none=True, so the field is omitted entirely. Missing/invalid tokens return authenticated: false; expired ones return no authenticated key at all. RefreshTokenExpiredError (:146-154, which the finder omitted) has the same defect -- it also falls to the tail. So the fix landed in the two unreachable copies and in two of the four live branches.
- **proposed:** In todo/exceptions/exception_handler.py, delete the dead branches at :98-145, then replace the four remaining auth branches with one table lookup. Add near the top of the module:

AUTH_ERROR_SPECS: dict[type, tuple[int, str]] = {
    TokenExpiredError: (status.HTTP_401_UNAUTHORIZED, AuthErrorMessages.TOKEN_EXPIRED_TITLE),
    TokenMissingError: (status.HTTP_401_UNAUTHORIZED, AuthErrorMessages.AUTHENTICATION_REQUIRED),
    TokenInvalidError: (status.HTTP_401_UNAUTHORIZED, AuthErrorMessages.INVALID_TOKEN_TITLE),
    RefreshTokenExpiredError: (status.HTTP_403_FORBIDDEN, AuthErrorMessages.TOKEN_EXPIRED_TITLE),
}

and a helper `_auth_error_response(exc) -> Response | None` that returns None when exc matches no key. handle_exception then opens with `auth_response = _auth_error_response(exc)` / `if auth_response is not None: return auth_response`, and the whole :56-154 span collapses to three lines. Ordering matters: iterate AUTH_ERROR_SPECS in insertion order so subclass relationships stay deterministic (all four are flat subclasses of BaseAuthException today, so any order works).

## 71. Two different UserNotFoundException classes; the repository raises the one the handler cannot see, so user-not-found returns 500
- **dimension:** exceptions | **severity:** high | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 4 | **~lines:** 25
- **sites:** todo/exceptions/auth_exceptions.py:40-42, todo/exceptions/user_exceptions.py:4-13, todo/exceptions/exception_handler.py:173-181, todo/exceptions/exception_handler.py:192-200, todo/repositories/user_repository.py:10, todo/repositories/user_repository.py:27, todo/repositories/user_repository.py:44, todo/services/user_service.py:4-7, todo/services/user_service.py:29
- **drift:** Confirmed, and the split is functional. RepositoryErrors.USER_NOT_FOUND is "User not found: {0}" (constants/messages.py:17) while ApiErrors.USER_NOT_FOUND is "User with ID {0} not found." (messages.py:53) -- two templates for one condition, and the auth variant passes the template through verbatim so the literal "{0}" reaches the client. Grep confirms the split by layer: todo/repositories/user_repository.py:10 and todo/services/user_service.py:4-7 import the auth_exceptions variant (raised bare at user_repository.py:27, :44 and user_service.py:29), while todo/services/task_service.py:21, todo/services/task_assignment_service.py:11 and todo/views/task_assignment.py:16 import the user_exceptions one. Since exception_handler.py:26 only knows the user_exceptions class, a repository-raised user-not-found matches neither branch, falls to the generic else at :262-281 with status_code still at its :54 default of HTTP_500_INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR, and returns 500 instead of 404. Test modules are split too (tests/unit/repositories/test_user_repository.py:8 and tests/unit/services/test_user_service.py:7 use auth; tests/unit/services/test_task_service.py:38 uses user).
- **proposed:** Keep exactly one class, in todo/exceptions/user_exceptions.py, and give it an optional message override so the auth call sites keep compiling:

    class UserNotFoundException(Exception):
        def __init__(self, user_id: str | None = None, message_template: str = ApiErrors.USER_NOT_FOUND)

Delete auth_exceptions.UserNotFoundException (auth_exceptions.py:40-42) and repoint the four importers -- todo/repositories/user_repository.py:10, todo/services/user_service.py:4-7, todo/tests/unit/repositories/test_user_repository.py:8, todo/tests/unit/services/test_user_service.py:7 -- at todo.exceptions.user_exceptions. Their raise sites are already `UserNotFoundException()` with no args, which hits the USER_NOT_FOUND_GENERIC branch and needs no edit. Then collapse exception_handler.py:173-181 and :192-200 into one branch computing source conditionally, and delete RepositoryErrors.USER_NOT_FOUND (messages.py:17) now that nothing reads it.

## 72. Nine mutually incompatible validation-error responses: _handle_validation_errors redefined eight times plus one inlined copy
- **dimension:** exceptions | **severity:** high | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 10 | **~lines:** 95
- **sites:** todo/views/task.py:169-195, todo/views/task.py:367-393, todo/views/team.py:102-123, todo/views/team.py:127-129, todo/views/team.py:319-326, todo/views/team.py:469-489, todo/views/team_creation_invite_code.py:28-30, todo/views/team_creation_invite_code.py:125-127, todo/views/task.py:424, todo/exceptions/exception_handler.py:29-45
- **drift:** Confirmed; five incompatible bodies for one event, and I found a sixth site the finder missed. (1) task.py:169-195 / :367-393 / team.py:102-123 emit the full ApiErrorDetail list. (2) team.py:127-129 and tcic:28-30 / :125-127 emit raw {"errors": serializer.errors} with no envelope. (3) todo/views/task.py:424 inlines that same raw shape without even defining the method: `return Response(data={"errors": serializer.errors}, status=status.HTTP_400_BAD_REQUEST)` -- a ninth site. (4) team.py:319-326 drops the field name: `errors=[{"detail": str(error)} for error in errors.values()]`. (5) team.py:469-489 bypasses the pydantic model completely, hand-building a dict with the literal "Validation Error" (duplicating ApiErrors.VALIDATION_ERROR), a title "Invalid value" used nowhere else, and an `authenticated` key no other copy has; its inner `for msg in msgs` assumes every value is iterable, so a scalar DRF error is iterated character-by-character into one detail per character. Only exception_handler.py:29-45 recurses into nested serializer dicts.
- **proposed:** Add todo/utils/error_response_utils.py with `validation_error_response(errors) -> Response`, delegating detail-building to the existing, most-capable `format_validation_errors` in todo/exceptions/exception_handler.py:29-45 (it is the only copy that recurses into nested dicts and the only one that tolerates a scalar). Delete all eight private methods and the inline copy at todo/views/task.py:424; each `if not serializer.is_valid():` becomes `return validation_error_response(serializer.errors)`. To avoid a views -> exceptions import cycle, move format_validation_errors into the new util module and have exception_handler.py import it from there. The end state is better still: have the views `raise DRFValidationError(serializer.errors)` and let handle_exception's existing :256-260 branch do it once, deleting the helper too.

## 73. The 500-fallback ApiErrorResponse envelope rebuilt in eleven view except-blocks, two of which lost the settings.DEBUG guard in opposite directions
- **dimension:** exceptions | **severity:** high | **effort:** small | **copies:** 11 | **~lines:** 78
- **sites:** todo/views/task.py:159-167, todo/views/task.py:357-365, todo/views/task.py:434-439, todo/views/team.py:50-57, todo/views/team.py:93-100, todo/views/team.py:179-184, todo/views/team.py:312-317, todo/views/task_assignment.py:77-84, todo/views/task_assignment.py:138-145, todo/views/task_assignment.py:301-308, todo/views/watchlist.py:101-108
- **drift:** Confirmed in both directions. todo/views/watchlist.py:104 is `errors=[{"detail": ApiErrors.INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR}]` -- the DEBUG guard is gone, so watchlist 500s are undebuggable even with DEBUG on. todo/views/task.py:437 is `errors=[{"detail": str(e)}]` -- the guard is gone the other way, so the assign-task endpoint leaks raw exception text in production. Nine copies get it right. Cosmetic drift on top: task.py:434 names the variable `error_response` while the other ten use `fallback_response`; team.py:184 and team.py:317 pass the bare int `500` to Response(..., status=500) where the other nine use status.HTTP_500_INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR. One correction to the finder: passing a raw dict for `errors` is not a latent bug -- ApiErrorResponse.errors is `List[ApiErrorDetail]` (todo/dto/responses/error_response.py:22) and pydantic coerces the dict, so all eleven work today; it is still worth passing a real ApiErrorDetail for typo safety.
- **proposed:** Add `internal_error_response(exc: Exception) -> Response` to the same todo/utils/error_response_utils.py introduced above, and reduce every `except Exception as e:` block in the four view modules to `return internal_error_response(e)`. That removes 78 lines and makes the DEBUG guard exist exactly once. Note that most of these blocks are arguably redundant with handle_exception (which already produces the same envelope at exception_handler.py:262-295), so the stronger version of this change is to delete the except-blocks outright and let the DRF handler run; the helper is the safe intermediate step that preserves current status codes.

## 74. "Unwrap an ApiErrorResponse smuggled through ValueError.args[0]" reimplemented ten times, seven of them without the empty-args guard
- **dimension:** exceptions | **severity:** high | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 10 | **~lines:** 45
- **sites:** todo/views/task.py:151-157, todo/views/task.py:349-355, todo/views/team.py:45-48, todo/views/team.py:88-91, todo/views/team.py:300-303, todo/views/watchlist.py:96-99, todo/services/task_service.py:655-657, todo/services/watchlist_service.py:101-103, todo/exceptions/exception_handler.py:249-255, todo/exceptions/exception_handler.py:283-285
- **drift:** Confirmed exactly as claimed. Only three of the ten sites guard the index. todo/views/task.py:350 is `if isinstance(e, ValueError) and e.args and isinstance(e.args[0], ApiErrorResponse):` and the two handler copies use `hasattr(exc, "args") and exc.args and ...`; the other seven (task.py:152, team.py:46, :89, :301, watchlist.py:97, task_service.py:656, watchlist_service.py:102) index e.args[0] unguarded, so a bare `raise ValueError()` raises IndexError from inside the except block and produces an unhandled 500 with a misleading traceback. Someone hardened one view and the handler and left the rest. The fallback path drifted too: eight sites fall through to a 500, but todo/views/team.py:305-310 falls through to `ApiErrorResponse(statusCode=400, message=str(e), errors=[{"detail": str(e)}])` and todo/views/team.py:171-177 to a 404 with message=str(e) -- so the same un-wrapped ValueError yields 400, 404 or 500 depending on which view caught it.
- **proposed:** Two steps. Interim (mechanical, no behavior change beyond fixing the IndexError): add `extract_api_error(exc: Exception) -> ApiErrorResponse | None` to todo/utils/error_response_utils.py implementing the guarded test once, and use it at all ten sites. Real fix: stop smuggling responses through ValueError. Add todo/exceptions/api_exceptions.py with `class ApiException(Exception)` carrying an `.error_response: ApiErrorResponse`, raise that from the services, and handle it in one branch of handle_exception. Because todo/services/task_service.py:318 and its siblings raise plain ValueErrors for genuine value errors in the same call paths, ApiException must NOT subclass ValueError -- introduce it alongside, keep the `except ValueError` catches for the plain ones, and migrate service by service.

## 75. Service-layer "wrap exception in ApiErrorResponse" envelope copied seven times across two services and one util, with inconsistent pseudo-sources
- **dimension:** exceptions | **severity:** medium | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 7 | **~lines:** 80
- **sites:** todo/services/task_service.py:658-670, todo/services/task_service.py:671-684, todo/services/watchlist_service.py:49-62, todo/services/watchlist_service.py:104-116, todo/services/watchlist_service.py:117-130, todo/utils/task_validation_utils.py:26-38, todo/utils/task_validation_utils.py:42-54
- **drift:** Confirmed. The `source` key is a free-for-all for the same class of failure: {ApiErrorSource.PARAMETER: "server"} and {ApiErrorSource.PARAMETER: "task_repository"} are pseudo-sources even though ApiErrorSource.PARAMETER is meant to name a request parameter (todo/dto/responses/error_response.py:6-10). todo/utils/task_validation_utils.py:32 and :48 use {ApiErrorSource.PARAMETER: "taskId"} for the same task-id error that todo/exceptions/exception_handler.py:230 and :244 report as {ApiErrorSource.PATH: "task_id"} -- parameter vs path, camelCase vs snake_case, for one field. The message strings drifted too: todo/utils/task_validation_utils.py:29/:34 use ApiErrors.INVALID_TASK_ID ("Invalid task ID format", messages.py:38) while exception_handler.py:232/:246 use ValidationErrors.INVALID_TASK_ID_FORMAT ("Please enter a valid Task ID format.", messages.py:76) for the identical condition. And the DEBUG-guarded detail expression is parenthesized in task_service and bare in watchlist_service -- harmless, but proof these were copied and lightly edited.
- **proposed:** Add todo/exceptions/api_error_factory.py exposing `repository_error(exc)`, `server_error(exc)` and `not_found(title, detail, source)`, each returning a fully built ApiErrorResponse with the DEBUG-guarded detail written once. Pair it with the ApiException from finding 5 so a service block reads `raise ApiException(server_error(e))`. While doing it, settle the task-id convention on {ApiErrorSource.PATH: "task_id"} everywhere and delete the duplicate ApiErrors.INVALID_TASK_ID in favour of ValidationErrors.INVALID_TASK_ID_FORMAT. Note that source keys serialize as the enum VALUE (Dict[ApiErrorSource, str] under pydantic), so switching PARAMETER to PATH changes the JSON key from "parameter" to "path" -- an API-visible change worth batching with the other error-shape work.

## 76. Three parallel role-exception to status-code mappings, one of them entirely dead, and the whole role subtree on a second error contract
- **dimension:** exceptions | **severity:** medium | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 3 | **~lines:** 55
- **sites:** todo/exceptions/global_exception_handler.py:16-36, todo/exceptions/global_exception_handler.py:39-68, todo/views/role.py:21-41
- **drift:** Confirmed. `grep -rn 'handle_exceptions' todo/ todo_project/` finds the decorator only at its definition (global_exception_handler.py:16) -- it is applied nowhere, so 21 lines are dead. The class copy grew a fourth branch the decorator never got: GlobalExceptionHandler.handle_validation_error (:57-61) returns a {"error": "Validation failed", "details": ..., "status_code": 400} shape, but todo/views/role.py never calls it (grep confirms only handle_role_not_found, handle_role_operation_error, handle_generic_error at role.py:34/:37/:40), so validation errors in role views silently take the generic 500 path. Both surviving copies hardcode the literal "Internal server error" (global_exception_handler.py:33 and :67) instead of ApiErrors.INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR (messages.py:29). And the entire role subtree emits {"error": "..."} while every other endpoint emits {statusCode, message, errors, authenticated} -- two incompatible error contracts in one API.
- **proposed:** Delete todo/exceptions/global_exception_handler.py entirely: the decorator at :16-36 is dead, and GlobalExceptionHandler at :39-68 exists only to be re-wrapped by role.py. Register the two role exceptions in the one handler that already exists, todo/exceptions/exception_handler.py, next to the TaskNotFoundException branch at :182-190; then BaseRoleView (todo/views/role.py:18-41) drops _handle_exceptions and RoleListView.get / RoleDetailView.get (role.py:113, :143) return `_execute()` directly, letting DRF route failures through handle_exception. Role endpoints then return the same ApiErrorResponse body as everything else. If the {"error": ...} contract must be frozen for /roles, the reduced win is still worth taking: delete the dead decorator and replace the two "Internal server error" literals with ApiErrors.INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR.

## 77. Identical exception __init__ bodies repeated across four modules, and two entity-not-found classes that drifted on template-formatting safety
- **dimension:** exceptions | **severity:** medium | **effort:** small | **copies:** 6 | **~lines:** 40
- **sites:** todo/exceptions/auth_exceptions.py:4-7, todo/exceptions/team_exceptions.py:4-7, todo/exceptions/task_exceptions.py:20-23, todo/exceptions/task_exceptions.py:13-17, todo/exceptions/task_exceptions.py:4-10, todo/exceptions/user_exceptions.py:4-13
- **drift:** The entity-not-found drift is real and is the part worth acting on. todo/exceptions/user_exceptions.py:7-10 hardened the template call:
    try:
        self.message = message_template.format(user_id)
    except (KeyError, ValueError):
        self.message = f"{message_template} (ID: {user_id})"
while todo/exceptions/task_exceptions.py:7 still calls `message_template.format(task_id)` bare -- a caller passing any template with a named or malformed placeholder gets a KeyError/ValueError raised from inside the exception constructor, replacing the intended 404 with an unhandled 500. Someone fixed one copy and not the other. Secondary drift: BaseAuthException.__init__ annotates `message: str` while todo/exceptions/team_exceptions.py:11, :16, :21 use a bare `message=...`; and NotTeamAdminException (team_exceptions.py:15-17) defaults to ApiErrors.UNAUTHORIZED_TITLE, a constant named as a title being used as a message body.
- **proposed:** Add todo/exceptions/base.py with two classes: `MessageException(Exception)` holding the shared `self.message` / `super().__init__` body, and `EntityNotFoundException(MessageException)` holding the guarded template logic. BaseAuthException, BaseTeamException and TaskStateConflictException become one-line subclasses of MessageException; TaskNotFoundException and UserNotFoundException become one-line subclasses of EntityNotFoundException declaring only their two constants, which is what fixes the unguarded format(). Keep the families as distinct subclasses -- the isinstance ladder in exception_handler.py depends on their identities and on BaseAuthException not being an ancestor of non-auth exceptions, so MessageException must stay a bare marker that nothing catches broadly (do not write `except MessageException`).

## 78. Constants shadowed inside their own class in constants/messages.py, plus a live reference to a member that does not exist
- **dimension:** exceptions | **severity:** medium | **effort:** small | **copies:** 6 | **~lines:** 6
- **sites:** todo/constants/messages.py:94, todo/constants/messages.py:99, todo/constants/messages.py:95, todo/constants/messages.py:101, todo/constants/messages.py:55, todo/constants/messages.py:84, todo/middlewares/jwt_auth.py:118
- **drift:** Confirmed, with one correction to the failure mode. `grep -rn 'INVALID_TOKEN' todo/` shows todo/middlewares/jwt_auth.py:118 is `raise TokenInvalidError(AuthErrorMessages.INVALID_TOKEN)` and messages.py defines no INVALID_TOKEN -- only TOKEN_INVALID (:95, :101) and INVALID_TOKEN_TITLE (:98). todo/utils/jwt_utils.py:73 and :93 use the correct TOKEN_INVALID name for the same exception, so the name split is already visible in the tree. The finder said this raises AttributeError instead of a 401; more precisely the AttributeError is raised from _set_user_data, propagates past the inner `except (TokenExpiredError, TokenInvalidError)` at jwt_auth.py:76-77, and is swallowed by the outer `except Exception: return False` at jwt_auth.py:83-84. The net effect is still a bug: instead of the specific TokenInvalidError that jwt_auth.py:81-82 would re-raise into _handle_auth_error, the user-row-missing path degrades to a generic "Authentication required" 401, so the real cause never reaches the client or the logs. Meanwhile auth_exceptions.py:16 and :26 silently take the :99/:101 wordings and the :94/:95 strings are unreachable from the moment the module is imported.
- **proposed:** Two cheap guards rather than the finder's StrEnum conversion, which would touch every comparison site for little gain. (1) Fix todo/middlewares/jwt_auth.py:118 to AuthErrorMessages.TOKEN_INVALID and delete the shadowed messages.py:94-95, keeping one wording per condition. (2) Add a single unit test under todo/tests/unit/constants/ that parses todo/constants/messages.py with `ast` and asserts no class body assigns the same name twice and no string value appears in two classes -- roughly 20 lines, catches both the shadowing and the ApiErrors/ValidationErrors.SEARCH_QUERY_EMPTY duplication, and needs no production change. Deciding which of the two TOKEN_EXPIRED/TOKEN_INVALID wordings survives is a product call, but the currently-live one (:99/:101) is the safe default since it is what ships today.

## 79. Message strings hardcoded inline that already exist as constants, one of which drifted by a trailing period
- **dimension:** exceptions | **severity:** medium | **effort:** small | **copies:** 9 | **~lines:** 9
- **sites:** todo/exceptions/global_exception_handler.py:33, todo/exceptions/global_exception_handler.py:67, todo/views/team_creation_invite_code.py:69, todo/views/team_creation_invite_code.py:184, todo/services/user_service.py:102, todo/services/task_service.py:129, todo/services/task_service.py:132, todo/services/task_service.py:135, todo/views/team.py:484
- **drift:** Confirmed, plus one site the finder missed. `grep -rn 'not authorized to perform' todo/` returns exactly three hits: ApiErrors.UNAUTHORIZED_TITLE = "You are not authorized to perform this action" (messages.py:52, no trailing period) and two literals ending in a period at todo/views/team_creation_invite_code.py:69 and :184. The constant is what todo/middlewares/team_access_middleware.py:44, todo/exceptions/exception_handler.py:206 and todo/services/team_service.py:535 return, so the same 403 renders two different strings depending on which endpoint refused. Missed site: todo/services/task_service.py:135 is a third literal duplicating ValidationErrors.MAX_LIMIT_EXCEEDED, so the whole _validate_pagination_params method is a hardcoded restatement of three existing constants. Also note the two team_creation_invite_code 403 blocks are themselves full copies -- :67-71 and :182-186 are byte-identical, each preceded by a byte-identical _check_authorization at :20-26 and :131-137.
- **proposed:** No new abstraction is needed for the literals -- point all nine sites at the existing todo/constants/messages.py symbols, picking the canonical wording for the 403 deliberately (and renaming ApiErrors.UNAUTHORIZED_TITLE to UNAUTHORIZED, since team_exceptions.py:16 and team_service.py:535 use it as a message body, not a title). The duplicated authorization gate does deserve an abstraction: lift _check_authorization plus its 403 response into a shared mixin or a small `require_admin_email(request) -> Response | None` in todo/utils/, used by both GenerateTeamCreationInviteCodeView and ListTeamCreationInviteCodesView. To stop the literals regressing, add an ast-based test that walks todo/**.py collecting ast.Constant strings and fails when one exactly matches a value defined in messages.py -- the same test harness as finding 9.

## 80. Three competing hardcoded "team not found" messages across two services and a view, none of them a constant and none typed as an exception
- **dimension:** exceptions | **severity:** medium | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 11 | **~lines:** 11
- **sites:** todo/services/team_service.py:246, todo/services/team_service.py:344, todo/services/team_service.py:405, todo/services/task_service.py:318, todo/services/task_service.py:401, todo/services/task_service.py:492, todo/services/task_service.py:611, todo/services/task_service.py:615, todo/services/task_assignment_service.py:37, todo/views/team.py:357, todo/views/team.py:403
- **drift:** Confirmed and worse than claimed. Four wordings now coexist for one condition -- "Team with id {id} not found" (team_service), "Team not found: {id}" (task_service, task_assignment_service), "Team not found." (views/team.py:357, :403) and the nested-class marker TeamService.TeamOrUserNotFound (team_service.py:481, raised at :491 and :508, caught by name at views/team.py:504 and rendered as the literal "Team or user not found.") -- and none matches the "... with ID {0} not found." punctuation of the two sibling constants. Because these are bare ValueErrors rather than a typed exception, handle_exception cannot map them: todo/views/team.py:171-177 compensates by catching bare ValueError and forcing a 404 with message=str(e), which means every genuine value error from get_team_by_id is also reported as a 404; the identical ValueErrors raised by task_service instead hit todo/views/task.py:151-167, fail the args[0] ApiErrorResponse test, and become a 500. Same missing team, 404 or 500 depending on the endpoint.
- **proposed:** Add ApiErrors.TEAM_NOT_FOUND = "Team with ID {0} not found." and TEAM_NOT_FOUND_GENERIC = "Team not found." to todo/constants/messages.py beside TASK_NOT_FOUND/USER_NOT_FOUND, and add TeamNotFoundException to todo/exceptions/team_exceptions.py built on the EntityNotFoundException base from finding 8. Replace all nine raise sites and fold TeamService.TeamOrUserNotFound (team_service.py:481) into it. Register one branch in handle_exception next to TaskNotFoundException (exception_handler.py:182-190) so team 404s stop depending on which view catches ValueError, then delete the compensating `except ValueError -> 404` at todo/views/team.py:171-177 and the by-name catch at todo/views/team.py:504. Sequencing matters: introduce and register the exception first, migrate raise sites, and only then remove views/team.py:171-177, otherwise genuine value errors there silently change status.

## 81. MISSED BY FINDER: the 401 envelope built three times in the JWT middleware, and a format template leaking as a user-facing message
- **dimension:** exceptions | **severity:** high | **effort:** small | **copies:** 3 | **~lines:** 35
- **sites:** todo/middlewares/jwt_auth.py:36-49, todo/middlewares/jwt_auth.py:54-67, todo/middlewares/jwt_auth.py:149-157
- **drift:** The one differing argument is a live bug. ApiErrors.AUTHENTICATION_FAILED is "Authentication failed: {0}" (todo/constants/messages.py:47) -- a format template. jwt_auth.py:56 passes it as `message` with no .format() call, so any request whose authentication trips the generic `except Exception` at :53 receives the literal body message "Authentication failed: {0}", placeholder and all, while the structurally identical block 18 lines above correctly uses AuthErrorMessages.AUTHENTICATION_REQUIRED ("Authentication required"). All three copies additionally pass the same raw template as `title`, so "{0}" leaks into every 401 this middleware emits. A fourth inconsistency across the boundary: none of the three sets authenticated=False, while the token branches in todo/exceptions/exception_handler.py:74-79 and :90-95 do -- so a 401 from the middleware and a 401 from the view layer have different response shapes.
- **proposed:** Add a private `_unauthorized(message: str, detail: str | None = None) -> JsonResponse` to JWTAuthenticationMiddleware in todo/middlewares/jwt_auth.py and route all three sites through it, so the envelope, the exclude_none dump and the authenticated=False flag are written once. Fix the title to a real title constant while collapsing them -- AuthErrorMessages.AUTHENTICATION_REQUIRED is the only non-template string available today; if a distinct title is wanted, add ApiErrors.AUTHENTICATION_FAILED_TITLE = "Authentication Failed" to messages.py rather than reusing the "Authentication failed: {0}" template. Longer term this helper and _auth_error_response from finding 1 are the same function and should live together in the shared error_response_utils module, since the middleware and the DRF handler are currently emitting two different 401 shapes for the same condition.

## 82. The dual-write ritual (build snake_case payload -> new EnhancedDualWriteService() -> create/update/delete_document -> function-local `import logging` guard) is pasted into 21 write paths
- **dimension:** dualwrite | **severity:** high | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 21 | **~lines:** 340
- **sites:** todo/repositories/team_repository.py:27-48, todo/repositories/team_repository.py:142-161, todo/repositories/team_repository.py:186-206, todo/repositories/team_repository.py:296-315, todo/repositories/task_repository.py:225-251, todo/repositories/task_repository.py:323-348, todo/repositories/task_assignment_repository.py:27-48, todo/repositories/task_assignment_repository.py:137-158, todo/repositories/task_assignment_repository.py:209-230, todo/repositories/task_assignment_repository.py:272-293, todo/repositories/task_assignment_repository.py:334-355, todo/repositories/task_assignment_repository.py:464-518, todo/repositories/watchlist_repository.py:44-63, todo/repositories/watchlist_repository.py:349-368, todo/repositories/user_role_repository.py:43-62, todo/repositories/user_role_repository.py:144-163, todo/repositories/user_repository.py:73-91, todo/repositories/audit_log_repository.py:18-42, todo/repositories/user_team_details_repository.py:56-65, todo/repositories/team_creation_invite_code_repository.py:39-58, todo/repositories/team_creation_invite_code_repository.py:73-92, todo/repositories/task_repository.py:403-406
- **drift:** All four drift claims verified, with two line-number corrections.
1) CONFIRMED and it is the sharpest signal: todo/repositories/user_role_repository.py already has `import logging` at line 3 and `logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)` at line 11, yet lines 59-62 and 160-163 still do `import logging` / `logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)` inside the guard. The block was pasted in without anyone reading the top of the file.
2) CONFIRMED, line numbers off by one: create writes `"createdAt": task.createdAt or datetime.now(timezone.utc)` at todo/repositories/task_repository.py:237 (not 238); update writes `"createdAt": task_model.createdAt` at :334 (not 335). The null-guard was added to one copy only.
3) CONFIRMED and it is a live bug. Neither task payload (task_repository.py:226-239, :324-338) contains a `labels` key, but `_transform_task_data` reads `"labels": data.get("labels", [])` at todo/services/dual_write_service.py:263 and `update_document` calls `self._sync_task_labels(postgres_instance, labels)` unconditionally at todo/services/dual_write_service.py:129-130, where `_sync_task_labels` opens with `PostgresTaskLabel.objects.filter(task=postgres_task).delete()` (line 346). Every task update therefore empties `postgres_task_labels` for that task.
4) CONFIRMED, line numbers corrected to todo/repositories/task_assignment_repository.py:496-512: the task payload in the batch spells `"updatedAt": datetime.now(timezone.utc)` (line 511) next to `"updated_by": str(performed_by_user_id)` (line 512) — one camelCase key and one snake_case key in one dict; `_transform_task_data` reads `data.get("updatedBy")` (dual_write_service.py:262), so the updater is silently dropped.
5) CONFIRMED: todo/repositories/team_repository.py:142-161 and :186-206 are the same block, identical apart from indentation and the enclosing `for user_team in user_teams:` at line 187 — payload 143-150 vs 188-195, call 153-155 vs 198-200, guard 158-161 vs 203-206.
Also: `EnhancedDualWriteService()` is constructed 21 times, so `self.sync_failures` (dual_write_service.py:46) is discarded after every call — `get_sync_metrics`/`retry_failed_sync`, which read it, can never see anything.
- **proposed:** New module todo/repositories/common/dual_write.py (sits beside the existing todo/repositories/common/mongo_repository.py) exporting one function with a module-level logger and a single process-wide service instance:

  sync_to_postgres(collection_name: str, mongo_id: str, payload: Mapping[str, Any] | None = None, *, operation: str = "create", action: str = "") -> bool

Contract preserved exactly: it never raises, it logs a warning and returns False on failure, so Mongo writes that currently succeed regardless keep doing so — that matters for the three irregular sites (inside a pymongo session at task_repository.py:225 and task_assignment_repository.py:464, the delete at user_team_details_repository.py:56, and the per-item loop at team_repository.py:186, where a single failure must not abort the batch). Every call site collapses from ~20 lines to one. Land it in two commits: introduce the helper and migrate the 18 uniform sites first, then the three irregular ones.

## 83. EnhancedDualWriteService repeats the same 5-line `if not self.enabled` guard four times to bolt a feature flag onto a base class written without one
- **dimension:** dualwrite | **severity:** medium | **effort:** small | **copies:** 4 | **~lines:** 35
- **sites:** todo/services/enhanced_dual_write_service.py:20-28, todo/services/enhanced_dual_write_service.py:30-38, todo/services/enhanced_dual_write_service.py:40-48, todo/services/enhanced_dual_write_service.py:50-58, todo/services/enhanced_dual_write_service.py:106-109
- **drift:** `_batch_operations_sync` calls `super().create_document` / `super().update_document` / `super().delete_document` at todo/services/enhanced_dual_write_service.py:73, 75, 77 rather than `self.` — it deliberately routes around its own class's overrides, which is only coherent if the flag lives on the base. That is the signature of a subclass bolted onto code already written without a flag. Confirmed dead: `grep` over the whole repo finds zero external callers of `get_sync_status` (:95), `get_sync_metrics` (:119), `retry_failed_sync` (:146), and zero for the base's `get_sync_failures` (dual_write_service.py:494) and `clear_sync_failures` (:498). `get_sync_metrics` and `retry_failed_sync` read `self.sync_failures`, which can never be non-empty because all 21 call sites build a throwaway instance.
TWO CORRECTIONS to the candidate: (a) the claim that todo/services/postgres_sync_service.py:20 'silently ignores DUAL_WRITE_ENABLED' is true of the instantiation but has no consequence — PostgresSyncService only ever touches `self.dual_write_service._transform_label_data` (:161) and `._transform_role_data` (:212), never a flag-gated method, and it has its own POSTGRES_SYNC_ENABLED gate at :21/:30. (b) The 'two independent failure-bookkeeping APIs' observation is dead code and a design smell, not near-identical duplication; I am not counting it as duplicated lines.
- **proposed:** Move `self.enabled = getattr(settings, "DUAL_WRITE_ENABLED", True)` into `DualWriteService.__init__` (todo/services/dual_write_service.py:45-46) and put the single guard at the top of the shared `_write` envelope proposed in the create/update/delete finding, so it is checked once. Move `batch_operations`/`_batch_operations_sync` down into the base too (rebinding its `super().x_document` calls to `self._write`). `todo/services/enhanced_dual_write_service.py` then holds nothing, so keep the import path alive for one release with a module containing only `EnhancedDualWriteService = DualWriteService`, then repoint the 10 importing modules and delete it. Note the behaviour change to guard: under DUAL_WRITE_ENABLED=False (todo_project/settings/test.py:3) `PostgresSyncService` would newly short-circuit if it ever calls a gated method — it does not today, but the conversion should not add such a call.

## 84. Eleven hand-written `_transform_<entity>_data` mappers plus an 11-branch if/elif that re-encodes routing already in COLLECTION_MODEL_MAP
- **dimension:** dualwrite | **severity:** high | **effort:** large | **copies:** 11 | **~lines:** 200

            "role_name": user_role.role_name.value if hasattr(user_role.role_name, "value") else user_role.role_name,

- Key casing was never unified between neighbours: `_transform_label_data` reads `data.get("createdAt")` / `data.get("updatedAt")` at :297-298 while `_transform_role_data` reads `data.get("created_at")` / `data.get("updated_at")` at :307-308.
- `_transform_user_role_data` (:410) and `_transform_audit_log_data` (:421) are the only two with no docstring — later additions pasted onto the pile.
- Create and update disagree about what a mapper typo does: `create_document` passes the dict straight to `objects.create(**postgres_data)` (:76) so it raises, while `update_document` filters with `if hasattr(postgres_instance, field) and field not in preserve_fields` (:121) so it silently ignores.
- **proposed:** New module todo/services/sync_schema.py holding two shared casts and one declarative table, replacing all eleven methods and the 25-line if/elif. `DualWriteService._transform_data_for_postgres` (todo/services/dual_write_service.py:189-235) collapses to `return transform(collection_name, data, mongo_id)`. Sequence the work so the risky parts stay explicit: (1) introduce `objectid_str` and `enum_value` and use them inside the existing eleven methods — this alone forces the 15-vs-14 empty-string/None split to be decided once; (2) convert the ten mechanical entities to FIELD_MAPS; (3) leave `tasks` as a hand-written entry with an `extra=` hook for the synthetic `labels` key that create/update pop back out at :74 and :115, and keep `_transform_generic_data` (:451-468) as the unmapped-collection fallback since its 24-character ObjectId heuristic has no declarative equivalent. Every entity's map should be diffed against its Postgres model's fields before the switch, because `objects.create` will start raising where a per-entity default used to paper over a missing Mongo field.

## 85. create_document / update_document / delete_document repeat the same model-lookup / transaction / log / record-failure envelope, and the label rule drifted between the create and update copies
- **dimension:** dualwrite | **severity:** high | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 3 | **~lines:** 75
- **sites:** todo/services/dual_write_service.py:48-89, todo/services/dual_write_service.py:91-142, todo/services/dual_write_service.py:144-183, todo/services/enhanced_dual_write_service.py:106-109
- **drift:** Label handling drifted between the two copies and it is a live data-loss bug. todo/services/dual_write_service.py:78-80 (create):
                # Handle labels for tasks
                if collection_name == "tasks" and labels:
                    self._sync_task_labels(postgres_instance, labels)
vs todo/services/dual_write_service.py:128-130 (update):
                # Handle labels for tasks
                if collection_name == "tasks":
                    self._sync_task_labels(postgres_labels := postgres_instance, labels)
(update's guard omits `and labels`), and `_sync_task_labels` opens with `PostgresTaskLabel.objects.filter(task=postgres_task).delete()` at :346. So an update that omits labels wipes the junction table; a create that omits them leaves it alone.
Recovery for the same missing-row condition diverged three ways: update falls back to `return self.create_document(...)` at :137, delete logs a warning and returns True at :177-178, create has no `DoesNotExist` handler at all.
`sync_status`/`sync_error` are reset explicitly in update (:124-125) and in the soft-delete branch of delete (:166-167) but nowhere in create, which relies on the transform dict at :202-206 instead.
ONE CORRECTION: the claimed NameError flaw in `except postgres_model.DoesNotExist` (:135, :176) is real in form but practically unreachable — `_get_postgres_model` (:185-187) is a bare `dict.get` that cannot raise, and the `if not postgres_model: return False` guard means the name is always bound by the time any later exception propagates. Worth tidying with the refactor, but it is not currently producing NameErrors.
- **proposed:** One private `_write(self, verb, collection_name, mongo_id, apply, data=None) -> bool` on DualWriteService owning the flag check, model lookup, `transaction.atomic()`, success logging and `_record_sync_failure`, with the mutate step passed in as `_apply_create` / `_apply_update` / `_apply_delete`. Hoist the model guard into `_require_model(collection_name)` raising a typed `UnknownCollection`, so the subclass's drifted fourth copy at enhanced_dual_write_service.py:107-109 can call it too. Give the task-labels rule one home — `_apply_task_labels(instance, labels, *, present)` where `present` says whether the payload carried a labels key — so create and update cannot disagree again. Keep the three public methods and their differing contracts (delete returns True for an absent row, update quietly creates one): those `DoesNotExist` fallbacks must sit in the per-operation wrappers, outside the shared `transaction.atomic()`, or the recovery write runs inside a broken transaction.

## 86. PostgresSyncService._sync_labels_table and _sync_roles_table are the same backfill function written twice, with the soft-delete filter hardcoded in two places per entity
- **dimension:** dualwrite | **severity:** medium | **effort:** small | **copies:** 2 | **~lines:** 95
- **sites:** todo/services/postgres_sync_service.py:128-180, todo/services/postgres_sync_service.py:182-228, todo/services/postgres_sync_service.py:99-104
- **drift:** The soft-delete filter is written twice per entity and the two copies disagree: labels queries `collection.find({"isDeleted": {"$ne": True}})` at :148, roles queries `collection.find({})` at :201 — and the same rule is hardcoded a second time inside the supposedly generic counter at :99-104 as `if collection_name == "labels": return collection.count_documents({"isDeleted": {"$ne": True}}) else: return collection.count_documents({})`. A per-entity rule leaking into shared code is exactly what the copy-per-entity shape forces.
The labels copy also gained two logging lines the roles copy never got: `logger.info(f"Will sync {mongo_count - postgres_count} labels to PostgreSQL")` at :144 and `logger.debug(f"Creating label in PostgreSQL: {postgres_data}")` at :165.
Both copies reach through into another service's privates (`self.dual_write_service._transform_label_data` at :161, `._transform_role_data` at :212) and both re-import their model on every loop iteration (:154, :206) instead of at module scope.
- **proposed:** A frozen `SyncSpec` dataclass plus one `_sync_collection(self, spec) -> bool` in todo/services/postgres_sync_service.py, and a module-level `SPECS` list that `sync_all_tables` (:37-40) iterates. The Mongo filter then lives once per entity instead of twice, and `_get_mongo_collection_count` loses its `if collection_name == "labels"` branch by taking `spec.mongo_filter`. Point `spec.transform` at the public `transform(collection, data, mongo_id)` from the sync_schema finding rather than at `dual_write_service._transform_*`, which removes the private-access reach-through at the same time; move the model imports to module scope. Note the count-based short-circuit `postgres_count >= mongo_count` is wrong in general (it skips a sync whenever Postgres has stale extra rows) — unifying it makes one shared bug instead of two, which is only an improvement if it is fixed in the same commit; a `spec.force` flag would also finally make the `--force` argument at todo/management/commands/sync_postgres_tables.py:9-13 mean something.

## 87. Idempotent 'seed fixed rows into Mongo' loop written three times, and the validate-then-insert step drifted so labels bypass every pydantic alias that roles get
- **dimension:** dualwrite | **severity:** medium | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 3 | **~lines:** 110
- **sites:** todo_project/db/migrations.py:75-132, todo_project/db/migrations.py:161-206, todo/management/commands/migrate_add_creator_to_teams.py:10-35
- **drift:** The validation step diverged in a way that changes what lands in Mongo. Labels construct the model and throw it away, inserting the raw dict — todo_project/db/migrations.py:105-111:
                try:
                    LabelModel(**label_document)
                except Exception as validation_error:
                    logger.error(f"Label validation failed for '{label_data['name']}': {validation_error}")
                    continue

                result = labels_collection.insert_one(label_document)
Roles insert the validated dump — todo_project/db/migrations.py:189-192:
                validated_role = RoleModel(**role_doc)
                validated_doc = validated_role.model_dump(mode="json", by_alias=True, exclude_none=True)

                result = roles_collection.insert_one(validated_doc)
So labels skip every alias and coercion the model defines while roles get them.
Error containment drifted: labels wrap each item's whole body in try/except-continue (:84, :119-121), roles leave the `find_one` outside the try (:170), so one bad existence probe aborts the entire roles migration.
Labels log a failure when `inserted_id` is falsy (:117); roles have no `else` branch (:193-195) and silently under-count.
Labels report `{total_labels} total` in the summary (:123-126); roles omit it (:201).
The third copy has none of the containment: no try/except, no skipped counter, no per-item validation, and `role_id="1"` as a bare literal at migrate_add_creator_to_teams.py:28.
- **proposed:** One `seed_collection(collection_name, rows, *, key_query, model, label) -> tuple[int, int]` in todo_project/db/migrations.py owning the existence probe, the validate-then-`model_dump` insert, the per-row try/except, the counters and the summary log. `migrate_fixed_labels` and `migrate_predefined_roles` shrink to their data literal plus a one-line `key_query` lambda; `migrate_add_creator_to_teams` becomes a caller instead of a fourth dialect. The honest caveat the finder raised holds: `key_query` carries the genuinely per-entity logic (case-insensitive name for labels, name+scope for roles, team_id+user_id+is_active for team creators), so the helper earns its keep on containment and the insert path rather than on the probe. Switching labels to `model_dump(by_alias=True)` to match roles is a behaviour change to seeded documents and must be checked against how todo/repositories/label_repository.py reads them — land that as its own commit, after the extraction.

## 88. migrate_labels and migrate_roles are the same command twice over the same argument-less runner, styled in one copy and not the other
- **dimension:** dualwrite | **severity:** medium | **effort:** small | **copies:** 3 | **~lines:** 40
- **sites:** todo/management/commands/migrate_labels.py:5-16, todo/management/commands/migrate_roles.py:5-16, todo/management/commands/sync_postgres_tables.py:15-31
- **drift:** Same output, different formatting, for identical work: migrate_labels wraps all three messages in `self.style.SUCCESS(...)` / `self.style.ERROR(...)` (todo/management/commands/migrate_labels.py:9, 14, 15) while migrate_roles writes them bare (todo/management/commands/migrate_roles.py:9, 14, 15) — the styling was added to one copy and never back-ported. Only sync_postgres_tables has the `except Exception -> self.style.ERROR` wrapper (:30-31); the other two crash with a traceback.
sync_postgres_tables declares a `--force` flag at :9-13 and merely prints "Force sync enabled - will sync all tables regardless of existing data" at :21-22 without passing it anywhere: `sync_all_tables()` takes no arguments (todo/services/postgres_sync_service.py:23) and unconditionally short-circuits on `postgres_count >= mongo_count` (:139, :193). The flag is inert.
- **proposed:** A `BaseRunnerCommand` in a new todo/management/commands/_base.py (leading underscore keeps Django from registering it as a command) holding `handle()` — styled start/success/failure plus the try/except wrapper that only sync_postgres_tables currently has — with subclasses supplying a `runner` staticmethod and a `label`. Safe ordering, because these names are deploy/runbook surface: first give `run_all_migrations(only=[...])` a real selector so the two names mean different things, then reduce migrate_labels/migrate_roles to `runner = partial(run_all_migrations, only=["labels"])` etc., and wire `--force` through to `sync_all_tables(force=...)`. Do not rename or delete either command in the same change — a Dockerfile or CI step may invoke it. Note that adding the missing try/except changes migrate_* exit behaviour from crash-with-traceback to a logged failure line.

## 89. run_all_migrations and sync_all_tables are the same (name, callable) runner loop written twice in two packages
- **dimension:** dualwrite | **severity:** low | **effort:** small | **copies:** 2 | **~lines:** 35
- **sites:** todo_project/db/migrations.py:218-239, todo/services/postgres_sync_service.py:37-57
- **drift:** The drift here is cosmetic, which is why I am scoring this low: `sync_all_tables` computes `total_operations` before the loop (postgres_sync_service.py:43) while `run_all_migrations` computes `total_migrations` after it (migrations.py:236). Only `sync_all_tables` has a disabled-flag early return (:30-32); `run_all_migrations` has no equivalent, so nothing can turn the Mongo seed migrations off — a real gap, but an absence rather than a divergence.
I REJECT the third claimed site, todo/services/enhanced_dual_write_service.py:60-93. `_batch_operations_sync` is not the same shape: it destructures op dicts (`op["collection_name"]`, `op["operation"]` at :67-70), dispatches on an operation string with an unknown-operation branch (:72-81) that the other two have no analogue for, tracks `failure_count` as well as `success_count`, and returns `failure_count == 0`. Forcing it into a `(name, callable)` helper would require wrapping every operation in a lambda and would not carry the dispatch — the abstraction would need per-caller branching to serve it.
- **proposed:** A small `run_steps(steps, *, what, logger) -> bool` in a new todo/utils/steps.py (the package already exists: invite_code_utils.py, jwt_utils.py, task_validation_utils.py). Both callers become a one-line `return run_steps(...)`. Value is modest — two copies of a 15-line loop, and the two sites log with different vocabulary that operators may grep for, so parameterise the phrasing via `what` and accept that individual step lines become uniform. Worth doing opportunistically when either file is next touched rather than as a standalone change; the payoff is that a disabled-flag early return and a consistent success/total accounting can then be added in one place.

## 90. The 12-line sync-metadata field block is pasted into all 11 Postgres models, and the created_at/updated_at save() override into 9 of them — the two that missed the paste are the only two that store the right timestamps
- **dimension:** dualwrite | **severity:** high | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 11 | **~lines:** 175
- **sites:** todo/models/postgres/user.py:24-35, todo/models/postgres/task.py:37-48, todo/models/postgres/team.py:31-42, todo/models/postgres/team.py:84-95, todo/models/postgres/label.py:22-33, todo/models/postgres/role.py:22-33, todo/models/postgres/task_assignment.py:30-41, todo/models/postgres/watchlist.py:25-36, todo/models/postgres/user_role.py:16-26, todo/models/postgres/audit_log.py:21-31, todo/models/postgres/team_creation_invite_code.py:27-38, todo/models/postgres/user.py:49-53, todo/models/postgres/label.py:46-50, todo/models/postgres/role.py:46-50, todo/models/postgres/task.py:65-69, todo/models/postgres/team.py:56-60, todo/models/postgres/team.py:111-115, todo/models/postgres/task_assignment.py:58-62, todo/models/postgres/watchlist.py:55-59, todo/models/postgres/team_creation_invite_code.py:53-56
- **drift:** Three drifts, all confirmed by reading every model file end to end.
1) Two of eleven never received the save() override: todo/models/postgres/user_role.py ends at line 48 with `__str__`, and todo/models/postgres/audit_log.py ends at line 46 with `__str__`. So those two persist whatever `created_at` the dual-write hands them, while the other nine overwrite it.
2) A third variant exists: todo/models/postgres/team_creation_invite_code.py:53-56 is the same override minus the `self.updated_at = timezone.now()` line (the model has no updated_at field).
3) The consequence of the majority variant is one bug repeated nine times: `_transform_*_data` explicitly forwards Mongo's timestamps (`"created_at": data.get("created_at"), "updated_at": data.get("updated_at")` at todo/services/dual_write_service.py:276-277, and :259-260 for tasks) and `create_document` passes them into `objects.create(**postgres_data)` at :76 — then save() stamps `timezone.now()` over both. Nine tables hold sync time; the two that skipped the paste hold source time.
4) Field defaults drifted for the same column: todo/models/postgres/team.py:29 and :82 declare `updated_at = models.DateTimeField(default=timezone.now)` while user.py:22, task.py:31, label.py:20, role.py:20, task_assignment.py:24 and watchlist.py:23 all use `models.DateTimeField(null=True, blank=True)`.
- **proposed:** An abstract `SyncedModel(models.Model)` in a new todo/models/postgres/base.py carrying `mongo_id`, `created_at`, `updated_at`, `last_sync_at`, `sync_status` (choices hoisted to a `SyncStatus` constant in todo/constants/, since the same three-tuple list is written 11 times and the same literal `"SYNCED"` appears at dual_write_service.py:124, :166, :204 and postgres_sync_service.py:163, :214), `sync_error`, `Meta.abstract = True` and `indexes = [Index("mongo_id"), Index("sync_status")]`. Each concrete model then declares only its business fields. Two things to get right: Django requires `class Meta(SyncedModel.Meta)` spelled explicitly or the base's indexes are silently dropped — an easy way to lose all 11 index sets in one commit; and the corrected save() (stamp only when the value is not already supplied) is a behaviour change to nine tables, so land the base class as a pure no-op refactor first, verify `makemigrations` produces an empty diff, and change save() in a separate, reviewed commit.

## 91. BasePostgresRepository subclasses re-paste get_by_user/get_by_team/get_by_<field> accessors the base already provides, and five of them name columns that do not exist on their models
- **dimension:** dualwrite | **severity:** low | **effort:** small | **copies:** 6 | **~lines:** 50
- **sites:** todo/repositories/postgres_repository.py:114-126, todo/repositories/postgres_repository.py:183-188, todo/repositories/postgres_repository.py:206-211, todo/repositories/postgres_repository.py:220-225, todo/repositories/postgres_repository.py:234-244, todo/repositories/postgres_repository.py:253-255, todo/repositories/postgres_repository.py:264-270, todo/repositories/postgres_repository.py:279-285, todo/repositories/postgres_repository.py:294-300
- **drift:** Five pasted accessors name columns that do not exist on their models — only possible because the line was copied, never written against the model. `grep -rn 'user_mongo_id\|team_mongo_id'` outside dual_write_service.py returns exactly these six lines and nothing else in the repo:
  postgres_repository.py:240 `PostgresTaskAssignment.objects.filter(user_mongo_id=user_id)` — the model declares `assignee_id` (todo/models/postgres/task_assignment.py:15)
  postgres_repository.py:244 `...filter(team_mongo_id=team_id)` — the model declares `team_id` (task_assignment.py:17)
  postgres_repository.py:255 `PostgresWatchlist.objects.filter(user_mongo_id=user_id)` — the model declares `user_id` (watchlist.py:16)
  postgres_repository.py:266/:270 `PostgresUserRole.objects.filter(user_mongo_id=...)` / `(team_mongo_id=...)` — the model declares `user_id`/`team_id` (user_role.py:8, :11)
  postgres_repository.py:296 `PostgresAuditLog.objects.filter(user_mongo_id=...)` and :300 `filter(collection_name=...)` — neither name appears anywhere in todo/models/postgres/audit_log.py
Only PostgresUserTeamDetailsRepository (:281, :285) uses the right names — the one place the pasted line happened to match. That dead `user_mongo_id`/`team_mongo_id`/`status`/`assigned_at` vocabulary is the same schema the unreferenced `_sync_task_assignment_update` writes to (todo/services/dual_write_service.py:372-390), so both were copied from a pre-rename generation of the models. Scored low because grep finds zero callers of any of these classes outside their own definitions — the drift is severe but the code is dead.
- **proposed:** Check for callers first (there are none today), then delete rather than extract: `get_all(filters={"assignee_id": uid})` already does the job.
