Where named accessors are wanted, declare the field as data on the subclass and put single implementations on `BasePostgresRepository`, so a wrong name is caught in one place instead of pasted into six. Note the failure modes differ and must be reconciled deliberately: the base's `_apply_filters` guards with `if hasattr(self.model_class, field)` (:90) and silently ignores an unknown field — returning everything — whereas today's `objects.filter(user_mongo_id=...)` raises FieldError. Routing through the base as-is would convert five loud errors into five silent wrong answers, so make `_apply_filters` raise on an unknown field in the same change.

## 92. The task-assignment deactivation payload is built four times inside one file, reported under four different log names, and one copy syncs a single row for a Mongo update_many
- **dimension:** dualwrite | **severity:** high | **effort:** small | **copies:** 4 | **~lines:** 85
- **sites:** todo/repositories/task_assignment_repository.py:137-158, todo/repositories/task_assignment_repository.py:209-230, todo/repositories/task_assignment_repository.py:272-293, todo/repositories/task_assignment_repository.py:334-355, todo/services/dual_write_service.py:360-396
- **drift:** Every drift claim confirmed.
1) One operation, four names in the logs: "task assignment deactivation" (:158), "task assignment deletion" (:230), "task assignment update" (:293), "task assignment deactivation" again (:355). No single grep finds all deactivation sync failures.
2) The update_executor copy silently changes three values mid-paste — `"assignee_id": str(executor_id)` (:275), `"user_type": "user"` (:276), `"is_active": current_assignment.is_active` (:278) instead of the hardcoded `False` the other three use. Correct for that call site, but invisible against three near-identical neighbours.
3) The deactivate_by_task_id copy reads a variable named `active_assignments` (:336-344) that holds a single model — it comes from `cls.get_by_task_id(task_id)` at :313, which does `collection.find_one(...)` — while the surrounding Mongo write is `collection.update_many(...)` at :319-328. A multi-row Mongo deactivation syncs exactly one row to Postgres.
4) A fifth, stranded implementation of the same deactivate-then-recreate flow sits unreferenced at todo/services/dual_write_service.py:360-396, written against fields the model no longer has: `PostgresTaskAssignment.objects.filter(task_mongo_id=...).update(status="REJECTED", ...)` at :371-374 and `objects.create(... user_mongo_id=..., team_mongo_id=..., status=..., assigned_at=..., assigned_by=...)` at :377-390, none of which exist on todo/models/postgres/task_assignment.py. grep finds zero callers.
- **proposed:** One classmethod on TaskAssignmentRepository built on the `sync_to_postgres` helper from the first finding, so the payload exists once and the log message is derived from a single `action` argument. The four call sites become one line each; `update_executor` passes its two overrides explicitly, which makes the difference visible instead of buried in a near-identical neighbour. Two follow-ups that are behaviour changes, not refactors, and should land as their own commits: (a) fix deactivate_by_task_id to loop over every matched assignment — that changes Postgres row counts; (b) delete `_sync_task_assignment_update` (todo/services/dual_write_service.py:360-396) rather than repairing it, since nothing calls it and its field names predate the model. Keep the helper non-raising: all four sites sit inside broad `except Exception: return False` handlers (:233-234, :296-297, :358-359), so a helper that raises would flip those methods from returning False to propagating.

## 93. MISSED BY THE FINDER: for 8 of the 11 entities the repository builds a payload that _transform_<entity>_data then rebuilds key-for-key — the same field mapping written twice, in two layers
- **dimension:** dualwrite | **severity:** high | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 8 | **~lines:** 65
- **sites:** todo/repositories/team_repository.py:28-37, todo/services/dual_write_service.py:266-278, todo/repositories/task_assignment_repository.py:28-37, todo/services/dual_write_service.py:311-323, todo/repositories/watchlist_repository.py:45-53, todo/services/dual_write_service.py:325-335, todo/repositories/user_repository.py:74-80, todo/services/dual_write_service.py:280-289, todo/repositories/team_repository.py:143-150, todo/services/dual_write_service.py:398-408, todo/repositories/user_role_repository.py:44-51, todo/services/dual_write_service.py:410-419, todo/repositories/audit_log_repository.py:19-32, todo/services/dual_write_service.py:421-437, todo/repositories/team_creation_invite_code_repository.py:74-82, todo/services/dual_write_service.py:439-449
- **drift:** Because the mapping exists twice, the two copies disagree about which layer normalises what — and the answer is different per entity, which is how you can tell it was never decided.
- Enum unwrapping for user_roles happens in the repository (todo/repositories/user_role_repository.py:46-47, `user_role.role_name.value if hasattr(user_role.role_name, "value")`) and NOT in the mapper (todo/services/dual_write_service.py:413-414 just does `data.get("role_name")`). For tasks it is the opposite: the mapper unwraps (dual_write_service.py:240-247) and the repository does not (task_repository.py:229-230 passes `task.priority` / `task.status` straight through).
- ObjectId stringification for user_roles happens in the mapper only — the repository passes `user_role.user_id` and `user_role.team_id` raw (:45, :48) while the mapper wraps them (:412, :415). For teams both layers do it (team_repository.py:32-34 and dual_write_service.py:272-274), so `str()` runs twice on the same value.
- The double mapping is what let the two task payloads drift apart at all: had the repository handed over the model, `"createdAt": task.createdAt or datetime.now(timezone.utc)` (task_repository.py:237) vs `"createdAt": task_model.createdAt` (:334) could not exist as two spellings, nor could the missing `labels` key that makes every task update wipe postgres_task_labels.
- **proposed:** Fold the repository-side dict into the same FIELD_MAPS table the mapper uses (see the sync_schema finding), so each entity's mapping is written exactly once and the repository hands over the model. Add `payload_for(collection_name, model)` to todo/services/sync_schema.py, which dumps the pydantic model and runs it through the entity's map; `sync_to_postgres` (first finding) accepts a model directly and calls it. That deletes ~65 lines of repository dict literals and, more importantly, removes the layer ambiguity: `enum_value` and `objectid_str` are declared once per field in the map, so neither layer can decide differently. Migrate the 8 identity entities first — they are mechanical. Leave `tasks` last and handle it deliberately, since its map is the only one that does real camelCase translation and carries the synthetic `labels` key.

## 94. Auth-error branches duplicated inside handle_exception; the second copy of each pair is unreachable dead code that has drifted from the live one
- **dimension:** auth_utils | **severity:** high | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 8 | **~lines:** 60
- **sites:** todo/exceptions/exception_handler.py:56-64, todo/exceptions/exception_handler.py:65-80, todo/exceptions/exception_handler.py:81-96, todo/exceptions/exception_handler.py:98-113, todo/exceptions/exception_handler.py:114-129, todo/exceptions/exception_handler.py:130-145, todo/exceptions/exception_handler.py:173-181, todo/exceptions/exception_handler.py:192-200
- **drift:** Real, and it is exactly the accidental kind. (a) TokenExpiredError hits the live branch at :56-64, which only appends to error_list and falls through to the generic tail at :289-295 -- so an expired-token 401 omits `authenticated` entirely, while a missing-token (:65-80) or invalid-token (:81-96) 401 carries `authenticated: false`. Same status, same layer, different envelope. (b) The dead UserNotFoundException copy at :192-200 uses the path-aware `source={ApiErrorSource.PATH: "user_id"} if user_id else None`, i.e. someone fixed the source in a branch that can never execute, while the live one at :173-181 still hardcodes `{ApiErrorSource.PARAMETER: "user_id"}`. Compare :182-190 (TaskNotFoundException), which already uses the path-aware form -- confirming the fix was propagated to the task branch and to a dead user branch, but not to the live user branch.
- **proposed:** In todo/exceptions/exception_handler.py, replace the six auth branches (:56-145) with one table plus one builder.

Add near the top of the module:
  AuthErrorSpec = namedtuple("AuthErrorSpec", "status_code title source_key")
  AUTH_ERROR_SPECS: dict[type[Exception], AuthErrorSpec] = {...}
  def _auth_error_response(exc, spec) -> Response

handle_exception then does a single dict lookup before the remaining chain. Deleting :98-145 is a pure no-op today (dead code). The behavioural decision to make once: whether TokenExpiredError also returns `authenticated=False` -- the table makes it a one-line answer instead of six edits. Ship the deletion of the dead branches first (zero observable change), then the `authenticated` unification as a separate, announced commit, because todo/tests/unit/exceptions/test_exception_handler.py and clients may key on the current inconsistency.

Call-site change: six 16-line elif blocks become zero lines plus four dict entries.

## 95. Auth-cookie attribute config built twice with a disagreeing httponly flag, and cleared with a third, mismatched attribute set
- **dimension:** auth_utils | **severity:** high | **effort:** small | **copies:** 3 | **~lines:** 35
- **sites:** todo/middlewares/jwt_auth.py:135-143, todo/views/auth.py:135-142, todo/middlewares/jwt_auth.py:123-133, todo/views/auth.py:144-157, todo/views/auth.py:187-194
- **drift:** Three verified disagreements over one cookie. (1) httponly: jwt_auth.py:141 hardcodes `True`; auth.py:140 reads `settings.COOKIE_SETTINGS.get("COOKIE_HTTPONLY")` (defined at todo_project/settings/base.py:150 as env-driven). Set COOKIE_HTTPONLY=False and the login-issued cookie is JS-readable while the silently-refreshed replacement for the SAME cookie name is not -- the flag flips mid-session. (2) `COOKIE_SETTINGS["COOKIE_PATH"]` is defined at todo_project/settings/base.py:152 and `grep -rn COOKIE_PATH` finds no reader anywhere -- both copies hardcode `"/"`, so the setting is inert. (3) The delete config omits `secure`, `httponly` and `samesite`, so the clearing cookie's attributes do not match the `SameSite=Strict; Secure` cookie that was set. (4) max_age for the same access cookie is sourced two ways: `settings.JWT_CONFIG["ACCESS_TOKEN_LIFETIME"]` at jwt_auth.py:104/130 vs `tokens["expires_in"]` at auth.py:149 (which jwt_utils.py:112 defines as that same setting -- an indirection only one copy knows about).
- **proposed:** New module todo/utils/cookie_utils.py owning all three operations, so the attribute set is written once and the clear provably matches the set:

  def auth_cookie_kwargs() -> dict
  def set_access_cookie(response, token, max_age=None) -> None
  def set_refresh_cookie(response, token) -> None
  def clear_auth_cookies(response) -> None

`auth_cookie_kwargs` is the single reader of COOKIE_PATH/COOKIE_DOMAIN/COOKIE_SECURE/COOKIE_HTTPONLY/COOKIE_SAMESITE. `clear_auth_cookies` derives its kwargs from the same function minus max_age, which is what makes the delete-header mismatch structurally impossible rather than a thing to remember.

Call-site changes: jwt_auth.py deletes `_get_cookie_config` (:135-143) and `_process_response`'s inline set_cookie (:126-132) becomes one call. auth.py deletes `_get_cookie_config` (:135-142), `_set_auth_cookies` (:144-157) and the auth half of `_clear_auth_cookies` (:187-194). Note the middleware picks up settings-driven httponly as a behaviour change; CI sets COOKIE_HTTPONLY="True" (.env.example:21) so it is a no-op there, but it should be called out.

## 96. JWT_CONFIG written out twice under TESTING, and the two copies read different env var names for the refresh lifetime
- **dimension:** auth_utils | **severity:** high | **effort:** small | **copies:** 2 | **~lines:** 15
- **sites:** todo_project/settings/base.py:129-135, todo_project/settings/base.py:137-143
- **drift:** Confirmed and it reaches production. Line 134 reads `REFRESH_LIFETIME`; line 142 reads `REFRESH_TOKEN_LIFETIME`. `grep -rn` across the repo shows every place that supplies the value uses the FIRST spelling: .env.example:15 `REFRESH_LIFETIME=604800`, .github/workflows/test.yml:23 `REFRESH_LIFETIME: "604800"`, and -- the decisive one the original finder missed -- .github/workflows/deploy.yml:67 `-e REFRESH_LIFETIME="${{ vars.REFRESH_LIFETIME }}"`. Nothing in the repo ever sets `REFRESH_TOKEN_LIFETIME`. So the deployed (non-TESTING) branch is the only one that reads the name nobody supplies: the operator-configured refresh-token lifetime is silently discarded in every real deployment and the hardcoded 604800 wins. That `ACCESS_LIFETIME` on the adjacent lines 133/141 is spelled identically in both arms is what proves the divergence was a copy-paste slip, not a decision.
- **proposed:** Build the shared part once in todo_project/settings/base.py and override only the three keys that actually differ, so a lifetime name can only ever be written in one place:

  _JWT_LIFETIMES = {"ACCESS_TOKEN_LIFETIME": ..., "REFRESH_TOKEN_LIFETIME": ...}
  _TEST_JWT_KEYS  = {"ALGORITHM": "HS256", ...}
  _RS256_JWT_KEYS = {"ALGORITHM": "RS256", ...}
  JWT_CONFIG = {**_JWT_LIFETIMES, **(_TEST_JWT_KEYS if TESTING else _RS256_JWT_KEYS)}

Unify on `REFRESH_LIFETIME`, the name .env.example, test.yml and deploy.yml all already use; that also makes the deploy-time `-e REFRESH_LIFETIME` start working, which is a live behaviour change for anyone whose `vars.REFRESH_LIFETIME` is not 604800 and needs announcing rather than silently shipping.

While in the file, the same read-and-coerce idiom is repeated elsewhere and deserves the same treatment: `os.getenv(X, "True").lower() == "true"` at base.py:149, :150 and :190, and `os.getenv(X, "").split(",")` at base.py:94. Small `env_int` / `env_bool` / `env_list` helpers put the coercion rule in one place. Check todo/tests/unit/settings/ for pinned values before landing.

## 97. jwt_utils generate/validate are two copy-pasted pairs differing only by token_type, with divergent error strings for identical failures
- **dimension:** auth_utils | **severity:** medium | **effort:** small | **copies:** 4 | **~lines:** 45
- **sites:** todo/utils/jwt_utils.py:14-36, todo/utils/jwt_utils.py:39-61, todo/utils/jwt_utils.py:64-82, todo/utils/jwt_utils.py:85-102
- **drift:** The copies already disagree on error text for the same failure while agreeing on text for two different failures -- the inversion is the tell. Generation: "Token generation failed: {e}" (:36) vs "Refresh token generation failed: {e}" (:61). Validation: "Invalid token: {e}" / "Token validation failed: {e}" (:80/:82) vs "Invalid refresh token: {e}" / "Refresh token validation failed: {e}" (:100/:102). Meanwhile the wrong-token_type case raises the identical `TokenInvalidError(AuthErrorMessages.TOKEN_INVALID)` at both :73 and :93 -- so presenting an access token where a refresh token is expected is indistinguishable from a corrupt refresh token, while two identical corruption failures produce four different strings. Separately, `"iss": "todo-app-auth"` is a bare literal duplicated at :20 and :45 and is verified by neither validator (no `issuer=` argument at the `jwt.decode` calls on :66-70 or :87-91), so the claim is decorative in two places at once.
- **proposed:** In todo/utils/jwt_utils.py, one private pair parameterised by token kind, with the four public names kept as thin wrappers so no caller changes:

  JWT_ISSUER = "todo-app-auth"
  def _generate_token(user_data: dict, *, token_type: str, lifetime_key: str) -> str
  def _validate_token(token: str, *, token_type: str, expired_exc: type[Exception]) -> dict

Three parameters replace ~45 duplicated lines, and each parameter corresponds to a genuine axis of variation -- no per-caller branching inside the helper. The error strings become uniform, which is the point: clients and todo/tests/unit/ that string-match "Invalid refresh token" vs "Invalid token" will need updating, so land the message unification as its own commit.

Actually passing `issuer=JWT_ISSUER` to jwt.decode is a separate behaviour change (it rejects any token minted before the claim was checked) and must ship after the de-duplication, not with it.

## 98. The 401 JSON error envelope is constructed three times inside JWTAuthenticationMiddleware, with a different `message` in each
- **dimension:** auth_utils | **severity:** medium | **effort:** small | **copies:** 3 | **~lines:** 30
- **sites:** todo/middlewares/jwt_auth.py:36-49, todo/middlewares/jwt_auth.py:54-67, todo/middlewares/jwt_auth.py:149-157
- **drift:** Three values of `message` for one status in one class: `AuthErrorMessages.AUTHENTICATION_REQUIRED` (:38), `ApiErrors.AUTHENTICATION_FAILED` (:56), `str(exception)` (:151). Worse, `ApiErrors.AUTHENTICATION_FAILED` is verified at todo/constants/messages.py:47 to be a format template, `"Authentication failed: {0}"`, used unformatted as a `title` in all three copies and as the user-facing `message` in the :54-67 copy -- clients literally receive the text `Authentication failed: {0}`. And none of the three sets `authenticated=False`, although exception_handler.py:74-80 and :90-96 set it for the same 401 conditions, so whether a 401 carries `authenticated` depends on whether the middleware or the DRF handler produced it.

I am DROPPING the claimed fourth site, todo/middlewares/team_access_middleware.py:34-50. Verified: it emits `JsonResponse({"detail": ...}, status=...)` one-liners at :35, :44 and :48-50, with no ApiErrorResponse, no ApiErrorDetail and no model_dump. That is an envelope INCONSISTENCY worth fixing, but it is not a near-identical copy of this code and counting it would overstate the duplication.
- **proposed:** A shared todo/middlewares/responses.py owning the envelope, the model_dump call and the `authenticated` convention:

  def auth_error_json(message: str, *, detail: str | None = None,
                      status_code: int = status.HTTP_401_UNAUTHORIZED) -> JsonResponse

All three sites in jwt_auth.py become one line. Add a non-template `ApiErrors.AUTHENTICATION_FAILED_TITLE = "Authentication failed"` to todo/constants/messages.py so the `{0}` stops leaking, and have the helper use it as the title.

Migrating TeamAccessMiddleware onto the same helper is worth doing for envelope consistency but is a separate, breaking change for any frontend reading `.detail` -- keep it out of this commit.

Risk to sequence: the middleware runs before DRF's exception handler, so its body is the raw contract for unauthenticated clients. todo/tests/unit/middlewares/test_jwt_auth.py:93 and :100 both assert `response_data["message"] == AuthErrorMessages.AUTHENTICATION_REQUIRED`, i.e. the :36-49 wording -- so unify on that one and the tests stay green; adding `authenticated: false` is the only payload change.

## 99. "Unwrap ApiErrorResponse from ValueError, else 500 fallback" repeated across eleven view methods, three of which have independently drifted
- **dimension:** auth_utils | **severity:** high | **effort:** large | **copies:** 11 | **~lines:** 120
- **sites:** todo/views/team.py:45-57, todo/views/team.py:88-100, todo/views/team.py:300-317, todo/views/task.py:151-167, todo/views/task.py:349-365, todo/views/watchlist.py:96-108, todo/views/team.py:171-184, todo/views/task.py:433-439, todo/views/task_assignment.py:76-84, todo/views/task_assignment.py:137-145, todo/views/task_assignment.py:300-308
- **drift:** Three independent divergences among copies meant to be identical. (1) todo/views/watchlist.py:101-105 drops the DEBUG guard: `errors=[{"detail": ApiErrors.INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR}]` with no `str(e) if settings.DEBUG` -- watchlist 500s are undebuggable even in DEBUG, unlike every other copy. (2) todo/views/task.py:434-438 drifted the opposite way: `errors=[{"detail": str(e)}]` with no guard at all, so raw exception text (which can carry Mongo/driver internals) reaches clients in production. (3) todo/views/task.py:350 hardened its guard to `if isinstance(e, ValueError) and e.args and isinstance(e.args[0], ApiErrorResponse)` because that handler also catches TaskNotFoundException and PermissionError -- while the other five full copies still do the unguarded `if isinstance(e.args[0], ApiErrorResponse)`, which raises IndexError on a bare `ValueError()`. The fix at :350 was never propagated. Note the whole pattern is redundant with todo/exceptions/exception_handler.py:249-255, which already unwraps ValueError-wrapped ApiErrorResponse for anything a view lets propagate.
- **proposed:** Two-stage, because a single sweep would silently change status codes.

Stage 1 (mechanical, safe): one decorator in todo/exceptions/api_error_boundary.py -- `@api_error_boundary` -- holding the single canonical version of the block, with the `e.args` guard from task.py:350 and the DEBUG-guarded detail. Each of the 11 methods loses its `try/except` tail and gains one decorator line. This alone fixes the watchlist and task.py:433 drift and the IndexError, without touching status-code selection.

Stage 2 (view by view, guarded by the existing view tests): delete the boundary where DRF's configured EXCEPTION_HANDLER already suffices -- `todo.exceptions.exception_handler.handle_exception`, wired at todo_project/settings/base.py:102, already handles ApiErrorResponse-in-ValueError (:249-255), PermissionError (:202-209), TaskNotFoundException (:182-190) and the DEBUG-guarded generic tail (:280-295). The blocker is that a few views map ValueError to a non-500: team.py:305-310 returns 400 and team.py:171-177 returns 404. Those must first raise a typed exception instead of a bare ValueError, or they will start returning `handle_exception`'s 500.

## 100. _handle_validation_errors redefined in eight view classes in four mutually incompatible flavours
- **dimension:** auth_utils | **severity:** high | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 8 | **~lines:** 90
- **sites:** todo/views/task.py:169-195, todo/views/task.py:367-393, todo/views/team.py:102-123, todo/views/team.py:127-129, todo/views/team.py:319-326, todo/views/team.py:469-489, todo/views/team_creation_invite_code.py:28-30, todo/views/team_creation_invite_code.py:125-127
- **drift:** Four incompatible response bodies for one condition, all verified in place. (a) task.py:169/:367 and team.py:102 emit the full ApiErrorResponse with per-field source/title/detail. (b) team.py:127-129 and team_creation_invite_code.py:28-30 / :125-127 emit the raw DRF dict with no envelope: `return Response(data={"errors": errors}, status=status.HTTP_400_BAD_REQUEST)`. (c) team.py:319-326 emits an ApiErrorResponse whose errors are `[{"detail": str(error)} for error in errors.values()]` -- the field name is dropped, so the client cannot tell which field failed. (d) team.py:469-489 hand-rolls a plain dict with string keys `{"source": field, "title": "Invalid value", "detail": str(msg)}` plus an `"authenticated": getattr(self.request, "user", None) is not None` key nothing else emits, and iterates `for msg in msgs` with no list/scalar guard -- a scalar error message gets iterated character by character.

Two copy-paste artifacts confirm the mechanism: team.py:469's def sits directly under the `@extend_schema(...)` block at :452-468, so the schema decorator decorates `_handle_validation_errors` rather than `delete`, which is at :491 (not :490 as claimed); and team_creation_invite_code.py:125-127 is defined AFTER the `post` at :106 that calls it, at the class tail, unlike its byte-identical twin at :28-30.
- **proposed:** A module-level function in todo/exceptions/, next to `format_validation_errors` at todo/exceptions/exception_handler.py:29-45 -- which already implements this loop correctly INCLUDING the nested-dict recursion (:34-36) that all eight copies lack:

  # todo/exceptions/validation_response.py
  def validation_error_response(errors) -> Response

All eight methods become one import and one call, and nested serializer errors start formatting correctly as a side effect.

Better still where it is safe: switch views to `serializer.is_valid(raise_exception=True)` and let the configured DRF handler's DRFValidationError branch (exception_handler.py:256-260) format it -- which is exactly what todo/views/watchlist.py:57 and :89 already do, so the pattern is established in-repo.

Name the shared function differently from the method (`validation_error_response`, not `_handle_validation_errors`): team.py:102 and team.py:127 live in different classes, so today's shared name is not an override, and a reader scanning the file will assume it is.

Risk: four shapes are on the wire, so unifying breaks at least three sets of clients and the view tests asserting them. Migrate the five ApiErrorResponse-shaped sites first (a/c/d), leaving the two raw-dict sites for a separate announced change.

## 101. Admin-email authorization gate and its hardcoded 403 body duplicated between two invite-code views
- **dimension:** auth_utils | **severity:** medium | **effort:** small | **copies:** 4 | **~lines:** 22
- **sites:** todo/views/team_creation_invite_code.py:20-26, todo/views/team_creation_invite_code.py:131-137, todo/views/team_creation_invite_code.py:65-71, todo/views/team_creation_invite_code.py:180-186
- **drift:** None between the two copies -- they are still byte-identical, which is why this is worth extracting before it drifts rather than after. The latent hazards are verified though: the 403 string is a hardcoded literal in both call sites even though `ApiErrors.UNAUTHORIZED_TITLE` at todo/constants/messages.py:52 is the same sentence minus the trailing period ("You are not authorized to perform this action"), so the codebase already carries three spellings of one message. And `VerifyTeamCreationInviteCodeView` (:87-127) sits BETWEEN the two copies with no `_check_authorization` at all -- with the check inlined per class, a reader cannot tell whether that omission is a deliberate policy decision or a third copy someone forgot to paste.
- **proposed:** A DRF permission class in todo/middlewares/permissions.py:

  class IsAdminEmail(BasePermission):
      message = ApiErrors.UNAUTHORIZED_TITLE
      def has_permission(self, request, view) -> bool

Both views declare `permission_classes = [IsAdminEmail]` and delete both methods (:20-26, :131-137) and both guard blocks (:65-71, :180-186). DRF raises PermissionDenied, which the configured handler already renders at exception_handler.py:202-209. The Verify view's lack of a gate becomes an explicit, reviewable fact -- an empty `permission_classes` line rather than a silent absence.

Two things must carry over. (1) `ADMIN_EMAILS = os.getenv("ADMIN_EMAILS", "").split(",")` (base.py:94) yields `['']` when unset, so a blank `request.user_email` would match; the existing `if user_email and ...` truthiness guard is what fails closed and must be preserved verbatim in `has_permission`. (2) The 403 body changes from `{"message": "..."}` to whatever `handle_exception` produces for PermissionDenied, and the check moves ahead of `dispatch` -- relevant only if an endpoint is expected to validate the body before authorizing, which neither of these does.

## 102. Task "exists + caller is creator or assignee" permission block repeated five times, four of them in TaskService
- **dimension:** auth_utils | **severity:** medium | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 5 | **~lines:** 52
- **sites:** todo/services/task_service.py:293-303, todo/services/task_service.py:376-386, todo/services/task_service.py:468-478, todo/services/task_service.py:544-554, todo/repositories/task_repository.py:270-279
- **drift:** The five copies have not diverged from each other, but the codebase already holds three OTHER answers to "does this task exist", which is the same drift one step earlier: todo/utils/task_validation_utils.py:9-56 (`validate_task_exists`, which raises `ValueError(ApiErrorResponse(...))` and performs an ObjectId-format check first, :22-38); todo/services/task_service.py:270-272 in `get_task_by_id` (raises TaskNotFoundException, no ObjectId guard); and todo/services/task_assignment_service.py:25-27 (`TaskRepository.get_by_id` + TaskNotFoundException under the comment `# Validate task exists`). `grep -rn validate_task_exists` shows the dedicated util in utils/ is imported by exactly one module -- todo/services/watchlist_service.py:19, used at :68 and :134. Every one of the five permission copies reimplemented the lookup instead, so none gets the ObjectId-format check and a malformed id reaches the driver as BsonInvalidId (handled at exception_handler.py:226-234) rather than the util's 400. All five also call `TaskRepository._get_assigned_task_ids_for_user`, reaching through the repository's underscore boundary from the service layer at four of them.
- **proposed:** One classmethod on TaskService performing the lookup and the creator-or-assignee check and returning the task:

  @classmethod
  def _get_task_for_mutation(cls, task_id: str, user_id: str) -> TaskModel

Each of update_task / update_task_with_assignee_from_dict / update_task_with_assignee / defer_task then opens with one line: `current_task = cls._get_task_for_mutation(task_id, user_id)`. Add a public `TaskRepository.is_task_assigned_to_user(task_id, user_id) -> bool` so the private `_get_assigned_task_ids_for_user` stops being called across the layer boundary; task_repository.py:270-279 keeps its own inline check (it already lives inside the repository and works on the raw document) but calls the same new public predicate, so the assignee half of the rule exists once.

Two judgement calls to make explicitly rather than by accident. (1) `validate_task_exists` raises `ValueError(ApiErrorResponse(...))` while TaskService raises `TaskNotFoundException`; the handler maps both to 404 but with different title/source payloads, so merging must pick one and will change one set of bodies. Folding in the ObjectId guard likewise converts today's BsonInvalidId-driven 400 into the util's 400 with a different `detail`. (2) `defer_task` uses the block for a semantically different operation than the three updates; a single helper makes it harder to give defer looser or stricter rules later -- acceptable today, worth a comment.

## 103. Identical "wrap failure as 500 REPOSITORY_ERROR" block in TaskService and WatchlistService, with the source label copied across
- **dimension:** auth_utils | **severity:** medium | **effort:** small | **copies:** 2 | **~lines:** 32
- **sites:** todo/services/task_service.py:655-670, todo/services/watchlist_service.py:101-116
- **drift:** The watchlist copy reports `source={ApiErrorSource.PARAMETER: "task_repository"}` (watchlist_service.py:110) for a failure raised while writing a WATCHLIST entry through WatchlistRepository -- `add_task` at :64-99 calls `WatchlistRepository.get_by_user_and_task` and `WatchlistRepository.create`, never a task repository. The label was carried over verbatim from the TaskService original and is now actively wrong in the error payload clients receive. The only other difference is cosmetic: task_service.py:666 parenthesises the conditional (`detail=(str(e) if ... else ...)`) where watchlist_service.py:112 does not -- the signature of a formatter having been run over one copy after the split.
- **proposed:** One helper beside the other error plumbing -- todo/exceptions/service_errors.py:

  def repository_error(exc: Exception, *, source: str) -> ValueError
  def server_error(exc: Exception) -> ValueError

returning the `ValueError(ApiErrorResponse(...))` with the DEBUG-guarded detail and a caller-supplied source. Both services then do `raise repository_error(e, source="watchlist_repository")`, so the source can no longer be inherited by accident -- it is a required keyword argument. The `except Exception` variants collapse onto `server_error`, covering three more copies.

Sequencing matters: this is the service-layer counterpart of the view-layer `@api_error_boundary` finding, and both route their DEBUG-vs-generic decision through the same predicate. Both blocks re-wrap into `ValueError(ApiErrorResponse(...))` purely so the view handlers and exception_handler.py:249-255 can unwrap them; if that convention is later replaced with typed exceptions, this helper should not be built at all. Decide the view-layer question first. Fixing the watchlist `source` value also changes an error payload clients may match on.

## 104. AuthErrorMessages assigns TOKEN_EXPIRED and TOKEN_INVALID twice each; the first values are dead
- **dimension:** auth_utils | **severity:** medium | **effort:** small | **copies:** 4 | **~lines:** 4
- **sites:** todo/constants/messages.py:94, todo/constants/messages.py:99, todo/constants/messages.py:95, todo/constants/messages.py:101
- **drift:** This is the constants-layer form of the same drift found in exception_handler.py and jwt_auth.py: one auth condition acquired a second name and nobody removed the first. Every consumer silently gets the later, terser text and never the more descriptive earlier one -- including `TokenExpiredError`'s default (todo/exceptions/auth_exceptions.py:16), `TokenInvalidError`'s default (:26), the wrong-token_type raises at todo/utils/jwt_utils.py:73 and :93, and `_set_user_data`'s `raise TokenInvalidError(AuthErrorMessages.INVALID_TOKEN)` path in the middleware. Related cross-class duplication in the same file: `ApiErrors.SEARCH_QUERY_EMPTY` (messages.py:55) and `ValidationErrors.SEARCH_QUERY_EMPTY` (messages.py:84) are two constants holding the identical string "Search query cannot be empty".
- **proposed:** Delete the shadowed assignments at todo/constants/messages.py:94-95 -- a runtime no-op today -- keeping one definition per concept. Then split the class along the axis it is actually used on, since the two roles are already mixed: `TOKEN_*` values that serve as exception message defaults versus `*_TITLE` values that serve as ApiErrorDetail titles.

Guard against recurrence: pyproject.toml:42 selects only E4/E7/E9/F, and F811 covers redefinition of functions and imports but not plain class-body assignments. Either add a ruff rule that catches it (PLW0127-family / a `flake8-simplify`-style check) or add a one-line test asserting the class has no duplicate values -- the cheap version is a test that reads the source with `ast` and fails on a repeated assignment target inside a constants class.

Caveat to state in review: removing the shadowed lines is free, but if a reviewer instead decides the FIRST wording was the intended one, changing the surviving value alters user-visible auth error text everywhere those exceptions default -- including strings the middleware puts directly on the wire via `message=str(exception)` at todo/middlewares/jwt_auth.py:151.

## 105. Test authentication cookie ritual hand-rolled in five places despite a base class that provides it
- **dimension:** auth_utils | **severity:** low | **effort:** small | **copies:** 5 | **~lines:** 25
- **sites:** todo/tests/integration/base_mongo_test.py:71-74, todo/tests/unit/views/test_label.py:21-31, todo/tests/unit/views/test_watchlist_check.py:19-27, todo/tests/unit/views/test_auth.py:198-206, todo/tests/unit/views/test_auth.py:243-251
- **drift:** The copies build different users and therefore need different mocks to pass, and that is the drift. `AuthenticatedMongoTestCase` derives user_data from the shared `google_auth_user_payload` fixture AND inserts the user into Mongo (base_mongo_test.py:48-69), so the middleware's `UserRepository.get_by_id` check at todo/middlewares/jwt_auth.py:116-118 genuinely passes. The hand-rolled copies skip the insert and compensate three different ways: test_label.py:34 patches out `JWTAuthenticationMiddleware._try_authentication` entirely (`@patch(..., return_value=True)`), test_watchlist_check.py:30 patches `UserRepository.get_by_id`, and test_auth.py does neither. So four suites exercise four different amounts of the auth middleware while their setUps look identical, and `AuthenticatedTestCase` in test_label.py:15-31 duplicates the name and the role of `AuthenticatedMongoTestCase` without sharing a line of code with it.
- **proposed:** Promote one `authenticate(client, user_data)` helper (or an `AuthenticatedClientMixin` exposing `_set_auth_cookies`) into todo/tests/, alongside the existing fixtures in todo/tests/fixtures/user.py, and have `AuthenticatedMongoTestCase`, test_label.py's `AuthenticatedTestCase`, test_watchlist_check.py and both test_auth.py setUps call it.

Keep the DB-insert step as a separate opt-in (`AuthenticatedMongoTestCase` keeps `_create_test_user`), so the difference between "the user really exists in Mongo" and "the middleware is stubbed" is stated at the call site rather than implied by which file you are reading.

Test-only, so no production risk -- but do it as a mechanical extraction of the cookie lines ONLY, leaving each suite's patching decisions alone. Consolidating further would force the stubbed suites (test_label) onto a level of middleware coverage that would require Mongo where none is set up today.

## 106. MISSED BY FINDER: the invalid-ObjectId 400 detail is built twice, byte-identically, in two adjacent handle_exception branches
- **dimension:** auth_utils | **severity:** low | **effort:** small | **copies:** 2 | **~lines:** 16
- **sites:** todo/exceptions/exception_handler.py:227-234, todo/exceptions/exception_handler.py:241-248
- **drift:** Not yet drifted, but the conditions on the second branch are already carrying the scars of the split: :239 string-matches TWO different literals (`ValidationErrors.INVALID_TASK_ID_FORMAT` and the bare `"Invalid ObjectId format"`) to catch the same condition, because the ValueError-raising sites spell the message differently -- todo/models/common/pyobjectid.py:15, todo/models/team.py:42, todo/models/task_assignment.py:34 and todo/models/team_creation_invite_code.py:30 all raise `ValueError(f"Invalid ObjectId: {v}")`, which matches NEITHER literal and therefore falls past both branches to the generic 500 tail at :280-295. Two duplicated bodies plus a two-literal string match is a strong signal that the condition, not the body, is what needs consolidating.
- **proposed:** Merge the two branches with a small predicate in todo/exceptions/exception_handler.py, so the response body exists once and the growing list of "ways a bad ObjectId arrives" lives in one named place:

  def _is_invalid_object_id(exc) -> bool

Then a single `elif _is_invalid_object_id(exc):` carries the one copy of the eight-line body. The predicate is also the right home for the currently-unmatched `"Invalid ObjectId: ..."` prefix raised by the four pydantic models, if the team decides those should be 400s rather than 500s -- but that is a behaviour change and belongs in a follow-up, not in the de-duplication commit.

This is a pure refactor as written: identical bodies, same status code, adjacent branches in the same if/elif chain, and no caller outside the function. Land it together with the Candidate 1 cleanup of the same function.

## 107. "permission denied if not creator or assignee" scenario copied five times; the two repository copies raise the exception themselves and test nothing
- **dimension:** tests | **severity:** high | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 5 | **~lines:** 80
- **sites:** todo/tests/unit/repositories/test_task_repository.py:348-363, todo/tests/unit/repositories/test_task_repository.py:505-517, todo/tests/unit/services/test_task_service.py:788-806, todo/tests/unit/services/test_task_service.py:926-939, todo/tests/unit/services/test_task_service.py:1231-1249
- **drift:** Confirmed and worse than claimed — I found a fifth copy the finder missed. (1) Both repository copies (test_task_repository.py:361-363 and 515-517) build the full mock scaffold and then execute `raise PermissionError(...)` inside the assertRaises block instead of calling TaskRepository.update / TaskRepository.delete_by_id. Neither method under test is ever invoked; `mock_get_by_id`, `mock_get_assigned` and `mock_collection.find_one_and_update` are never asserted. These two tests assert that Python's `raise` statement works. (2) The three service copies do call the code under test, but drift among themselves: 788-806 and 1231-1249 assert `mock_get_by_id.assert_called_once_with(task_id)` + `mock_update.assert_not_called()`, while 1251-1270 (test_delete_task_permission_denied) asserts the exact opposite — `mock_get_by_id.assert_not_called()` and `mock_get_assigned.assert_not_called()` — because delete pushes the check into the repository. (3) The sentinel string drifted: `createdBy = "some_other_user"` at test_task_service.py:797 and 1240 and test_task_repository.py:356/512, but `"different_user"` at test_task_service.py:930. (4) test_task_service.py:1239 does `task_model = self.task_model` (no `.model_copy(deep=True)`, unlike its twin at 796) and then mutates `createdBy` on the shared setUp instance, leaking into any test that runs after it in that class.
- **proposed:** Two pieces, both in a new todo/tests/fixtures/permissions.py:

1. `def non_owner_task(model_or_doc, owner="some_other_user")` — returns a deep copy with createdBy stamped, so the sentinel string is defined once and the copy-vs-mutate bug at test_task_service.py:1239 becomes impossible.
2. `def assert_permission_denied(testcase, call, *, not_called=(), called_once_with=())` — runs `call()` inside `assertRaises(PermissionError)`, asserts `str(exc) == ApiErrors.UNAUTHORIZED_TITLE`, then applies the not_called / called_once_with expectations.

Call sites pass a zero-arg lambda for the operation under test, so the helper cannot be satisfied by a bare `raise` — writing the two repository tests through it forces them to name TaskRepository.update / TaskRepository.delete_by_id, which is exactly the point. Expect those two to then fail or need deletion (the permission check may live in the service, not the repository); that decision is the deliverable of this refactor, not a side effect.

## 108. Mongo task + task_details seeding block copy-pasted into four integration setUps, with three different id encodings
- **dimension:** tests | **severity:** high | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 4 | **~lines:** 110
- **sites:** todo/tests/integration/test_task_detail_api.py:16-40, todo/tests/integration/test_tasks_delete.py:17-43, todo/tests/integration/test_task_update_api.py:17-45, todo/tests/integration/test_task_defer_api.py:17-51
- **drift:** Verified, with one correction to the finder. (1) Three id encodings for one collection: detail:25-35 writes `_id`, `assignee_id`, `task_id`, `created_by` all as str; update:29-40 and defer:37-48 write all four as ObjectId; delete:27-38 is a hybrid — ObjectId for `_id`/`assignee_id`/`created_by` but `"task_id": str(task_doc["_id"])`. (2) detail:25-35 is the only copy missing `"is_action_taken": False`. (3) CORRECTION to the finder's claim: detail and delete do both reuse the fixture's own `_id` via `task_doc.pop("id")`, but they seed *different* ids — detail uses `tasks_db_data[1]` (672f... no, 674c726ca89aab38040cb964) and delete uses `tasks_db_data[0]` (672f7c5b775ee9f4471ff1dd), so the "two suites seed identical _ids" part of the claim is false; the real point stands (two suites depend on the shared fixture's literal id, two mint fresh ones). (4) detail:21 and delete:23 set `task_doc["updatedBy"]`; update and defer do not. (5) delete:29 and :34 read `ObjectId(self.user_data["user_id"])` where the other three use `ObjectId(self.user_id)` for the same value. (6) Invalid-id literal: `"invalid-task-id"` (detail:40, delete:43) vs `"bad-task-id"` (update:45); the id attribute names drifted too — existing_task_id/non_existent_id/invalid_task_id vs valid_id/missing_id/bad_id.
- **proposed:** Promote test_task_defer_api.py's `_insert_task` onto AuthenticatedMongoTestCase in todo/tests/integration/base_mongo_test.py as two methods:

`def seed_task(self, *, fixture_index=0, new_id=True, **overrides) -> str` — copies tasks_db_data[fixture_index], sets `_id` (fresh ObjectId when new_id, else the fixture's own), drops `assignee`, stamps createdBy from self.user_id, applies **overrides, inserts, returns str(task_id).

`def seed_task_assignment(self, task_id, *, assignee_id=None, user_type="user") -> ObjectId` — inserts the task_details doc with ONE canonical id encoding.

Also add `self.invalid_task_id = "invalid-task-id"` and `self.non_existent_id = str(ObjectId())` to the base setUp so the four suites stop re-declaring them under three different names. Each of the four setUps collapses to two lines. Decide the encoding by reading what TaskAssignmentRepository writes in production before migrating — test_task_detail_api.py:55 asserts `data["assignee"]["assignee_id"] == str(self.user_id)` and may be passing only because that suite writes strings.

## 109. API error-envelope assertion sequences (404 / 400) restated ten times instead of one assert helper
- **dimension:** tests | **severity:** high | **effort:** small | **copies:** 10 | **~lines:** 130
- **sites:** todo/tests/integration/test_task_detail_api.py:61-69, todo/tests/integration/test_tasks_delete.py:53-60, todo/tests/integration/test_task_update_api.py:71-77, todo/tests/unit/views/test_task.py:126-133, todo/tests/unit/views/test_task.py:796-802, todo/tests/integration/test_task_detail_api.py:74-80, todo/tests/integration/test_tasks_delete.py:65-71, todo/tests/integration/test_task_update_api.py:82-89, todo/tests/unit/views/test_task.py:142-149, todo/tests/unit/views/test_task.py:822-827
- **drift:** Confirmed, with one correction. CORRECTION: the finder said "the two integration 404 copies never assert statusCode" — it is all THREE integration 404 copies (test_task_detail_api.py:61-69, test_tasks_delete.py:53-60, test_task_update_api.py:71-77); only the two unit copies (test_task.py:127, :798) check it. `len(response.data["errors"]) == 1` is asserted only at test_task.py:129 and :145 — no integration copy checks the envelope carries exactly one error. Accessor style forked four ways for one value: `data["errors"][0]["source"]["path"]` (detail:78, delete:69), `error = data["errors"][0]` then `error["source"]["path"]` (detail:67, delete:58), `err = body["errors"][0]` (update:86), and whole-dict equality `response.data["errors"][0]["source"] == {"path": "task_id"}` (test_task.py:130, :146). test_task_update_api.py:71-77 asserts a strict subset of test_task_detail_api.py:61-69 in a different order (title, detail, source instead of source, title, detail). statusCode itself is spelled as a bare `400` at detail:76 and delete:67 but as `HTTPStatus.BAD_REQUEST` at update:84.
- **proposed:** A mixin in a new todo/tests/fixtures/assertions.py:

`class ApiErrorAssertions:
    def assertApiError(self, response, *, status, message, title, source=None, detail=None, error_count=None)`

It normalises `response.json()` vs `response.data` internally (try `response.json()`, fall back to `response.data`), asserts status_code and `statusCode`, `message`, `errors[0]["title"]`, `errors[0]["detail"]` (defaulting detail to message), the `source` dict when given, and `len(errors)` when error_count is passed. Mix it into AuthenticatedMongoTestCase so all ten sites get it for free. Keep the `error_count` default at None for the first migration and tighten it per-endpoint afterwards, as the finder correctly noted.

## 110. `mock_get_tasks.assert_called_once_with(...)` seven-kwarg blob repeated thirteen times
- **dimension:** tests | **severity:** high | **effort:** small | **copies:** 13 | **~lines:** 115
- **sites:** todo/tests/unit/views/test_task.py:48-56, todo/tests/unit/views/test_task.py:67-75, todo/tests/unit/views/test_task.py:178-186, todo/tests/unit/views/test_task.py:196-204, todo/tests/unit/views/test_task.py:244-252, todo/tests/unit/views/test_task.py:261-269, todo/tests/unit/views/test_task.py:290-298, todo/tests/unit/views/test_task.py:313-321, todo/tests/unit/views/test_task.py:348-356, todo/tests/unit/views/test_task.py:365-373, todo/tests/unit/views/test_task.py:382-390, todo/tests/integration/test_tasks_pagination.py:23-31, todo/tests/integration/test_tasks_pagination.py:38-46
- **drift:** Confirmed. `sort_by` is the bare string "updatedAt" in six copies (test_task.py:51, 70, 181, 199 and test_tasks_pagination.py:26, 41) and the SORT_FIELD_UPDATED_AT constant in two (test_task.py:368, 385) — a rename of the constant would break only half the assertions. `order` has the same split: the literal "desc" at test_task.py:52, 71, 182, 200, 248, 369 vs SORT_ORDER_DESC at :265. `limit` reads from settings (`settings.REST_FRAMEWORK["DEFAULT_PAGINATION_SETTINGS"]["DEFAULT_PAGE_LIMIT"]`) at test_task.py:66-69, :177-180 and test_tasks_pagination.py:18-25 but is hardcoded `limit=20` at test_task.py:246, 263, 292, 315, 350, 367, 384 — seven places that silently encode the settings value. The assertion verb also drifted: `assert_called_once_with` in the unit copies vs `assert_called_with` in both integration copies.
- **proposed:** A method on a shared view-test base (a `TaskViewAssertions` mixin in todo/tests/fixtures/assertions.py, mixed into the AuthenticatedMongoTestCase subclasses in test_task.py):

`def assertGetTasksCalled(self, mock_get_tasks, *, page=1, limit=None, sort_by=SORT_FIELD_UPDATED_AT, order=SORT_ORDER_DESC, **extra)`

It fills `user_id=str(self.user_id)`, defaults `limit` from settings, and defaults `team_id`/`status_filter` to None unless overridden in **extra, then delegates to `assert_called_once_with`. One line per site; adding an eighth service kwarg becomes a one-line edit instead of thirteen. Per the finder's own risk note, require **extra to be explicit rather than merging silently so a test that should pass team_id cannot accidentally assert None.

## 111. UpdateTaskSerializer/DeferTaskSerializer mock preamble restated eleven times, in two competing idioms
- **dimension:** tests | **severity:** medium | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 11 | **~lines:** 55
- **sites:** todo/tests/unit/views/test_task.py:598-608, todo/tests/unit/views/test_task.py:634-640, todo/tests/unit/views/test_task.py:653-656, todo/tests/unit/views/test_task.py:670-673, todo/tests/unit/views/test_task.py:735-738, todo/tests/unit/views/test_task.py:759-762, todo/tests/unit/views/test_task.py:787-790, todo/tests/unit/views/test_task.py:813-816, todo/tests/unit/views/test_task.py:836-839, todo/tests/unit/views/test_task.py:866-869, todo/tests/unit/views/test_task.py:893-896
- **drift:** Confirmed. The same file runs two idioms for the same job: the standalone `Mock()` + explicit `.return_value` wiring (nine copies, listed above) and the shorter `mock_x_serializer_class.return_value` (test_task.py:917-918, 935-937, 954-956, 968-970, 987-989). The @patch pair order drifted with it: `(UpdateTaskSerializer, TaskService.update_task*)` at 583-584, 648-649, 665-666, 726-727 giving the signature `(mock_service_update_task, mock_update_serializer_class)`, but reversed to `(TaskService.update_task, UpdateTaskSerializer)` at 782-783, 804-805, 829-830, 859-860, 886-887 giving `(mock_update_serializer_class, mock_service_update_task)` — an easy place to bind the wrong mock. And the two `update_task_with_assignee_from_dict` tests assert HTTP 500 for a TaskNotFoundException (test_task.py:662) and a PermissionError (test_task.py:679), while the sibling test on the `update_task` path asserts 404 for the same TaskNotFoundException (test_task.py:796) — a divergence a shared arrange helper would have put side by side.
- **proposed:** A free function in a new todo/tests/fixtures/serializer_mocks.py:

`def stub_serializer(mock_cls, *, validated_data=None, errors=None, raises=None) -> Mock`

It creates the instance, sets `is_valid.return_value = True` (or `is_valid.side_effect = raises`), sets `validated_data` / `errors`, wires `mock_cls.return_value`, and returns the instance so tests can still assert on it. Every one of the eleven sites becomes one line, and the two idioms collapse into one. Do the migration one class at a time; if you also hoist the @patch pair to a class-level decorator on TaskDetailViewPatchTests, do it in the same commit as the signature rewrites, since class-level @patch changes the positional order of injected mocks for every method at once.

## 112. JWT cookie / APIClient auth bootstrap rebuilt in five places, four re-inlining the user fixture without "picture"
- **dimension:** tests | **severity:** medium | **effort:** small | **copies:** 5 | **~lines:** 45
- **sites:** todo/tests/integration/base_mongo_test.py:54-57, todo/tests/integration/base_mongo_test.py:71-74, todo/tests/unit/views/test_label.py:21-31, todo/tests/unit/views/test_auth.py:198-206, todo/tests/unit/views/test_auth.py:243-251, todo/tests/unit/views/test_watchlist_check.py:19-27
- **drift:** Confirmed, and slightly worse than claimed. todo/tests/fixtures/user.py:24-30 defines `google_auth_user_payload` with five keys including "picture"; only base_mongo_test.py:11,55 imports it. FOUR inline copies re-type the dict and all four dropped "picture" (test_label.py:22-27, test_auth.py:198-203, test_auth.py:243-248, test_watchlist_check.py:19-24) — so a JWT claim present in integration tokens is absent from every unit-test token. test_auth.py:198-203 is the sharpest case: that file already imports `google_auth_user_payload` (test_auth.py:11) and cherry-picks three of its five keys back out by hand (`"google_id": google_auth_user_payload["google_id"], "email": ...["email"], "name": ...["name"]`) rather than spreading it. Wrapper drift too: test_label.py:15-31 invented `AuthenticatedTestCase(APISimpleTestCase)` with `_setup_auth_cookies`, base_mongo_test.py:71 names the same method `_set_auth_cookies`, and test_auth.py / test_watchlist_check.py inline it with no method at all. test_label.py:34 then patches out `JWTAuthenticationMiddleware._try_authentication` at class level, so its cookie setup is dead weight there.
- **proposed:** One DB-free mixin in a new todo/tests/fixtures/auth.py:

`class AuthCookieMixin:
    def authenticate(self, client=None, **payload_overrides) -> dict`

It merges overrides over `google_auth_user_payload` (fresh user_id per call), calls `generate_token_pair`, sets both cookies on `client or self.client`, stores and returns the payload. `AuthenticatedMongoTestCase._set_auth_cookies` becomes `self.user_data = self.authenticate(**{"user_id": str(self.user_id)})`; test_label.py's `AuthenticatedTestCase`, test_auth.py:243-251 and test_watchlist_check.py:19-27 each collapse to `self.user_data = self.authenticate()`. Keeping the mixin DB-free is what lets it serve both APISimpleTestCase (test_label.py, no DB) and AuthenticatedMongoTestCase — the `db.users.insert_one` half stays in AuthenticatedMongoTestCase._create_test_user.

## 113. CreateTaskDTO literal and create_task mock scaffold repeated across four near-identical service tests, two of which are the same test twice
- **dimension:** tests | **severity:** medium | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 4 | **~lines:** 120
- **sites:** todo/tests/unit/services/test_task_service.py:211-260, todo/tests/unit/services/test_task_service.py:262-298, todo/tests/unit/services/test_task_service.py:300-329, todo/tests/unit/services/test_task_service.py:331-374
- **drift:** Confirmed. `test_create_task_with_user_assignment_and_team_id` (211-260) and `test_create_task_passes_team_id_to_assignment_service` (331-374) are the same test twice — identical five-@patch stacks (211-215 vs 331-335, same targets in the same order), identical DTO, identical mock scaffold, and overlapping assertions on `assignment_call_args.team_id` / `.assignee_id` / `.user_type`; the second merely drops the `mock_prepare_dto.assert_called_once_with(...)` and `result.data` checks. `test_create_task_with_user_assignment_without_team_id` (262-298) forgot the `mock_assignment_response` block the other three set up (282-289 has no such block), so `mock_create_assignment` returns a bare MagicMock there and the test would not notice if the service started reading `.data` off it. CORRECTION to the finder: the decorator counts are four (262-298, no TeamRepository patch) vs five, not "three vs five".
- **proposed:** Add to todo/tests/fixtures/task.py:

`def make_create_task_dto(**overrides) -> CreateTaskDTO` — the canonical Test Task / HIGH / TODO / labels=[] / dueAt=now+1d shape, with `assignee` and `createdBy` as the usual overrides.

And a private `_arrange_create_task(self, mock_create, mock_prepare_dto, mock_create_assignment, *, assignee_id)` on the test class that wires mock_task_model / mock_task_dto / mock_assignment_response and returns them as a SimpleNamespace. Then fold 211-260 and 331-374 into one test, and drive the team_id-present / team_id-absent pair with subTest so the missing `mock_assignment_response` in the no-team case cannot recur.

## 114. Inline TaskDTO fixtures rebuilt three times in the view tests beside the canonical fixture module, all four disagreeing on the nested assignment
- **dimension:** tests | **severity:** medium | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 5 | **~lines:** 140
- **sites:** todo/tests/fixtures/task.py:47-72, todo/tests/fixtures/task.py:73-98, todo/tests/unit/views/test_task.py:412-437, todo/tests/unit/views/test_task.py:553-581, todo/tests/unit/views/test_task.py:696-724
- **drift:** Confirmed. Four TaskDTO shapes, no two agreeing on the nested TaskAssignmentDTO: fixtures/task.py:53-62 sets assignee_name="SYSTEM" and omits updated_by/updated_at; test_task.py:568-578 sets updated_by=None and updated_at=None but omits assignee_name; test_task.py:703-712 sets assignee_name="SYSTEM" and omits updated_by/updated_at; test_task.py:419-428 matches the fixture's shape but with different literal values. Optional-field coverage drifted the same way: test_task.py:579-580 passes both `deferredDetails=None` and `in_watchlist=None`, :719 passes only `in_watchlist=None`, and :412-437 passes neither. The two `self.updated_task_dto_fixture` blocks at 553-581 and 696-724 exist to serve the same purpose in two adjacent classes. Also note the two entries in `task_dtos` itself (fixtures/task.py:47-72 and 73-98) are near-identical 26-line literals differing in four values — both carry `displayId="#1"`, which looks like a copy-paste slip in the second.
- **proposed:** Extend todo/tests/fixtures/task.py with two factories returning fresh instances:

`def make_task_assignment_dto(**overrides) -> TaskAssignmentDTO` — canonical field set including updated_by/updated_at/assignee_name.
`def make_task_dto(**overrides) -> TaskDTO` — canonical shape with a nested `make_task_assignment_dto()`, explicit `deferredDetails=None` and `in_watchlist=None`.

The two setUp blocks become one line each. Derive the existing module-level `task_dtos` list from `make_task_dto` so the list and the factory cannot drift; returning fresh instances also preserves the reason test_task.py:928 currently calls `.model_copy(deep=True)` before mutating.

## 115. Repository get_collection patcher setUp/tearDown repeated four times, plus three decorator-based re-dos, one silently dropping spec=Collection
- **dimension:** tests | **severity:** medium | **effort:** small | **copies:** 7 | **~lines:** 32
- **sites:** todo/tests/unit/repositories/test_task_repository.py:56-59, todo/tests/unit/repositories/test_task_repository.py:61-62, todo/tests/unit/repositories/test_task_repository.py:233-236, todo/tests/unit/repositories/test_task_repository.py:263-264, todo/tests/unit/repositories/test_task_repository.py:368-371, todo/tests/unit/repositories/test_task_repository.py:377-378, todo/tests/unit/repositories/test_task_repository.py:469-472, todo/tests/unit/repositories/test_task_repository.py:493-496, todo/tests/unit/repositories/test_task_repository.py:505-508, todo/tests/unit/repositories/test_label_repository.py:16-19, todo/tests/unit/repositories/test_label_repository.py:21-22
- **drift:** Confirmed. `MagicMock(spec=Collection)` at test_task_repository.py:58 and :235 and test_label_repository.py:18, but a bare `MagicMock()` at test_task_repository.py:370 — so TaskRepositorySortingTests silently accepts calls to methods that do not exist on a pymongo Collection while TaskRepositoryTests and TaskRepositoryUpdateTests would catch them. A fifth class in the same file, TestRepositoryDeleteTaskById, abandoned the setUp form entirely and re-does the same wiring as a per-method @patch decorator three times (test_task_repository.py:469-472, 493-496, 505-508), each `mock_collection = MagicMock(); mock_get_collection.return_value = mock_collection` — also without spec. All four setUp copies also hand-write a tearDown instead of `self.addCleanup(patcher.stop)`, so a failure inside setUp leaks the patch.
- **proposed:** A shared base in a new todo/tests/unit/repositories/base.py:

`class MongoCollectionTestCase(TestCase):
    repository = None  # subclass sets e.g. TaskRepository`

Its setUp patches `f"{cls.__module__}.{cls.__name__}.get_collection"` for `self.repository`, registers `self.addCleanup(patcher.stop)` (deleting all four hand-written tearDowns), and exposes `self.mock_get_collection` and `self.mock_collection = MagicMock(spec=Collection)` — one spec decision for every repository test. TaskRepositoryTests, TaskRepositoryUpdateTests, TaskRepositorySortingTests and LabelRepositoryTests each keep only their own extra setUp lines, and TestRepositoryDeleteTaskById drops its three @patch decorators. Run the suite after migrating: test_label_repository.py:43 asserts `self.mock_get_collection.assert_not_called()`, which stays valid only because the base setUp itself never calls it.

## 116. Identical nine-line `default_task_model = TaskModel(...)` setUp copied into three consecutive service test classes, with a fourth variant already split off

Each of the three setUps becomes `self.default_task_model = make_task_model(id=ObjectId(self.task_id_str), createdBy=self.user_id_str)`, and TaskServiceDeferTests expresses its variant as `make_task_model(displayId="TASK-1", dueAt=self.due_at, createdBy=self.user_id)`. ## 117. Pagination query-param serializer suites duplicated method-for-method for tasks and labels, now encoding two different contracts
- **dimension:** tests | **severity:** medium | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 2 | **~lines:** 75
- **sites:** todo/tests/unit/serializers/test_get_tasks_serializer.py:18-72, todo/tests/unit/serializers/test_get_tasks_serializer.py:74-92, todo/tests/unit/serializers/test_get_labels_serializer.py:10-65
- **drift:** Confirmed — and the drift traces to a real production divergence, which strengthens the case. todo/serializers/get_labels_serializer.py:12-14 and :21-23 set `error_messages={"min_value": ValidationErrors.PAGE_POSITIVE}` / `LIMIT_POSITIVE` ("Page must be a positive integer", todo/constants/messages.py:71); get_tasks_serializer.py sets no such override, so the tasks suite asserts the raw literal "page must be greater than or equal to 1" — the same condition, two messages, and neither test file knows the other exists. Default page size likewise: get_labels_serializer.py:18 hardcodes `default=10` while get_tasks_serializer.py:25 reads `settings.REST_FRAMEWORK["DEFAULT_PAGINATION_SETTINGS"]["DEFAULT_PAGE_LIMIT"]` (= 20, todo_project/settings/base.py:104), and the two test suites faithfully encode both (labels:22 `self.assertEqual(..., 10)` vs tasks:29-32 reading settings). Within the tasks file, `test_serializer_uses_django_settings_values` (74-92) is a third copy of assertions already made at 25-32 and 48-54.
- **proposed:** A mixin in a new todo/tests/unit/serializers/pagination_contract.py:

`class PaginationQueryParamsContractMixin:
    serializer_class = None
    expected_default_limit = None
    page_min_message = ValidationErrors.PAGE_POSITIVE
    limit_min_message = ValidationErrors.LIMIT_POSITIVE`

with the seven contract tests written once and the min/max cases driven by subTest over `[("page", "0", self.page_min_message), ("limit", "0", self.limit_min_message), ("limit", max+1, f"Ensure this value is less than or equal to {max}")]`. GetTaskQueryParamsSerializerTest and GetLabelQueryParamsSerializerTest then declare two or three attributes and keep only their own extras (sorting, search). Delete test_get_tasks_serializer.py:74-92. The message attributes are per-subclass on purpose: the two serializers genuinely emit different text today, and the mixin makes that difference a single visible line instead of a buried literal — aligning the serializers is a separate production decision.

## 118. TeamService five-decorator patch stacks duplicated in two scenario families, with unequal assertion strength
- **dimension:** tests | **severity:** medium | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 4 | **~lines:** 90
- **sites:** todo/tests/unit/services/test_team_service.py:261-284, todo/tests/unit/services/test_team_service.py:286-309, todo/tests/unit/services/test_team_service.py:362-384, todo/tests/unit/services/test_team_service.py:419-444
- **drift:** Confirmed for the assertion-strength drift, REJECTED for the patch-target claim. Real: test_team_service.py:278-279 asserts `mock_remove_roles.assert_called_once()` and `mock_reassign_tasks.assert_called_once()` with no arguments, while its twin at :303-304 asserts `assert_called_once_with(self.member_id, self.team_id)` and `assert_called_once_with(self.member_id, self.team_id, self.member_id)` — the weaker copy would not catch a swapped-argument regression. Real: the update-team family feeds different fakes into the same slot — a fresh inline `UserDTO(...)` at :344-346 and :407-409 versus `self.user_model` at :372 and :429. CORRECTION to the finder: the claim that the two UserRepository patch targets mean "two of the four stub a lookup the other two leave live" is FALSE. todo/dto/update_team_dto.py:3 does `from todo.repositories.user_repository import UserRepository`, so `patch("todo.dto.update_team_dto.UserRepository.get_by_id")` (:333, :398) and `patch("todo.repositories.user_repository.UserRepository.get_by_id")` (:365, :422) resolve to the same class object and patch the same attribute. The inconsistency is cosmetic, not behavioural — worth normalising in the shared stack, but it is not evidence of a coverage hole.
- **proposed:** Two contextmanager helpers on TeamServiceTests (or a todo/tests/fixtures/team_service_mocks.py), each defined once with one agreed patch target per dependency and yielding a SimpleNamespace of mocks:

`@contextmanager def patch_remove_member()` — the five remove-member targets plus the four `return_value = True/None` lines.
`@contextmanager def patch_update_team()` — the five update-team targets plus `team_get/team_update/user_get/has_role` defaults.

Pair them with `def assertAuditLog(self, mock_create, *, action, team_id, performed_by)` replacing the five-line tail, which forces both remove-member copies to the stronger form. Prefer the contextmanager over a class-level @patch stack: test_update_team_fails_for_non_admin (386-396) needs only two patches and test_update_team_poc_not_team_member_raises_error (398-417) four, so a class-level stack would over-patch them and renumber every injected mock.

## 119. The sort-field/default-order matrix expanded into one method per row at the repository and service layers, while two other layers already use a subTest table
- **dimension:** tests | **severity:** medium | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 4 | **~lines:** 250
- **sites:** todo/tests/unit/repositories/test_task_repository.py:380-451, todo/tests/unit/services/test_task_service.py:427-498, todo/tests/unit/views/test_task.py:271-298, todo/tests/integration/test_task_sorting_integration.py:61-85
- **drift:** Confirmed. Two of the four layers hold the whole matrix in a list of tuples (test_task.py:275-281, test_task_sorting_integration.py:67-73) while the other two still spell it out one method at a time — and the tables have already diverged. test_task_repository.py imports only SORT_FIELD_PRIORITY, SORT_FIELD_DUE_AT, SORT_FIELD_CREATED_AT and SORT_FIELD_ASSIGNEE (test_task_repository.py:12-21) — SORT_FIELD_UPDATED_AT is not imported and never tested there, despite updatedAt being the production default asserted at test_task.py:368 and test_task_sorting_integration.py:119. And test_task_repository.py:380-393 encodes the priority sort direction inverted relative to every other field (`SORT_ORDER_DESC -> [(SORT_FIELD_PRIORITY, 1)]`, `SORT_ORDER_ASC -> [(SORT_FIELD_PRIORITY, -1)]`, versus createdAt desc -> -1 at :399) — a real inversion that only one of the four layers records, and only in a docstring.
- **proposed:** Scope this narrower than the finder proposed — do NOT build one cross-layer expectation table, because the layers legitimately assert different things (mongo sort tuples with an inverted priority direction, repository call kwargs, HTTP status). Instead:

1. One shared input table `SORT_DEFAULTS = [(SORT_FIELD_PRIORITY, SORT_ORDER_DESC), (SORT_FIELD_DUE_AT, SORT_ORDER_ASC), (SORT_FIELD_CREATED_AT, SORT_ORDER_DESC), (SORT_FIELD_UPDATED_AT, SORT_ORDER_DESC), (SORT_FIELD_ASSIGNEE, SORT_ORDER_ASC)]` in a new todo/tests/fixtures/sorting.py, imported by all four layers so adding a sort field touches one line.
2. Each layer keeps its own single subTest-driven method with its own expectation function — collapsing test_task_repository.py:380-433 (eight methods) and test_task_service.py:427-498 (six methods) into one each, matching the form test_task.py:271-298 and test_task_sorting_integration.py:61-85 already use.

The repository loop needs an explicit direction column (`MONGO_DIRECTIONS`) because of the priority inversion — writing that inversion down as data, rather than leaving it implicit in one of eight method bodies, is most of the value here.

## 120. "Re-authenticate as another user, expect 403" copied across four integration suites, asserting two incompatible envelopes
- **dimension:** tests | **severity:** medium | **effort:** small | **copies:** 4 | **~lines:** 55
- **sites:** todo/tests/integration/test_tasks_delete.py:73-84, todo/tests/integration/test_task_update_api.py:91-108, todo/tests/integration/test_task_defer_api.py:129-144, todo/tests/integration/test_team_update.py:137-147

Real: test_tasks_delete.py:83-84 dropped the intermediate `err = ...` binding that test_task_update_api.py:107 and test_task_defer_api.py:143 kept. CORRECTION: the finder claims `other_user_id` is "bound and then never referenced" at test_task_defer_api.py:135 and test_tasks_delete.py:74 — it is referenced, on the very next line, as the argument to `_create_test_user(other_user_id)`; all four copies do the same thing, so there is no leftover and no divergence there.
- **proposed:** `def reauthenticate_as_new_user(self) -> ObjectId` on AuthenticatedMongoTestCase in todo/tests/integration/base_mongo_test.py, wrapping the `_create_test_user(ObjectId())` / `_set_auth_cookies()` pair and RETURNING THE PREVIOUS user id, so a test that still needs the original identity has it (the helper mutates self.user_id/self.user_data in place, which is the finder's own stated risk — returning the old id disarms it). Pair each call with the `assertApiError` helper so the three task copies agree on one shape, and give the middleware envelope its own named assertion (`assertMiddlewareForbidden`, keyed on "detail") so the view-vs-middleware split is recorded deliberately instead of by accident.

Better still for WatchlistCheckViewTests, where six of the nine live: hoist it into setUp behind a class-level `@patch("todo.repositories.user_repository.UserRepository.get_by_id")`, then drop the per-method decorator — done in one pass per class, since it renumbers the injected mocks for every method including test_check_missing_task_id and test_check_invalid_task_id, which patch only the user repo.
