## 122. UpdateTaskSerializer single-field tests: ~13 four-line near-copies, two of them byte-identical
- **dimension:** tests | **severity:** medium | **effort:** small | **copies:** 12 | **~lines:** 70
- **sites:** todo/tests/unit/serializers/test_update_task_serializer.py:124-128, todo/tests/unit/serializers/test_update_task_serializer.py:136-140, todo/tests/unit/serializers/test_update_task_serializer.py:72-77, todo/tests/unit/serializers/test_update_task_serializer.py:85-90, todo/tests/unit/serializers/test_update_task_serializer.py:105-110, todo/tests/unit/serializers/test_update_task_serializer.py:118-122, todo/tests/unit/serializers/test_update_task_serializer.py:142-147, todo/tests/unit/serializers/test_update_task_serializer.py:149-154, todo/tests/unit/serializers/test_update_task_serializer.py:167-171, todo/tests/unit/serializers/test_update_task_serializer.py:179-183, todo/tests/unit/serializers/test_update_task_serializer.py:192-198, todo/tests/unit/serializers/test_update_task_serializer.py:200-205
- **drift:** Confirmed. `test_assignee_validation_blank_string_becomes_none` (124-128) and `test_assignee_can_be_null` (136-140) have byte-identical bodies — same `data = {"assignee": None}`, same three assertions. The first test's name promises a blank-string-to-None coercion its body never exercises (it never passes a blank string), so one of the two is a stale copy-paste that has been silently redundant. Assertion strength drifted across the reject family for the same class of failure: `assertEqual` on the exact message at :77, :110, :198; `assertIn` against `str(errors[...])` at :90; `assertIn` on a substring at :147 and :154; and a hardcoded DRF English string at :205 (`'Expected a list of items but got type "str".'`). `serializer = UpdateTaskSerializer(data=data, partial=True)` appears on ~20 lines of this one file.
- **proposed:** Two helpers on UpdateTaskSerializerTests:

`def assertFieldRejected(self, field, value, message, *, exact=True)` — builds `{field: value}`, asserts not valid, asserts field in errors, and compares the message (exact by default, so the assertion strength stops varying per field).
`def assertFieldAccepted(self, field, value, expected=SENTINEL)` — asserts valid and compares `validated_data[field]`.

Then collapse the reject family into one subTest-driven method over a table of `(field, bad value, ValidationErrors constant)` rows and the null/blank family into another. Delete whichever of 124-128 / 136-140 is redundant, or give the blank-string test an actual blank string. Use `subTest(field=...)` so per-field identity survives in the runner output.

## 123. Minimal `TaskModel(...)` literal built four times in one repository test file, one of them dead
- **dimension:** tests | **severity:** low | **effort:** small | **copies:** 4 | **~lines:** 32
- **sites:** todo/tests/unit/repositories/test_task_repository.py:156-164, todo/tests/unit/repositories/test_task_repository.py:168-174, todo/tests/unit/repositories/test_task_repository.py:191-197, todo/tests/unit/repositories/test_task_repository.py:214-220
- **drift:** Confirmed. `self.task` built in TaskRepositoryCreateTests.setUp (156-164) is dead — grep for `self.task` in the file returns exactly one hit, line 156, the assignment itself; all three tests build their own local `task`. And setUp's copy is the only one that passes `labels=[]` and `description="Sample"`, so the abandoned fixture and the live ones no longer agree on the shape. `test_create_task_successfully_inserts_and_returns_task` (166-187) and `test_create_task_creates_counter_if_not_exists` (189-210) are otherwise the same test with displayId "#42" vs "#1", and both are decorated `@patch("todo.repositories.task_repository.TaskRepository.create")` (:166, :189) — they patch the very method they call, so neither exercises any repository code; each asserts only that a Mock returns what it was told to return.
- **proposed:** Reuse the `make_task_model(**overrides)` factory proposed for the service tests (todo/tests/fixtures/task.py) — each site becomes `task = make_task_model(title="Happy path task", priority=TaskPriority.LOW, createdBy="system")`, and the dead `self.task` in setUp becomes obviously redundant and deletable. The two displayId variants then collapse into one subTest over `["#42", "#1"]`. Be honest about what this exposes: because these three tests patch `TaskRepository.create` itself, consolidating them makes it plain they assert nothing about the repository; the real follow-up is to rewrite them against `self.mock_collection` (as TaskRepositoryUpdateTests does) or delete them, which is a bigger change than the deduplication.

## 124. Model "missing required fields" subTest loop duplicated, with a third weaker variant that cannot name the field
- **dimension:** tests | **severity:** medium | **effort:** small | **copies:** 3 | **~lines:** 40
- **sites:** todo/tests/unit/models/test_task.py:20-32, todo/tests/unit/models/test_user.py:22-34, todo/tests/unit/models/test_label.py:20-33
- **drift:** Confirmed. todo/tests/unit/models/test_label.py:20-33 solves the same problem a third way and strictly more weakly: it deletes ALL FOUR required fields at once (`for field_name in required_fields: del incomplete_data[field_name]`) and then counts the resulting errors, so it can never tell you which single field stopped being required — a regression that made `color` optional would still pass. It also uses `del incomplete_data[field_name]` where the other two use `.pop(field, None)`. The ValidationError import drifted with it: test_task.py:3 and test_label.py:6 import from the private `pydantic_core._pydantic_core`, test_user.py:3 imports the public `pydantic.ValidationError`.
- **proposed:** `class RequiredFieldsContractMixin` in a new todo/tests/unit/models/contracts.py, with `model_class`, `valid_data` and `required_fields` class attributes and the one subTest loop, importing `ValidationError` from the public `pydantic` package once. TaskModelTest, UserModelTest and LabelModelTest each declare three attributes and delete their loop. Migrating test_label.py to the per-field form is a real assertion change, not a refactor artifact — it will surface any field on LabelModel that is not in fact required, which is the point.

## 125. "Unauthenticated request returns 401" written five times, three spellings of the client and three of the status
- **dimension:** tests | **severity:** low | **effort:** small | **copies:** 5 | **~lines:** 18
- **sites:** todo/tests/integration/test_task_profile_api.py:13-16, todo/tests/integration/test_user_profile_api.py:11-14, todo/tests/unit/views/test_task.py:1022-1025, todo/tests/unit/views/test_task.py:682-686, todo/tests/unit/views/test_auth.py:253-256
- **drift:** Confirmed, five copies (the finder listed four and correctly flagged the fifth). Client construction has two spellings: `self.client.__class__()` (test_task_profile_api.py:14, test_user_profile_api.py:12) — a copy-paste workaround for not importing APIClient — versus `APIClient()` (test_task.py:1023, test_task.py:684, test_auth.py:254). The expected status has three: `HTTPStatus.UNAUTHORIZED` in the two integration copies, the bare int `401` at test_task.py:1025 and test_auth.py:256, and `status.HTTP_401_UNAUTHORIZED` at test_task.py:686. Three ways of writing one constant across five copies of one three-line test.
- **proposed:** `def assertRequiresAuth(self, url, method="get", **kwargs)` on the shared test base (todo/tests/fixtures/assertions.py, mixed into AuthenticatedMongoTestCase), building a cookie-less `APIClient()` internally and asserting `status.HTTP_401_UNAUTHORIZED`. Each site becomes `self.assertRequiresAuth(self.url + "?profile=true")`, and the PATCH variant at test_task.py:682-686 becomes `self.assertRequiresAuth(self.task_url, method="patch", data={}, format="json")`. Keep it on the authenticated bases only — test_label.py patches `JWTAuthenticationMiddleware._try_authentication` at class level (test_label.py:34), so the helper would not mean what it says there.

## 126. Label query-param error assertions: five methods with one six-line body in a single class
- **dimension:** tests | **severity:** low | **effort:** small | **copies:** 5 | **~lines:** 46
- **sites:** todo/tests/integration/test_get_labels.py:75-82, todo/tests/integration/test_get_labels.py:84-91, todo/tests/integration/test_get_labels.py:93-102, todo/tests/integration/test_get_labels.py:104-111, todo/tests/integration/test_get_labels.py:113-120
- **drift:** The five bodies are confirmed near-identical, but I must correct the finder's characterisation of the drift. It claims the expected-message half "drifted between rows of what is one table" — in fact the rows assert different messages because they trigger different validators: :82 and :111 assert DRF's default "A valid integer is required." for a type failure (get_labels_serializer.py defines no `invalid` override), while :91 and :120 assert the custom min_value messages the serializer does define (get_labels_serializer.py:13, :22). That is correct behaviour, not accidental drift. What IS real: two rows hardcode DRF's English string as a literal instead of referencing a constant, so a DRF upgrade breaks them silently; and :97-102 reads MAX_PAGE_LIMIT from settings at test time while the sibling rows use literals. Also real: the same five-row table is exercised at the unit level in todo/tests/unit/serializers/test_get_labels_serializer.py:31-51, where only two of the five rows appear and they assert through `str(context.exception)` rather than the response envelope.
- **proposed:** One subTest-driven method over a row table, using the shared `assertApiError` helper so the envelope walk is written once:

`rows = [("limit", "invalid", DRF_INVALID_INTEGER), ("limit", 0, ValidationErrors.LIMIT_POSITIVE), ("limit", 1000, f"Ensure this value is less than or equal to {MAX_PAGE_LIMIT}."), ("page", "invalid", DRF_INVALID_INTEGER), ("page", 0, ValidationErrors.PAGE_POSITIVE)]`

with `DRF_INVALID_INTEGER` named once (ideally sourced from `serializers.IntegerField.default_error_messages["invalid"]` rather than retyped) and MAX_PAGE_LIMIT read from settings inside the loop, not frozen at import. The row table is then the only thing that grows when a new query param gets validated.

## 127. `mock_request = MagicMock()` view harness repeated ten times in test_team.py, plus a verbatim-duplicated TeamDTO literal
- **dimension:** tests | **severity:** low | **effort:** small | **copies:** 10 | **~lines:** 45
- **sites:** todo/tests/unit/views/test_team.py:22-32, todo/tests/unit/views/test_team.py:91-101, todo/tests/unit/views/test_team.py:38-39, todo/tests/unit/views/test_team.py:58-59, todo/tests/unit/views/test_team.py:73-74, todo/tests/unit/views/test_team.py:103-105, todo/tests/unit/views/test_team.py:113-115, todo/tests/unit/views/test_team.py:123-125, todo/tests/unit/views/test_team.py:131-133, todo/tests/unit/views/test_team.py:150-151, todo/tests/unit/views/test_team.py:166-167, todo/tests/unit/views/test_team.py:178-179
- **drift:** Confirmed. The harness itself has drifted from every other view test in the repo: test_team.py bypasses the URL router and calls `self.view.get(mock_request)` / `.post(...)` / `.delete(...)` on a hand-instantiated view (test_team.py:15, :86, :141), while every other file in todo/tests/unit/views/ goes through APIClient + reverse(). Two of its three classes construct `self.client = APIClient()` in setUp (test_team.py:14, :85) and then never use it — grep for `self.client` in the file returns exactly those two assignment lines and nothing else, so it is dead scaffolding copied from the sibling files. RemoveTeamMemberViewTests (139-144) dropped the unused client but kept the mock_request idiom, leaving three classes at three points on one drift line.
- **proposed:** Two small factories: `def make_team_dto(**overrides) -> TeamDTO` in a new todo/tests/fixtures/team.py (there is no team fixture module today, which is why the literal was retyped) and `def fake_request(user_id, **attrs) -> MagicMock` in a new todo/tests/fixtures/requests.py. The two TeamDTO literals become `make_team_dto()`; the ten harness blocks become one line each; and the two dead `self.client = APIClient()` lines get deleted in the same pass. The larger cleanup — moving these to APIClient + reverse() like every sibling file — is a scope change, not a mechanical extraction: it would route through JWTAuthenticationMiddleware and TeamAccessMiddleware and require the auth cookie mixin. `fake_request` is the safe half and is worth doing on its own.

## 128. GoogleLoginViewTests: one three-line mock arrange copied into all six tests, and two tests with byte-identical assertion bodies (finder missed this)
- **dimension:** tests | **severity:** medium | **effort:** small | **copies:** 6 | **~lines:** 40

The two JSON tests (32-45 and 47-61) differ only in how the JSON response is requested (`HTTP_ACCEPT="application/json"` at :38 vs `?format=json` at :54) and are otherwise the same test, including the same hardcoded English message literal "Google OAuth URL generated successfully" typed twice (:42, :58) rather than referenced from todo.constants.messages like the sibling logout test does at :212 (`AppMessages.GOOGLE_LOGOUT_SUCCESS`). The `session = self.client.session / session[...] = ... / session.save()` block appears four times (:135-137, :164-166, :180-182, :218-221) with the state value drifting between "correct_state" and "test_state".
- **proposed:** Two changes inside todo/tests/unit/views/test_auth.py, no new module needed:

1. Move the three-line arrange into `GoogleLoginViewTests.setUp` as `self.mock_auth_url` / `self.mock_state`, and apply the `@patch("...GoogleOAuthService.get_authorization_url")` decorator at class level so each test just sets `mock_get_auth_url.return_value = (self.mock_auth_url, self.mock_state)` once in setUp — six copies become zero.
2. `def prime_oauth_session(self, state="test_state")` on the test class, replacing the four session blocks, and one canonical message constant (add `AppMessages.GOOGLE_AUTH_URL_GENERATED` to todo/constants/messages.py) replacing the twice-typed English literal.

Then fold the two JSON tests into one subTest over `[{"HTTP_ACCEPT": "application/json"}, {"query": "?format=json"}]`, since their bodies are already identical.

## 129. Watchlist validate_task_exists failure tests: three copies of one arrange/act/assert, with a fourth accessor style next door
- **dimension:** tests | **severity:** low | **effort:** small | **copies:** 3 | **~lines:** 45
- **sites:** todo/tests/unit/services/test_watchlist_service.py:124-140, todo/tests/unit/services/test_watchlist_service.py:142-158, todo/tests/unit/services/test_watchlist_service.py:160-172
- **drift:** Confirmed. The three copies build their inputs differently for no reason: :130-132 and :148-150 pass `str(ObjectId())` ids into a `CreateWatchlistDTO`, while :162-164 passes raw `ObjectId()` objects and a plain dict `{"isActive": True}` instead of a DTO. The same assertRaises/ApiErrorResponse shape also appears independently in todo/tests/unit/utils/test_task_validation_utils.py:21-31 and :33-44 — testing the very function these three mock out — with a different accessor style: `error_response = context.exception.args[0]` followed by `assertIsInstance` and per-attribute `assertEqual` on statusCode and message, versus `self.assertEqual(context.exception.args[0], error_response)` here. So the same error contract is asserted in two places two ways.
- **proposed:** `def assertRaisesApiError(self, call, *, status_code, message)` on a shared service-test base — runs `call()` inside `assertRaises(ValueError)`, pulls `context.exception.args[0]`, asserts it is an ApiErrorResponse and checks statusCode and message by attribute (the stronger of the two existing styles, matching test_task_validation_utils.py). Plus `def make_watchlist_dto(**overrides) -> CreateWatchlistDTO` in a new todo/tests/fixtures/watchlist.py — there is no watchlist fixture module today, which is exactly why every literal is inline. The three tests collapse into one subTest over `[(400, ApiErrors.INVALID_TASK_ID, add_task_call), (404, ApiErrors.TASK_NOT_FOUND.format(task_id), add_task_call), (400, ApiErrors.INVALID_TASK_ID, update_task_call)]`, with a callable per row so the differing service signature at :160-172 costs one lambda rather than a separate test.

## 130. Boilerplate the shared base already provides: no-op setUp overrides and redundant per-suite collection wipes
- **dimension:** tests | **severity:** low | **effort:** small | **copies:** 16 | **~lines:** 22
- **sites:** todo/tests/integration/test_task_sorting_integration.py:16-17, todo/tests/integration/test_tasks_pagination.py:10-11, todo/tests/unit/views/test_task.py:166-167, todo/tests/unit/views/test_task.py:234-235, todo/tests/integration/test_task_detail_api.py:13-14, todo/tests/integration/test_tasks_delete.py:14-15, todo/tests/integration/test_task_update_api.py:14-15, todo/tests/integration/test_task_defer_api.py:14-15, todo/tests/integration/test_task_profile_api.py:10, todo/tests/integration/test_get_labels.py:15, todo/tests/integration/test_team_update.py:14-15
- **drift:** Confirmed, with corrected counts: I count 12 redundant `delete_many({})` calls in setUp across 7 files (test_team_update.py:14-15, test_task_profile_api.py:10, test_get_labels.py:15, test_task_update_api.py:14-15, test_task_defer_api.py:14-15, test_tasks_delete.py:14-15, test_task_detail_api.py:13-14), not "ten across six". They are inconsistent about which collections they name — tasks+task_details in four files, tasks only in test_task_profile_api.py:10, labels only in test_get_labels.py:15, teams+user_roles in test_team_update.py:14-15 — and test_team_update.py:45,55 then inserts into `db.roles`, which it never clears, relying on the base wipe it evidently does not know about. test_task_profile_api.py wipes db.tasks twice, at setUp:10 and again inside a test at :19. test_task_profile_api.py:11 even carries the comment `# Remove manual user insertion; AuthenticatedMongoTestCase already creates the user` — evidence this cleanup was started and abandoned half-done.
- **proposed:** Mostly deletion, not extraction: remove the four empty setUp overrides and the twelve redundant `delete_many` calls. If any suite genuinely needs isolation beyond the base wipe, express it declaratively — add `collections_to_reset: tuple[str, ...] = ()` as a class attribute on BaseMongoTestCase, honoured by its setUp after the global wipe — so the intent is declared once per suite instead of re-implemented per file. Worth one full integration run afterwards: the base wipe iterates `list_collection_names()`, so a collection created lazily mid-test by production code is not guaranteed to exist at the next setUp (though it also would not need wiping in that case).

## 131. `_handle_validation_errors` redefined in 8 view classes across 3 files, now emitting 4 incompatible 400 bodies
- **dimension:** mechanical | **severity:** high | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 8 | **~lines:** 114
- **sites:** todo/views/task.py:169-195 (TaskListView, canonical), todo/views/task.py:367-393 (TaskUpdateView, byte-identical to the above), todo/views/team.py:102-123 (TeamListView, same code reflowed), todo/views/team.py:127-129 (TeamDetailView, degenerate), todo/views/team.py:319-326 (AddTeamMembersView, drops field name), todo/views/team.py:469-489 (RemoveTeamMemberView, hand-rolled JSON), todo/views/team_creation_invite_code.py:28-30 (GenerateTeamCreationInviteCodeView, degenerate), todo/views/team_creation_invite_code.py:125-127 (VerifyTeamCreationInviteCodeView, degenerate), call sites: todo/views/task.py:143,339; todo/views/team.py:79,215,290,498; todo/views/team_creation_invite_code.py:76,113
- **drift:** Four incompatible wire formats for one condition, all verified: (a) task.py:169 / task.py:367 / team.py:102 -> {statusCode, message, errors:[{source:{parameter:field},title,detail}]}; (b) team.py:127 / tcic:28 / tcic:125 -> raw DRF {"errors": {...}}, no envelope; (c) team.py:319-326 -> envelope with errors=[{"detail": str(error)} for error in errors.values()], field name discarded; (d) team.py:469-489 -> hand-built dict inlining the literals "Invalid value" and "Validation Error" instead of ApiErrors.VALIDATION_ERROR, plus an `authenticated` key no other copy emits. Two copies in the SAME file already disagree (team.py:102 vs team.py:127). The team.py:469 copy also drops the `if isinstance(messages, list)` guard that the canonical copy has, so a non-list DRF error is iterated character by character. Extra defect found during verification, not in the candidate: the @extend_schema decorator at todo/views/team.py:452-468 lands on `_handle_validation_errors` (line 469) rather than on `delete` (line 491), because the helper was pasted into the middle of the class -- the RemoveTeamMemberView.delete endpoint therefore carries no OpenAPI schema.
- **proposed:** Delete all 8 methods and let the already-registered DRF handler do the work. todo/exceptions/exception_handler.py:28-45 `format_validation_errors` already implements this formatting recursively, and todo_project/settings/base.py:102 wires `handle_exception` as EXCEPTION_HANDLER; views in the same codebase already take that route (todo/views/task.py:79, todo/views/label.py:16, todo/views/watchlist.py:57, and 5 more `is_valid(raise_exception=True)` sites). Each call site changes from a 2-line if/return into one line. Where a view genuinely must not raise, add a single module-level `todo/exceptions/responses.py::validation_error_response(errors) -> Response` holding the canonical task.py:169-195 body (the only copy that handles the non-list case) and import it.

## 132. The `raise ValueError(ApiErrorResponse)` / unwrap-in-view protocol is hand-implemented 16 times across 2 layers; only one copy got the `e.args` fix
- **dimension:** mechanical | **severity:** high | **effort:** large | **copies:** 16 | **~lines:** 200
- **sites:** todo/views/task.py:151-167 (unwrap + fallback), todo/views/task.py:349-365 (unwrap + fallback, the ONLY fixed copy), todo/views/team.py:45-57 (unwrap + fallback), todo/views/team.py:88-100 (unwrap + fallback), todo/views/team.py:300-317 (unwrap + fallback, maps to 400), todo/views/watchlist.py:96-108 (unwrap + fallback), todo/views/task.py:433-439 (fallback only, no DEBUG gate), todo/views/team.py:178-184 (fallback only, literal 500), todo/views/task_assignment.py:76-84 (fallback only), todo/views/task_assignment.py:137-145 (fallback only), todo/views/task_assignment.py:300-308 (fallback only), todo/services/task_service.py:655-670 (raise repository-error envelope), todo/services/task_service.py:671-684 (raise server-error envelope), todo/services/watchlist_service.py:49-62 (raise server-error envelope -- MISSED by the finder), todo/services/watchlist_service.py:101-116 (raise repository-error envelope), todo/services/watchlist_service.py:117-130 (raise server-error envelope), central handler that already does this correctly: todo/exceptions/exception_handler.py:249-255
- **drift:** Three independent divergences, all verified by grep of every `args[0]` site. (1) Bare-args crash: 7 of the 8 unwrap sites use `if isinstance(e.args[0], ApiErrorResponse)`, which raises IndexError on a plain `ValueError()` -- todo/views/task.py:152, team.py:46, team.py:89, team.py:301, watchlist.py:97, and the two service copies at task_service.py:656 and watchlist_service.py:102. todo/views/task.py:350 is the single fixed copy: `if isinstance(e, ValueError) and e.args and isinstance(e.args[0], ApiErrorResponse)`. The central handler at exception_handler.py:250 carries the same `exc.args and` guard, confirming the fix is the intended form and was never propagated. (2) Debug leak: 9 fallbacks gate the detail on settings.DEBUG, but todo/views/task.py:437 emits `errors=[{"detail": str(e)}]` unconditionally (leaks exception internals in production) while todo/views/watchlist.py:105 went the other way with `errors=[{"detail": ApiErrors.INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR}]`, never showing str(e) even in DEBUG. (3) Status inconsistency: team.py:184 and team.py:317 return the literal `status=500` where siblings use `status.HTTP_500_INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR`, and team.py:300-310 maps the ValueError branch to 400 with `message=str(e)` where every other copy maps it to 500. The finder missed watchlist_service.py:49-62, a third byte-identical copy of the server-error envelope.
- **proposed:** Introduce a real exception type instead of smuggling a response object inside ValueError. Add `todo/exceptions/api_exception.py::ApiError(Exception)` carrying an `ApiErrorResponse`, plus two factories `server_error(e) -> ApiError` and `repository_error(e) -> ApiError` that hold the envelope currently hand-built 5 times in task_service/watchlist_service. Add one `isinstance(exc, ApiError)` branch to `todo/exceptions/exception_handler.py:handle_exception` (next to the existing ValueError branch at :249) and delete all 11 view-layer try/except blocks -- the registered handler already produces the identical body. Order of work matters: fix the dead branches in handle_exception first (see the exception_handler finding), because removing the per-view catches changes which exceptions reach it. Audit the non-envelope `raise ValueError(...)` sites (e.g. task_service.py:400 `raise ValueError(f"Team not found: {assignee_id}")`) before flipping the protocol.

## 133. `handle_exception` tests four exception types twice each -- 4 unreachable branches whose bodies disagree with their live twins -- and pastes the same response tail 6 times
- **dimension:** mechanical | **severity:** high | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 8 | **~lines:** 95
- **sites:** todo/exceptions/exception_handler.py:56-64 (TokenExpiredError, LIVE, no early return), todo/exceptions/exception_handler.py:114-129 (TokenExpiredError, UNREACHABLE), todo/exceptions/exception_handler.py:65-80 (TokenMissingError, LIVE), todo/exceptions/exception_handler.py:98-113 (TokenMissingError, UNREACHABLE), todo/exceptions/exception_handler.py:81-96 (TokenInvalidError, LIVE), todo/exceptions/exception_handler.py:130-145 (TokenInvalidError, UNREACHABLE), todo/exceptions/exception_handler.py:173-181 (UserNotFoundException, LIVE), todo/exceptions/exception_handler.py:192-200 (UserNotFoundException, UNREACHABLE), duplicated response tail: todo/exceptions/exception_handler.py:74-80, 90-96, 107-113, 123-129, 139-145, and a 6th near-copy at 290-295
- **drift:** Which twin wins is an accident of ordering, and it produces an observable inconsistency. The LIVE TokenExpiredError branch (:56-64) only appends to error_list and falls through to the tail at :290-295, which does NOT set authenticated=False; its unreachable twin (:114-129) returns early WITH authenticated=False. The LIVE TokenMissingError (:65-80) and TokenInvalidError (:81-96) both return early with authenticated=False. Since `ApiErrorResponse.authenticated` is `bool | None = None` (todo/dto/responses/error_response.py:23) and every dump uses `exclude_none=True`, an expired-token 401 omits `authenticated` entirely while a missing-token or invalid-token 401 includes `authenticated: false` -- for no reason anyone chose. The UserNotFoundException pair also differs: the live copy at :176 uses `source={ApiErrorSource.PARAMETER: "user_id"}`, the dead copy at :195 uses `source={ApiErrorSource.PATH: "user_id"} if user_id else None`, which is exactly the form used by the neighbouring live TaskNotFoundException branch at :185 -- strong evidence the dead copy is the newer, intended one.
- **proposed:** Replace the chain with a lookup table plus one builder, both in todo/exceptions/exception_handler.py: `_AUTH_EXC_MAP: dict[type[Exception], tuple[int, str]]` mapping exception class to (status_code, title), and `_build_error_response(status_code, error_list, authenticated=None) -> Response` holding the 6-line tail once. Delete the four unreachable branches after deciding per pair which body is correct -- the evidence points to keeping the dead UserNotFound body (PATH source, matching its TaskNotFound sibling) and to making the expired-token branch return `authenticated=False` like its two peers. Both are observable API changes that todo/tests/unit/exceptions/test_exception_handler.py likely pins, and the `message=str(exc) if not error_list else error_list[0].detail` expression is subtle enough that it must be moved verbatim.

## 134. The task creator-or-assignee authorization rule is re-implemented 5 times, spanning the service/repository boundary and reaching through a private method
- **dimension:** mechanical | **severity:** medium | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 5 | **~lines:** 30
- **sites:** todo/services/task_service.py:298-303 (update_task), todo/services/task_service.py:381-386 (update_task_with_assignee_from_dict), todo/services/task_service.py:473-478 (update_task_with_assignee), todo/services/task_service.py:549-554 (defer_task), todo/repositories/task_repository.py:274-279 (delete_by_id)
- **drift:** The rule itself has not yet diverged -- which is the argument for extracting now, before one of the five is patched alone. Two structural problems already travel with the clone. First, a layering violation repeated four times: the service reaches into `TaskRepository._get_assigned_task_ids_for_user`, an underscore-private repository method (todo/repositories/task_repository.py:117), at task_service.py:301, :384, :476 and :552; grep confirms those are the only cross-layer callers of any underscore-private repository method. Second, the two layers read different sources for the same field -- the service reads the parsed model's `current_task.createdBy` (declared `createdBy: str` at todo/models/task.py:39) while the repository reads the raw Mongo document via `task.get("createdBy")`, so any change to how the field is stored breaks one side silently. Note: the candidate's claim that the id comparison uses two different type contracts does NOT hold -- `PyObjectId` subclasses `bson.ObjectId` and its validator returns `ObjectId(value)` (todo/models/common/pyobjectid.py:4-15), so `current_task.id` and the `task_id` parameter are both ObjectId and compare identically.
- **proposed:** One authorization helper beside the existing todo/utils/task_validation_utils.py: `todo/utils/task_permissions.py::assert_can_modify_task(task: TaskModel, user_id: str) -> None`, holding the rule and raising `PermissionError(ApiErrors.UNAUTHORIZED_TITLE)`. Promote `_get_assigned_task_ids_for_user` to a public `TaskRepository.get_assigned_task_ids_for_user` (5 internal callers at task_repository.py:84, :117, :162, :277, :360 plus the 4 service callers) so the helper is not reaching through a private door. Do NOT delete the repository-side copy until every delete path routes through the service: todo/views/task.py:247 calls TaskService.delete_task, which at task_service.py:688 delegates straight to TaskRepository.delete_by_id without checking, so the repository check is currently the only guard on deletes. Either move the check up into delete_task (loading the model first, as the other four service methods do) or have delete_by_id call the same helper.

## 135. Every repository hand-builds a Mongo->Postgres field map that DualWriteService immediately re-maps, then pastes the same 21-copy dual-write epilogue with a function-local `import logging`
- **dimension:** mechanical | **severity:** high | **effort:** large | **copies:** 21 | **~lines:** 215
- **sites:** todo/repositories/team_repository.py:27-48, 157-161, 202-206, 311-315, todo/repositories/task_repository.py:225-251, 323-348, todo/repositories/watchlist_repository.py:44-63, 364-368, todo/repositories/task_assignment_repository.py:44-48, 154-158, 226-230, 289-293, 351-355, todo/repositories/user_repository.py:87-91, todo/repositories/user_role_repository.py:43-62, 159-163, todo/repositories/audit_log_repository.py:38-42, todo/repositories/team_creation_invite_code_repository.py:54-58, 88-92, todo/repositories/user_team_details_repository.py:61-65, matching transforms: todo/services/dual_write_service.py:237-264 (_transform_task_data), :266-278 (_transform_team_data), and 9 more
- **drift:** Four verified divergences. (1) Key convention decided per entity: teams and watchlists emit snake_case (team_repository.py:29-37, watchlist_repository.py:46-52) matching _transform_team_data/_transform_watchlist_data, while tasks emit camelCase -- task_repository.py:227-240 sends displayId, isAcknowledged, startedAt, dueAt, createdAt, createdBy -- because _transform_task_data (dual_write_service.py:249-263) reads camelCase. Two conventions for one interface. (2) Task labels never sync: _transform_task_data returns `"labels": data.get("labels", [])` (dual_write_service.py:263) and create_document pops it to drive _sync_task_labels (dual_write_service.py:73-80), but neither task payload includes a `labels` key, so the pop always yields [] and the label sync is dead. (3) The two task payloads disagree with each other: task_repository.py:236 writes `"createdAt": task.createdAt or datetime.now(timezone.utc)` while the update copy at :334 writes the bare `"createdAt": task_model.createdAt`. (4) The epilogue's function-local `import logging` is now provably redundant in at least one place: todo/repositories/user_role_repository.py already imports logging at module level (line 3), yet line 59 re-imports it inside the function anyway -- pure copy-paste residue. 21 blocks, 21 chances to word the warning differently, and they do ("Failed to sync task" / "Failed to sync task update" / "Failed to sync task assignment deactivation" ...).
- **proposed:** Give DualWriteService the model and let it own the column names end to end. Add `sync_create(collection_name: str, model: BaseModel) -> None`, `sync_update(...)`, `sync_delete(...)` to todo/services/enhanced_dual_write_service.py; each calls the existing _transform_*_data (fixed to take `model.model_dump()` as its single input contract), then logs the failure once using a module-level logger. Delete the 11 repository-side payload dicts and all 21 epilogue blocks. Migrate the transforms as a set, not piecemeal -- each currently reads whatever key convention its matching repository happens to emit, so a partial migration writes NULLs silently. Confirm the `labels` gap is a bug (it is: the key is produced by the transform and consumed by create_document, but no caller supplies it) before the refactor changes that behaviour.

## 136. `PaginationConfig` defined three times and pagination re-validated in three layers, with three different limits and three wordings of the same message
- **dimension:** mechanical | **severity:** high | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 3 | **~lines:** 110
- **sites:** todo/services/label_service.py:14-19 (@dataclass, DEFAULT_LIMIT hardcoded 10), todo/services/task_service.py:51-55 (@dataclass), todo/services/watchlist_service.py:23-26 (plain class, no @dataclass), todo/services/task_service.py:139-157 (_build_pagination_links + build_page_url), todo/services/watchlist_service.py:189-208 (_build_pagination_links + build_page_url), todo/services/label_service.py:64-83 (prepare_pagination_links + build_page_url), todo/services/task_service.py:127-135 (_validate_pagination_params), todo/services/user_service.py:98-114 (_validate_search_params), todo/serializers/get_labels_serializer.py:8-24, todo/serializers/get_tasks_serializer.py:15-31, todo/serializers/get_watchlist_tasks_serializer.py:8-24
- **drift:** Four divergences, all verified (candidate line numbers were off by one in three places; corrected here). (1) todo/services/label_service.py:17 hardcodes `DEFAULT_LIMIT: int = 10` while the other two read DEFAULT_PAGE_LIMIT (=20, todo_project/settings/base.py:104), so /labels silently paginates at a different size, and todo/serializers/get_labels_serializer.py:18 hardcodes `default=10` to match, entrenching it. (2) watchlist_service.py:23 is a plain class while the other two carry @dataclass. (3) Total pages computed two ways: `math.ceil(total_count / limit)` at task_service.py:141 and watchlist_service.py:192, versus the integer-safe `(total_count + limit - 1) // limit` at label_service.py:32 and team_creation_invite_code_service.py. (4) One message, four spellings: the constants exist as ValidationErrors.PAGE_POSITIVE / LIMIT_POSITIVE / MAX_LIMIT_EXCEEDED (todo/constants/messages.py:71-73) and get_labels_serializer.py:13,21 uses them, but task_service.py:129-135 retypes the identical English as string literals, get_tasks_serializer.py:20 and :29 invent a lowercase third wording ("page must be greater than or equal to 1"), and todo/services/user_service.py:110 hardcodes `if limit > 100` with `MAX_LIMIT_EXCEEDED.format(100)` instead of PaginationConfig.MAX_LIMIT (=200) -- so /users caps at 100 and /tasks at 200 with nothing stating why.
- **proposed:** One `todo/constants/pagination.py::PaginationConfig` imported by all three services (keeping label's extra SEARCH default as a per-service concern, not a fourth config class), and one `todo/utils/pagination.py::build_pagination_links(route_name: str, page: int, limit: int, total_count: int, **extra_params) -> LinksData` owning the integer-safe total_pages computation and the reverse_lazy+urlencode construction, so each service passes only its route name and extra query keys. Bounds checking should live in the serializer layer only, sourcing error_messages from ValidationErrors; task_service._validate_pagination_params (127-135) and user_service._validate_search_params (98-114) then become redundant -- but confirm every paginated entry point goes through a serializer first, because team_creation_invite_code_service.get_all_codes currently does not. Treat the two default changes (/labels 10->20, /users max 100->200) as deliberate product decisions, not silent normalization.

## 137. `update_task_with_assignee` and `update_task_with_assignee_from_dict` are a 90-line clone; only the live one got the deferred-task rules
- **dimension:** mechanical | **severity:** high | **effort:** small | **copies:** 2 | **~lines:** 78
- **sites:** todo/services/task_service.py:370-460 (update_task_with_assignee_from_dict, the only one with a production caller), todo/services/task_service.py:462-540 (update_task_with_assignee, no production caller)
- **drift:** The _from_dict copy grew two deferral rules the DTO copy never received, todo/services/task_service.py:433-441:

        if (validated_data.get("status") is not None
                and validated_data.get("status") != TaskStatus.DEFERRED.value
                and current_task.deferredDetails):
            update_payload["deferredDetails"] = None

        if validated_data.get("status") == TaskStatus.DEFERRED.value:
            update_payload["status"] = current_task.status

So moving a deferred task to another status clears deferredDetails on one path and leaves it stale on the other. The two also seed the loop differently: the dict version iterates validated_data.items() with an explicit `if field == "assignee": continue` (task_service.py:408-410), the DTO version pre-filters via `dto.model_dump(exclude_none=True, exclude={"assignee", "createdBy"})` (task_service.py:498), so the DTO path cannot express "set this field to null" at all. grep confirms the split: todo/views/task.py:343 is the only production caller and it calls the _from_dict variant; `update_task_with_assignee` is referenced only from todo/tests/unit/services/test_task_service.py (6 call sites at :871, :908, :922, :937, :954, :971, :995). The copy that is dead in production is the copy that never got the fix.
- **proposed:** Delete `update_task_with_assignee` (todo/services/task_service.py:462-540) outright and delete or repoint its 6 unit tests -- it has no production caller and its only distinguishing behaviour is the absence of two bug fixes. If a DTO-shaped entry point is still wanted, keep it as a two-line adapter that normalizes to a dict and delegates. The adapter is not a drop-in as written: `exclude_none=True` must be reconciled with the dict path's ability to write nulls first, otherwise a DTO caller silently loses the ability to clear a field.

## 138. Sync-metadata block copied into all 11 Postgres models with no abstract base; `updated_at` already has two definitions and `save()` has two bodies
- **dimension:** mechanical | **severity:** medium | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 11 | **~lines:** 185
- **sites:** todo/models/postgres/task.py:37-48, todo/models/postgres/team.py:31-42, todo/models/postgres/team.py:84-95, todo/models/postgres/user.py:24-35, todo/models/postgres/label.py:22-33, todo/models/postgres/role.py:22-33, todo/models/postgres/task_assignment.py:30-41, todo/models/postgres/watchlist.py:25-36, todo/models/postgres/user_role.py:16-26, todo/models/postgres/audit_log.py:21-31, todo/models/postgres/team_creation_invite_code.py:27-38, save() overrides: task.py:65-69, team.py:56-60, team.py:111-115, user.py:49-53, label.py:46-50, role.py:46-50, task_assignment.py:58-62, watchlist.py:55-59, team_creation_invite_code.py:53-56 (MISSED by the finder)
- **drift:** Three verified divergences. (1) The field that should be identical is declared two ways: `updated_at = models.DateTimeField(null=True, blank=True)` in task.py:31, user.py:22, label.py:20, role.py:20, task_assignment.py:24, watchlist.py:23 versus `updated_at = models.DateTimeField(default=timezone.now)` in team.py:29 and team.py:82 -- so 2 of the 8 timestamped tables get a non-null updated_at at insert and 6 get NULL, purely by which file was written when. (2) A 9th save() copy the candidate missed, todo/models/postgres/team_creation_invite_code.py:53-56, is the same block with the `self.updated_at = timezone.now()` line removed (that model has no updated_at) -- a hand-edited variant of a pasted block. PostgresUserRole and PostgresAuditLog carry the sync fields but no save() at all. (3) One bug replicated 8 times and unfixable in one place as written: every save() copy overwrites `self.created_at = timezone.now()` on insert, discarding the created_at that DualWriteService._transform_*_data carefully copies out of Mongo (e.g. dual_write_service.py:275 `"created_at": data.get("created_at")`). The literal "SYNCED" appears 49 times across the codebase.
- **proposed:** An abstract base in todo/models/postgres/base.py: `class PostgresSyncedModel(models.Model)` holding mongo_id, last_sync_at, sync_status, sync_error with `class Meta: abstract = True`, plus SYNC_STATUS_CHOICES hoisted to a module constant (and the bare "SYNCED" literal moved into todo/constants/ beside it). A second abstract mixin `TimestampedModel` carries the agreed created_at/updated_at pair and the save() override defined once, so the created_at-clobbering bug is fixed in one place. Two cautions: Meta.indexes is not inherited additively, so each child must re-declare the shared indexes in its own Meta (`indexes = PostgresSyncedModel.Meta.indexes + [...]`) or they are silently dropped; and Django will generate migrations even where the SQL is unchanged, with index names regenerated. Reconciling updated_at is a genuine schema change on postgres_teams and postgres_user_team_details.

## 139. "Try ObjectId, then try the raw string" query fallback pasted 7 times in 2 repositories, in 4 mutually inconsistent forms, one of which is broken
- **dimension:** mechanical | **severity:** high | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 7 | **~lines:** 110
- **sites:** todo/repositories/task_assignment_repository.py:57-67 (find_one form), todo/repositories/task_assignment_repository.py:76-88 (find form -- BROKEN, cursor exhaustion), todo/repositories/task_assignment_repository.py:112-130 (update_many, both fired unconditionally), todo/repositories/task_assignment_repository.py:183-205 (update_one, second guarded on modified_count), todo/repositories/task_assignment_repository.py:308-330 (update_many, second guarded on modified_count), todo/repositories/user_team_details_repository.py:11-32 (cartesian form), todo/repositories/user_team_details_repository.py:36-50 (cartesian form, near-byte-identical to the above)
- **drift:** Four incompatible implementations of one idea, and one is a live bug. todo/repositories/task_assignment_repository.py:79-87 probes the cursor with `if not list(task_assignments_data):` -- which consumes it -- and then returns `[TaskAssignmentModel(**data) for data in task_assignments_data]` over the now-exhausted cursor, so whenever the ObjectId form MATCHES the method returns []; its find_one sibling at :57-67 has no such problem because find_one returns a document, not a cursor. Beyond that: update_assignment (:112-130) fires both update_many calls unconditionally, while :183-205 and :308-330 guard the second on `result.modified_count == 0`; and :183-205 uses update_one where the structurally identical :308-330 uses update_many, over an identical filter and identical $set. The two user_team_details copies are byte-identical for their first 20 lines yet neither shares with the other. The `except Exception: user_id_obj = user_id` swallow also degrades a genuinely malformed id into a string probe instead of erroring. The cursor bug feeds _get_assigned_task_ids_for_user (task_repository.py:119) and therefore the task permission checks in the finding above.
- **proposed:** One id-normalization module, todo/utils/object_id.py, with `as_object_id(value) -> ObjectId | None` and `id_query(field: str, value) -> dict` returning `{field: {"$in": [ObjectId(v), v]}}` so a single query matches both storage forms instead of two round trips plus hand-rolled retry. That collapses each of the 7 sites to one expression and eliminates the cursor-exhaustion class of bug structurally. Two caveats to respect: `$in` does not preserve the current defined preference for the ObjectId form when both exist for one logical id, and fixing get_by_assignee_id is a behaviour change (callers that receive [] today would start receiving assignments), so pin it with a test on both the ObjectId-stored and string-stored cases first. The real fix underneath is to normalize the stored ids so the fallback is unnecessary; this helper is the single place to put that deprecation once the data migration lands.

## 140. `TeamDTO` constructed field-by-field 6 times in one service, then un-built by `pop("invite_code")` in 5 view sites
- **dimension:** mechanical | **severity:** medium | **effort:** small | **copies:** 6 | **~lines:** 65
- **sites:** todo/services/team_service.py:145-155, todo/services/team_service.py:212-222, todo/services/team_service.py:247-257, todo/services/team_service.py:314-324, todo/services/team_service.py:371-381, todo/services/team_service.py:466-476, redaction sites: todo/views/team.py:42, :169, :225, :252, :297
- **drift:** The six copies have not yet diverged from each other, but the drift is already visible at the boundary they create. Because every copy unconditionally includes invite_code -- a field the API deliberately hides -- the view layer has to strip it back out, and grep of todo/views/team.py finds FIVE such sites, not the two the candidate claimed: line 42 (`for team in data.get("teams", []): team.pop("invite_code", None)`), and lines 169, 225, 252, 297 each doing `data.pop("invite_code", None)`. Meanwhile todo/views/team.py:361 deliberately returns it (`return Response({"invite_code": team.invite_code})`). So one redaction decision is re-made in five places against six construction sites, and whether the field leaks depends on which construction site fed which view path -- exactly the failure mode a shared constructor prevents. Five copies of a redaction is also five chances to forget one.
- **proposed:** `TeamService.prepare_team_dto(cls, team: TeamModel, include_invite_code: bool = False) -> TeamDTO` in todo/services/team_service.py, mirroring the existing prepare_task_dto / prepare_label_dto naming, called from all six sites. With the flag defaulting to False the five `pop("invite_code", None)` calls in todo/views/team.py delete themselves and the redaction decision lives in one place. Only todo/views/team.py:350-361 (the endpoint that deliberately returns the code to creators and POCs) passes include_invite_code=True. Check each of the five current pop sites before flipping the default, since any client receiving the field today from a path that forgot to pop would stop seeing it.

## 141. "This field must be a valid ObjectId" written 11 times across models, DTOs and serializers, with five different rejection messages and two opposite null contracts
- **dimension:** mechanical | **severity:** medium | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 11 | **~lines:** 65
- **sites:** todo/models/team.py:37-43 (TeamModel.validate_object_id), todo/models/team_creation_invite_code.py:25-31 (byte-identical body), todo/models/task_assignment.py:29-35 (byte-identical body), todo/models/team.py:10-17 (ObjectIdValidatorMixin.validate_object_id -- opposite null semantics), todo/models/team.py:61-63 (UserTeamDetailsModel delegates to the mixin), todo/serializers/create_task_assignment_serializer.py:13-16, 18-21, todo/serializers/remove_from_team_serializer.py:10-13, 15-18, todo/serializers/update_team_serializer.py:15-18, todo/dto/task_assignment_dto.py:13-18, 20-25, 34-39, todo/dto/task_dto.py:59-63, user_type duplicate: todo/models/task_assignment.py:37-41, todo/dto/task_assignment_dto.py:27-32, todo/serializers/create_task_assignment_serializer.py:23-26
- **drift:** Five wordings for one condition, all verified: (1) ValidationErrors.INVALID_OBJECT_ID = "{0} is not a valid ObjectId." (todo/constants/messages.py:64) used by the 5 serializer copies and todo/dto/task_dto.py:62; (2) f"Invalid ObjectId: {v}" in the three model copies and in todo/models/common/pyobjectid.py:14; (3) f"Invalid Object ID format: {v}" in the mixin at todo/models/team.py:16; (4) three ad-hoc DTO messages -- "Invalid task ID: {value}", "Invalid assignee ID: {value}", "Invalid original team ID: {value}" (todo/dto/task_assignment_dto.py:17, :24, :38) despite the file being one import away from the constant; (5) a bare {"message": "Invalid task_id"} at todo/views/watchlist.py:174. Null handling is outright contradictory inside ONE file: ObjectIdValidatorMixin.validate_object_id (todo/models/team.py:10-17) REJECTS None with "Object ID cannot be None" and uses PyObjectId.is_valid, while the validate_object_id defined 20 lines below it at :37-43 ACCEPTS None and uses ObjectId.is_valid -- and `class TeamModel(Document, ObjectIdValidatorMixin)` (line 20) inherits the mixin while overriding that exact method name, so which semantics apply depends on which field you look at. UserTeamDetailsModel (team.py:61-63) delegates to the mixin and so rejects None where its sibling models allow it. Separately the user_type check is written three times verbatim (`if v not in ["user", "team"]`) and is dead in two of them: the DTO field is already `user_type: Literal["user", "team"]` (task_assignment_dto.py:10) and the serializer field is already `ChoiceField(choices=["user", "team"])` (create_task_assignment_serializer.py:9-11).
- **proposed:** The project is on pydantic 2.10.1 (requirements.txt:14), so express the rule as a type rather than a method. In todo/models/common/pyobjectid.py -- which already hosts PyObjectId -- add `ObjectIdField = Annotated[PyObjectId, BeforeValidator(_coerce_object_id)]` and `OptionalObjectIdField` for the nullable case, so nullability lives in the type instead of an `if v is None` each copy decides for itself, and delete the three model validators plus the now-dead ObjectIdValidatorMixin. For the serializer layer, one `todo/serializers/fields.py::ObjectIdSerializerField(serializers.CharField)` whose to_internal_value raises ValidationErrors.INVALID_OBJECT_ID, replacing the 5 validate_* methods. Delete the three redundant user_type validators outright. Apply the annotated type field by field, not wholesale: making created_by reject None where it currently coerces could reject documents already in Mongo, and the message text change will be visible to tests and clients parsing `detail`.

## 142. `_get_cookie_config` defined twice under the same name in unrelated classes, disagreeing on the security-relevant `httponly` key
- **dimension:** mechanical | **severity:** medium | **effort:** small | **copies:** 2 | **~lines:** 18
- **sites:** todo/views/auth.py:135-142 (GoogleCallbackView), todo/middlewares/jwt_auth.py:135-143 (JWT auth middleware), partial third copy: todo/views/auth.py:188-191 (delete_config)
- **drift:** The two copies disagree on exactly one key and it is the security-relevant one. COOKIE_HTTPONLY is a real, env-driven setting -- todo_project/settings/base.py:150 reads `os.getenv("COOKIE_HTTPONLY", "True").lower() == "true"`. The login path honours it; the silent-refresh path in the middleware ignores it and always sends httponly=True. So the same access-token cookie carries different flags depending on whether it was minted at login or on refresh, and a deployment that sets COOKIE_HTTPONLY=False gets behaviour it never asked for on one of the two paths. Nothing in either file explains the difference, and the shared private method name is strong evidence one was pasted from the other. A third partial copy at todo/views/auth.py:188-191 repeats the path/domain pair that delete_cookie must match for logout to actually clear the cookie -- if the set-side path or domain ever changes and that copy does not, logout silently fails.
- **proposed:** A single `todo/utils/cookie_utils.py::auth_cookie_config() -> dict` returning the five keys from settings.COOKIE_SETTINGS, imported by both call sites, plus `auth_cookie_delete_config() -> dict` returning the path/domain subset that _clear_auth_cookies needs -- deriving the delete kwargs from the same source keeps them in lockstep with the set kwargs. Resolving the httponly disagreement is a deliberate security decision, not a mechanical merge: adopting the setting means a misconfigured COOKIE_HTTPONLY=False would newly apply to refreshed cookies, while hardcoding True everywhere overrides documented config. Hardcoding True is the safer default, but then COOKIE_HTTPONLY becomes dead config and should be removed from base.py:150, .env.example and both CI workflows rather than left misleading.

## 143. `_prepare_label_dtos` duplicated between two services; one converts ids to ObjectId, the other does not, and its type hint is wrong
- **dimension:** mechanical | **severity:** medium | **effort:** small | **copies:** 2 | **~lines:** 24
- **sites:** todo/services/task_service.py:203-214, todo/services/watchlist_service.py:140-152
- **drift:** The watchlist copy carries a conversion line with an exclamatory inline comment -- `# Convert here!` at todo/services/watchlist_service.py:142 -- that reads exactly like a fix applied at one call site and never carried back. The shared callee declares `list_by_ids(cls, ids: List[ObjectId])` and queries `{"_id": {"$in": ids}}` (todo/repositories/label_repository.py:12-18), where Mongo will not match string ids against ObjectId _id values, so the conversion is load-bearing. The task copy works only by accident: TaskModel.labels is declared `List[PyObjectId]` (todo/models/task.py:32), so it is already handing over ObjectIds -- which makes its own `label_ids: List[str]` annotation a lie and actively misleading to anyone who tries to reuse it. Two copies of one function with silently different input contracts, documented by neither.
- **proposed:** One `LabelService.prepare_label_dtos(label_ids: Iterable[str | ObjectId]) -> list[LabelDTO]` in todo/services/label_service.py -- which already owns the singular prepare_label_dto at :86 -- normalizing ids through the shared `as_object_id` helper so callers may pass either form, and delete both private copies. Both call sites (todo/services/task_service.py:161 and todo/services/watchlist_service.py:156) already guard with `if ... labels else []`, so the change is contained. The one trap: the normalizing version must pass PyObjectId/ObjectId instances through unchanged rather than re-wrapping, or the task path regresses.

## 144. `migrate_labels` and `migrate_roles` are the same 16-line command running the same function; the labels/roles sync pair is copy-pasted again in the Postgres sync service
- **dimension:** mechanical | **severity:** medium | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 2 | **~lines:** 95
- **sites:** todo/management/commands/migrate_labels.py:1-16, todo/management/commands/migrate_roles.py:1-16, todo/services/postgres_sync_service.py:128-180 (_sync_labels_table), todo/services/postgres_sync_service.py:182-228 (_sync_roles_table), todo_project/db/migrations.py:12-132 (migrate_fixed_labels), todo_project/db/migrations.py:135-206 (migrate_predefined_roles)
- **drift:** Both commands call the identical `run_all_migrations()`, so `migrate_labels` and `migrate_roles` do exactly the same thing and neither is scoped to what its name promises -- and they have already drifted cosmetically, with migrate_labels.py wrapping output in self.style.SUCCESS/ERROR while migrate_roles.py:9-16 dropped the styling entirely. In postgres_sync_service.py the labels copy logs two extra lines the roles copy lacks (`logger.info(f"Will sync {mongo_count - postgres_count} labels to PostgreSQL")` at :144 and a logger.debug at :164) and filters `collection.find({"isDeleted": {"$ne": True}})` at :148 where the roles copy passes `{}` at :201. In todo_project/db/migrations.py the divergence is functional: the labels seeder validates and then inserts the RAW dict, discarding the validated model (`LabelModel(**label_document)` at :106 with the result thrown away, then `insert_one(label_document)` at :111), while the roles seeder inserts the validated output (`validated_doc = validated_role.model_dump(mode="json", by_alias=True, exclude_none=True)` at :191, then insert_one(validated_doc) at :193) -- so pydantic coercion and aliasing apply to roles but not to labels. The labels dedupe query also filters `"isDeleted": {"$ne": True}` (:86) where the roles query filters on scope instead (:171).
- **proposed:** Collapse the two commands into one `seed_reference_data` command, keeping migrate_labels/migrate_roles as thin aliases that import and call it, since removing a management command breaks deploy scripts and runbooks. In todo/services/postgres_sync_service.py extract `_sync_table(self, table_name: str, collection_name: str, model_cls, transform, mongo_filter: dict | None = None) -> bool` and drive it from the `sync_operations` list already sitting at :37-40 -- that list is the natural home for the per-entity config and already names exactly these two entities. Treat todo_project/db/migrations.py separately and more carefully: it is the weakest of the three pairs, because the two seeders use different dedupe keys (name+isDeleted vs name+scope) and different document schemas, so a `_seed_collection` helper would need a dedupe-query callback that risks costing more than it saves. The validate-then-discard bug in the labels seeder is worth fixing on its own merits first, and standardizing on inserting the validated model changes what actually lands in the labels collection, so diff the resulting documents before shipping it.

## 145. `AuthErrorMessages` declares TOKEN_EXPIRED and TOKEN_INVALID twice each with different text; the second silently wins
- **dimension:** mechanical | **severity:** medium | **effort:** small | **copies:** 2 | **~lines:** 6
- **sites:** todo/constants/messages.py:94 and :99 (TOKEN_EXPIRED), todo/constants/messages.py:95 and :101 (TOKEN_INVALID), todo/constants/messages.py:55 and :84 (SEARCH_QUERY_EMPTY, two classes), todo/services/user_service.py:102 (same string hardcoded a third time)
- **drift:** Python keeps the last binding, so lines 94 and 95 are dead and every consumer -- todo/utils/jwt_utils.py:73 and :93, todo/middlewares/jwt_auth.py:118 -- receives "Access token has expired" and "Invalid token" while the constants file appears to offer "Authentication token has expired" and "Invalid authentication token". Anyone opening the file to find the user-facing wording reads the wrong line, and a future edit to line 94 would change nothing while looking correct in review. The shape -- two names, each bound twice, inside one 13-line class, with the second block also introducing REFRESH_TOKEN_EXPIRED -- is a merge that stacked two versions of the same block rather than reconciling them. The cross-class SEARCH_QUERY_EMPTY duplication has the same signature: one string, two homes, plus a hardcoded third copy.
- **proposed:** Delete the shadowed lines 94 and 95 after making a deliberate choice about which wording is correct -- the deletion is the easy half, but the dead lines may be the intended text that someone later reinstates without noticing they were previously shadowed. Then make recurrence impossible: convert these message classes in todo/constants/messages.py to `enum.StrEnum`, which raises TypeError on a duplicate member name at import time instead of silently rebinding. That is the larger change since it touches every `.format()` call site on these constants; a `ruff` rule for class-body redefinition (F811-style) is the cheap alternative. For SEARCH_QUERY_EMPTY keep one definition (ValidationErrors, since it is a field-validation message), delete ApiErrors.SEARCH_QUERY_EMPTY, and point todo/services/user_service.py:102 at the survivor.

## 146. Three competing error mechanisms for role views -- an unused decorator, a static-method class, and a base view that reimplements the decorator -- plus a 4-copy dual-write guard
- **dimension:** mechanical | **severity:** medium | **effort:** medium | **copies:** 4 | **~lines:** 50
- **sites:** todo/exceptions/global_exception_handler.py:16-36 (handle_exceptions decorator -- zero callers), todo/views/role.py:21-41 (BaseRoleView._handle_exceptions -- live), todo/exceptions/global_exception_handler.py:39-68 (GlobalExceptionHandler static methods), todo/services/enhanced_dual_write_service.py:24-26, 34-36, 44-46, 54-56 (identical guard)
- **drift:** grep for `handle_exceptions` across todo/ returns no callers -- todo/views/role.py:11 imports only GlobalExceptionHandler, never the decorator -- so the codebase carries two implementations of one policy and uses the more verbose one, while the terser one rots unexercised. GlobalExceptionHandler.handle_validation_error (:57-61) is likewise never called. Both surviving implementations hardcode the literal "Internal server error" (global_exception_handler.py:33 and :67) even though ApiErrors.INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR holds exactly that string and is used for the same purpose everywhere else in the project. And the whole cluster constitutes a fourth error wire format -- {"error": "..."} -- alongside the ApiErrorResponse envelope from the registered DRF handler, the raw {"errors": {...}} from the degenerate validation handlers, and the hand-built dict in team.py:469-489, so /roles answers in a shape no other endpoint in the API uses.
- **proposed:** Delete both `handle_exceptions` (global_exception_handler.py:16-36) and `GlobalExceptionHandler` (:39-68), and register RoleNotFoundException / RoleOperationException as branches in the one handler that is actually wired up -- todo/exceptions/exception_handler.py:handle_exception, referenced by todo_project/settings/base.py:102. BaseRoleView then needs no try/except at all (role.py:113 and :143 stop wrapping their _execute closures) and /roles answers in the same ApiErrorResponse envelope as every other endpoint. That is a breaking change to the two role endpoints' response body, so version or announce it, and the existing role view tests will pin the current {"error": ...} shape. For the dual-write guard, a small `@skip_if_disabled` decorator in the same module replaces the four pasted blocks; it must preserve the `return True` semantics (disabled means "success, nothing to do"), which is easy to invert by accident.

## 147. MISSED BY FINDER: `_check_authorization` admin-email gate duplicated byte-for-byte in two view classes in one file, and absent from the third
- **dimension:** mechanical | **severity:** low | **effort:** small | **copies:** 2 | **~lines:** 14
- **sites:** todo/views/team_creation_invite_code.py:20-26 (GenerateTeamCreationInviteCodeView), todo/views/team_creation_invite_code.py:131-137 (ListTeamCreationInviteCodesView)
- **drift:** Not yet textually diverged, but this is an authorization gate duplicated across sibling classes, which is the category where drift is least acceptable: a change to the admin rule (say, adding a role check or a case-insensitive email comparison) has to be made in two places, and nothing links them. The gap is already visible in coverage rather than in text -- the third view class in the same file, VerifyTeamCreationInviteCodeView (todo/views/team_creation_invite_code.py:87-128), has no _check_authorization at all and no authorization call in its post handler, while both of its neighbours gate on admin email. Whether that omission is intentional is not documented anywhere; a shared mixin would have made the exception explicit instead of invisible. This file is also the one carrying two of the eight degenerate _handle_validation_errors copies, so it was assembled by pasting whole method blocks between classes.
- **proposed:** A small mixin beside the views, `todo/views/mixins.py::AdminOnlyMixin` with the single `_check_authorization(self, user_email: str = None) -> bool` method, mixed into both classes so the admin rule has one definition. Both call sites keep their current `if not self._check_authorization(user_email): return <403>` line unchanged. While making the change, record a decision on VerifyTeamCreationInviteCodeView: either mix the check in there too or add a comment stating that verification is deliberately open, so the asymmetry stops being accidental-looking.
